*Previously: August 4, 2026 — Session 205 — **Built and validated (against real Ford Pro data, dry-run only, nothing posted yet) the first slice of the loan-statement reconciliation pipeline David asked for: a Supabase schema + two edge functions that ingest a pulled lender statement, compute the principal/interest split, find the matching Xero bank transaction, and (only on explicit confirm) split it and attach the source CSV as a permanent record.** Trigger: David's most time-consuming monthly accounting task is downloading ~15 loan statements (vehicles, credit lines, SBA loans) for his CPA to manually split principal vs. interest in Xero — he wants an app that logs into each lender, pulls statements, and completes the Xero reconciliation directly. Scoped this down with David first: full unattended login automation deferred (storing credentials for 25-30 lenders + bypassing 2FA is a real security tradeoff, not something to rush into) — Phase 1 is Claude driving David's own already-authenticated Chrome session to pull statements, with a review-before-post step; pilot lender is **Ford Pro FinSimple** (5 of his ~15 loans, all on one portal). **Design confirmed with David via 2 questions:** (1) review/approve UI lives in a new "Loans" tab in the existing Admin Dashboard (not a chat-based monthly report) — built this session, see below; (2) build the real persistent schema now, not an ad hoc one-off — this is what makes "attach the CSV to the Xero transaction as permanent proof" (David's mid-session addition) actually work, and scales cleanly to the other ~10 loans later. **Schema (migrations `loan_reconciliation_tables` + `loan_reconciliation_storage_policies`):** `loan_accounts` (one row per lender loan — lender, lender_account_number, VIN, `ingestion_method` for future lender types beyond portal-pull, and the confirmed Xero mapping: `xero_account_code`/`xero_account_id` for the liability account, `xero_bank_account_id` for which bank feed the autopay hits), `loan_statements` (one row per pulled statement — balances, due dates, `storage_path` into a new private `loan-statements` Storage bucket holding the raw CSV/PDF as the permanent proof record), `loan_splits` (one row per period per loan — computed principal/interest, `status` pending_review → posted, `matched_xero_bank_transaction_id` once posted). RLS on all three tables + the storage bucket restricted to admin/manager (finance data), matching the project's role convention. **Ford Pro → Xero account mapping (all 5 loans, confirmed with David across several turns this + prior session, including catching and fixing my own mid-session BalanceSheet-report mistake — see session note below):** the naming convention between Ford Pro account numbers and Xero account names is inconsistent (sometimes keyed off the last 4 of the VIN, sometimes off the last 4 of the Ford Pro account number) — worked out account-by-account rather than assuming a pattern: 61564140→`242 E-Transit Loan - 4140`, 61797019→`244 E-Transit Loan E4-9744`, 63204751→`332 E-Transit Loan E5-4751`, 63982094→`338 E-Transit Loan E6-7410`, 61178562 (paid off per Ford Pro, flagged to CPA re: its lingering $900.95 Xero balance — see below)→`238 E-Transit N202 Loan - 8562`. **Loose end found, not yet resolved:** Xero also has a `239 E-Transit N205 Loan - 0309` with no corresponding Ford Pro account identified yet — flag to David next session. **The math (validated against 2 real months of CSVs already pulled via Chrome):** principal paid = prior statement's `Principal_Balance` minus current statement's; interest paid = `Total_Amount_Due` minus principal — no PDF/OCR needed, the CSV's balance field alone is sufficient and was cross-checked against real numbers (e.g. loan 4140: $17,814.75→$16,755.81 principal, $1,180.32 total due → $121.38 interest). **`loan-ingest-statement`** (new edge function, service-role): stores the raw CSV in the `loan-statements` bucket, upserts `loan_statements`, and if a prior statement exists for that loan, auto-computes and upserts the `loan_splits` row. Backfilled all 8 already-downloaded June/July CSVs through it — correctly computed 3 real July splits (loans 4140/4751/7410) and correctly produced *no* split for loan 9744 (its statement showed `Total_Amount_Due: $0.00` both months — flat balance, nothing due, matches the $16,223.76 already cross-verified against Xero's TrialBalance in the prior session). **`loan-xero-post`** (new edge function, dry-run by default, `confirm:true` required to write): given a `loan_split_id`, searches the Wells Fargo bank feed (the same account Stripe payouts land on) for the matching autopay debit — **key fix mid-build:** the obvious anchor date (current statement's `payment_due_date`) is actually the *next* upcoming payment, not the one that already happened; the real anchor is the *prior* statement's due date, corrected after the first search came back empty. Once matched, builds a plan splitting that transaction's single line item into two (principal → the loan's liability account, interest → `800 Interest Expense`), preserving the original total exactly (reconciliation-safe in principle — Xero's `where` filter has been unreliable for this org on other endpoints, so the search has a client-side-filter fallback if the filtered query 400s, same defensive pattern as the payout pipeline). On `confirm:true`, also downloads the CSV back out of Storage and attaches it to the Xero transaction via the Attachments API — the permanent CPA-facing proof record David asked for. **Verified live in dry-run for all 3 July splits — all 3 auto-matched to exactly one real Xero bank transaction each, all already reconciled and (as expected, confirming the actual problem) currently posted as a single line to the loan liability account for the FULL payment amount (principal + interest lumped together, overstating the loan paydown and understating interest expense) — exactly what this pipeline exists to correct.** Nothing has been posted to Xero yet — these 3 are ready and waiting on David's go-ahead. **Also cleaned up this session:** neutered 6 leftover temporary diagnostic edge functions from the prior session's loan-balance investigation (`xero-loan-balances`, `-v2`, `xero-org-check`, `xero-account-ledger-check`, `xero-trial-balance-check`, `xero-8562-history`) to 404 stubs, per the disposable-helper convention — `xero-trial-balance-check`'s stub carries forward the finding that Xero's `Reports/TrialBalance` is the verified-reliable balance source for this org (BalanceSheet gave incorrect figures, root cause not fully diagnosed). **Same session — built the "Loans" admin dashboard tab (`admin-dashboard/index.html`), closing the loop end-to-end.** Trigger for the design: David asked where pulled statements actually "live" — walked through the 3 places involved (his Downloads folder as a transient Chrome side-effect, this session's workspace as a transient processing step, and the `loan-statements` Storage bucket as the real permanent home, doubled by the copy attached directly to the Xero transaction) — then David explicitly asked for **one single ingestion point** that also solves lenders/statement types Chrome can't reach, explicitly framing it as mirroring the Phase 1 division of labor (he downloads, Claude processes). Used an Explore agent first to map the existing tab-system conventions (`showPage`/`page-X` divs, `ALL_PAGE_IDS`/`pageTitles`/`pageCTAs`/`PAGE_LABELS` lookup tables, the `role_permissions`-driven `canAccessPage` guard, the `.card`/`.badge`/`.modal-overlay` CSS vocabulary, and the fetch+session-JWT edge-function-calling pattern from the refund modal) so the new tab matches house style exactly rather than inventing a new one. **Built:** nav item + `page-loans` (summary counts, a Pending Review table that opens a review modal, an All Loans table) inserted after SMS Delivery in both the nav and the Analytics section; `loans` added to `ALL_PAGE_IDS`/`pageTitles`/`pageCTAs`/`PAGE_LABELS` (defaults to admin-only access via the existing role_permissions self-heal pattern — David can grant `manager` access later from Team → Permissions, same as any other page, no code change needed for that). **The upload modal is the single ingestion point David asked for:** picks a loan, accepts a CSV or PDF, and auto-parses Ford Pro's known CSV column format to fill in the date/balance/due-amount fields automatically (matching the loan by account number if the dropdown wasn't preselected) — any file that doesn't match a known format (every other lender's statements, mostly PDFs) still gets attached and stored, just with the four number fields left for manual entry instead of blocking on OCR/parsing work that doesn't exist yet. Submits straight to `loan-ingest-statement`. **Small correctness fix made while building this:** both edge functions were hardcoding `Content-Type: text/csv` on the stored file and the Xero attachment, which would have mislabeled every future PDF statement — added a shared `contentTypeFor(filename)` helper (csv/pdf/octet-stream by extension) to both `loan-ingest-statement` and `loan-xero-post` before this mattered. **Review modal:** clicking a pending split calls `loan-xero-post` in dry-run mode and shows the matched Xero transaction, what it's currently posted as vs. the proposed principal/interest split, and the reconciled-transaction caution note if relevant, with an "Approve & Post to Xero" button wired to the same function with `confirm:true` — this is the actual permission gate for the financial write, nothing posts without a click here. Verified the full script block still parses (`node --check` on the extracted `<script>` content) before shipping. **Shipped via the device bridge** (this repo lives on David's Mac, not in this session) — staged the file in, applied all edits, wrote it back, verified byte-for-byte via the mtime guard. **Committed locally, not yet pushed** — same device-bridge no-network-access limitation as every other session touching this repo; needs `git push origin main` from David's own Terminal (or ask Claude to do it next time chatting from that machine). **Pending:** David's go-ahead to actually post the 3 ready July splits (now doable either from this chat or from the new Loans tab's review modal); resolving the unmapped `239 E-Transit N205 Loan - 0309`; expanding to the other ~10 loans (Funding Circle/iBusiness — email-based statements, different ingestion method; Dexter/PCV/Verdant — schedule-driven amortization with no statement to pull at all; various SBA/alt-lender loans) once the Ford Pro pattern is proven out over a couple of real months; the manual-entry fields in the upload modal are the near-term answer for PDF-only lenders — real PDF parsing isn't built yet. **Continued same session — corrected a mapping mistake, closed a security gap, and added CPA access + amortization-schedule ingestion:** David corrected a mistake — I'd flagged the unmapped `239 E-Transit N205 Loan - 0309` as an open loose end without checking its balance first; verified via a temporary TrialBalance diagnostic that it's $0.00/fully paid off (not a live loan), neutered the diagnostic, and adopted a standing rule going forward: only flag Xero loan accounts with a nonzero balance as needing lender-side reconciliation, never old paid-off ones. **CPA access (David's request — give his CPA restricted dashboard access with system-flagged issues):** added a `cpa` role (migration altering the `profiles_role_check` constraint), split every loan-table RLS policy from one 'staff can do everything' policy into separate read (admin/manager/cpa) vs. write (admin/manager only) policies so CPA read-only is enforced at the database level, not just hidden in the UI, and added a `role_permissions` row granting `cpa` access to the Loans page. **Caught and fixed a real gap before any CPA account exists:** the two loan edge functions ran with the service-role key and only checked JWT validity, not the caller's actual role — hiding the 'Post to Xero' button for a CPA wouldn't have stopped a CPA account from calling the function directly. Added a `callerRole()` check (validates the JWT, looks up `profiles.role`) to both `loan-ingest-statement` and `loan-xero-post`, redeployed. **Needs Attention:** added `loan_accounts.flagged_note` (set on loan 61178562/8562 with the $900.95 residual-balance note David specifically asked to have flagged for his CPA) and a reserved `loan_splits.status = 'needs_attention'`, both surfaced in a new dedicated card in the Loans tab (`renderLoansAttention()`) — built in `admin-dashboard/index.html` but not yet re-synced to David's Mac/committed (pending). **Amortization schedules (David's request — ingest schedules too when available, and compare against statements):** new tables `loan_amortization_schedules` (one row per uploaded schedule, pointing at the stored PDF as the proof record) and `loan_amortization_rows` (full fidelity of every row — payments, rate changes, annual/grand totals — nothing dropped at ingestion; `row_type` distinguishes real payment events from summary/marker rows so downstream logic can filter cleanly). `loan_splits` extended with a nullable `amortization_row_id` and a `source` column ('statement_delta' vs 'amortization_schedule') since schedule-only lenders have no monthly statement to diff against — `current_statement_id` made nullable with a check constraint requiring at least one of a statement or a schedule row as provenance. New edge function `loan-ingest-amortization` (same auth/content-type conventions as the statement pipeline) stores the file and rows; new `loan-generate-schedule-split` creates a period's split directly from the schedule for schedule-only lenders. `loan-ingest-statement` now cross-checks any newly computed statement-based split against a matching schedule row (±$2 tolerance) and flags `needs_attention` with an explanation on mismatch — satisfies David's 'compare them, flag issues for the CPA' ask for lenders that eventually have both. **First real use:** David shared a sample schedule (Dexter Financial, contract 103973-SP2, $257,377.78 at 6.64% from 8/31/21, rate change to 5.89% on 8/31/26, payoff 8/31/28) — read and parsed all 97 rows; empirically matched it to Xero account `233 Dexter Loan 2` by comparing the schedule's balance ($89,411.25 as of 7/31/26) against Xero's TrialBalance for all three 'Dexter Loan' accounts (233: $88,898.10 — the ~$513 gap is exactly one month's interest, consistent with Dexter's payments historically being lumped in full against the loan account rather than split, not a wrong-account mismatch; 303: off by two orders of magnitude; 292: no balance) and confirmed its $3,839.38 auto-payment hits Wells Fargo Checking (4384) from contact 'Dexter Financial' across 4 real transactions — added as a new `loan_accounts` row (`ingestion_method: 'amortization_schedule'`) and ingested the full 97-row schedule for real (verified July 2026's row cleanly isolates via the `row_type='payment'` filter, and August 2026's rate-change month correctly separates its `rate_change` marker row from its `payment` row). **Pending:** re-sync + commit the outstanding `admin-dashboard/index.html` changes (CPA permissions grid, Needs Attention card, upload-modal CPA guard); hide the 'Approve & Post to Xero' button for `cpa` role in the review modal footer (server-side enforcement already exists, UI polish doesn't yet); get the CPA's name/email from David and confirm before sending an invite (David said hold off for now); build the Loans-tab upload path for amortization schedules (statements have client-side auto-parse, schedules will need the same Claude-assisted-parse-then-submit flow used for Dexter, since formats vary too much across lenders to auto-parse and uploads are rare). **Posted the 3 verified July splits — but not the way `loan-xero-post` was designed, and this is a real architecture fix, not a one-off:** David said "go ahead and post them." First attempt (edit the matched bank transaction's line items, as originally built) failed live with a Xero `ValidationException`: **"This Bank Transaction cannot be edited as it has been reconciled with a Bank Statement"** — all 3 candidates were already reconciled. Root cause: Xero hard-blocks editing line items on any reconciled bank transaction, full stop, regardless of whether the total changes. **Correct fix (now in `loan-xero-post` v6, replacing the edit-in-place approach entirely, not just for these 3):** leave the original reconciled bank transaction completely untouched, and instead post a Manual Journal that reallocates the interest amount — debit `800 Interest Expense`, credit the loan's own liability account for the same amount (undoes the over-reduction from the original lumped payment, which effectively debited the loan account for the full principal+interest). This works regardless of reconciliation status, so it's now the only code path (dry-run also updated to show `proposed_journal` instead of the old `proposed_plan`/edited-line-items shape) — added a migration for `loan_splits.xero_manual_journal_id` to track it alongside the untouched `matched_xero_bank_transaction_id`. **Posted for real, all 3 succeeded:** loan 4140 journal `fbb21e11…` ($121.38 interest), loan 4751 journal `c8ca1314…` ($253.62 interest), loan 7410 journal `e92e082a…` ($170.77 interest) — `loan_splits.status` now `posted` for all three. **Found a second real gap while doing this — the CSV attachment step fails too, separately from the reconciliation issue:** attaching the source statement (to either the bank transaction or the new journal) returns Xero `401 AuthorizationUnsuccessful`, confirmed even against a fresh unreconciled test — this Xero Custom Connection's OAuth scope doesn't include attachments write access at all. This means the "attach the statement as permanent CPA-facing proof" part of the original design has never actually worked (no confirm:true post had happened until today, so this was untested until now). Not fixable from here — **David needs to add the Files/Attachments scope to the Custom Connection in Xero's Developer app settings and reconnect**; the attachment code is left in place in `loan-xero-post` so it starts working automatically once that scope exists, no redeploy needed. **Reviewed the admin-dashboard review modal against the new response shape** (`proposed_journal`/`JournalLines` instead of `proposed_plan`/`LineItems`) and fixed it in the same pass — would otherwise have shown blank/broken data on next use. **Separately, David asked whether July's Stripe payouts could be retroactively split by revenue category the same way session 199-204 built for new payouts.** Investigated live (temporary diagnostic, neutered after): all 24 July payouts are posted as a single lumped line to account 403 (Delivery), none have ever run through the categorized pipeline (`xero_payout_syncs` only has 2 rows, both from Aug 3-4 — the pipeline went live literally days after July ended), and **all 24 are reconciled**, meaning the exact same Xero restriction found above applies — they can't be edited in place either, only fixed via the same Manual Journal reallocation pattern, extended to cover Subscription/Retail-WF/Retail-Vending/Gift-card/Credits/Discounts/Refunds/fees instead of just principal/interest. Technically feasible (the classification logic already exists in `xero-payout-sync`'s `classifyPayout`/`buildPlan`), but bigger in scope than anything done so far — 24 real reconciled transactions in one batch — and one payout (`po_1ToBkiGACgbvEugHXd02pwJz`, $125,000, arrived 7/1) matched 2 different Xero transactions by amount and needs individual disambiguation before it could be touched at all. Answered David with these specifics rather than a guess; waiting on his go-ahead before building or running anything for July. **David then asked a genuinely good question: "what if I simply unreconcile transactions and have you split them cleanly?"** Researched rather than guessed (Agent tool, not training-data assumption): confirmed Xero has explicitly declined a long-standing 280+ vote feature request to support reconcile/unreconcile via API, citing regulatory obligations around bank data — `IsReconciled` is read-only, no workaround exists including delete+recreate; unreconciling is only possible manually in the Xero UI, one transaction at a time. Explained the tradeoff plainly (manual per-transaction work vs. the audit-trail case for preferring journal entries over editing historical reconciled records, with the appropriate "this is your CPA's call to confirm" caveat) — David initially chose "you unreconcile in Xero, then I'll edit the line items directly," then reversed mid-turn: **"nevermind what I said, let's go with the more correct journal approach."** **Built `xero-payout-reallocate`** (new production edge function, `requireAdmin`-gated, dry-run by default) — the productionized version of the same Manual Journal reallocation pattern just proven for loans, extended to payout categories: for a given payout, finds the original (untouched) reconciled bank transaction, runs it through the existing `classifyPayout`/`buildPlan` logic from `xero-payout-sync` (duplicated rather than shared — no shared-module setup exists yet in this project, noted in code comments as a known shortcut), and builds a journal with one line per non-Delivery category plus a single offsetting line back on account 403 (Delivery) — algebraically this leaves 403 holding exactly the correct Delivery amount without ever touching the original transaction. Migration added `xero_payout_syncs.xero_manual_journal_id` + `.method` (`new_bank_transaction` vs `reallocation_journal`) to distinguish future clean payouts (posted directly) from these retroactive corrections. **Resolved the $125,000 ambiguity** the dry-run had flagged as blocking: David clarified directly ("the $125,000 funds received was the Stripe Loan") and confirmed via a chart-of-accounts screenshot that only one real "Stripe Capital Loan" account exists in Xero — so the $125,000 arrival is the loan disbursement itself, correctly excluded from revenue-category reallocation (also independently corroborated by `classifyPayout` returning it as fully unclassified, i.e. not resembling normal revenue transactions). **Flagged, not touched:** a second Xero entry also for $125,000 (id `442354f1-ddb2-4da3-b7e1-a12a9efdb791`, contact "Family Laundry", description "Stripe Loam", account 471) looks like a possible duplicate or miscoded entry — worth David or the CPA taking a separate look, deliberately left alone. **Dry-run presented to David in full before posting anything:** 22 of 23 non-loan July payouts clean and ready ($146,398.41 total), 1 excluded pending investigation (`po_1TvoN0GACgbvEugHBUZlA2Fs`, $10,630.05 — contains an $18.27 charge, `ch_3TuZBPGACgbvEugH1hPuO9RC`, with no matching `orders` row, so it can't be auto-classified; not investigated further, needs David's input before it can be included). David: "yes please." **Posted all 22 — every one succeeded.** (Batched via a temporary helper, `temp-payout-reallocate-post`, in groups of 3 to stay under Supabase's edge-function execution timeout; the auto-mode safety classifier correctly paused the first attempt as an irreversible external financial write even after the "yes please," so the exact scope and dollar impact were restated and a fresh "proceed" obtained before retrying — working as intended, not a bug.) **Verified against the database, not just the function's return value:** `select count(*), sum(payout_amount) from xero_payout_syncs where method='reallocation_journal' and status='posted' and payout_arrival_date >= '2026-07-01' and < '2026-08-01'` returned `posted_count: 22, total_amount: 146398.41` — exact match to the pre-approved dry-run total. **Net effect:** $13,571.84 moved out of the lumped "Delivery – Wash & Fold" account (403) into where it actually belonged — Subscription +$19,673.50, Retail-WF +$9,988.56, Vending +$647.96, Gift cards +$100, Credits −$1,116.60, Discounts −$411.30, Stripe processing/account fees −$5,073.20/−$137.80, Refunds −$802.53, and notably Stripe Capital loan repayment −$9,296.75 (this last one had been silently inflating apparent Delivery revenue all along — worth remembering as a pattern to watch for in other lumped historical payouts). `temp-payout-reallocate-post` neutered to a 404 stub after use, per the disposable-helper convention. **David asked to delete the possibly-duplicate $125,000 "Family Laundry"/"Stripe Loam" (account 471) entry — checked before touching anything, turned out to be a non-issue:** pulled the bank transaction directly from Xero by ID and found `"Status": "DELETED"`, dated 2026-07-24 (before this session's investigation even started) — it was already voided, doesn't count toward any balance or report, no duplicate revenue was ever double-counted, nothing to delete. Diagnostic neutered after confirming. **Then David caught a real bug by eye, comparing the July and August P&L Income Statements side by side:** Subscription, Retail-Vending, and Gift card sales were showing as *negative* revenue in July, and Merchant Fees was showing as a negative cost (reducing COGS instead of adding to it) — August (posted through the live, non-retroactive `xero-payout-sync` pipeline) looked completely correct by contrast. **Root cause, confirmed empirically (pulled one live posted journal + the Chart of Accounts Type/Class for every touched code, not guessed):** `xero-payout-reallocate`'s journal-building step took `UnitAmount` values straight from `buildPlan()` — a function written for BankTransaction LineItems, where sign just means "add to" or "subtract from" the transaction total, no debit/credit concept — and copied them directly into `ManualJournal.LineAmount`, which is a completely different convention (positive = debit, negative = credit, full stop). Every single line in every one of the 22 posted journals had its sign exactly inverted: revenue accounts (405 Subscription, 401 Vending, 404 Retail-WF, 403 Delivery-WF) were debited (positive) when they needed to be credited (negative) to increase, and cost/contra accounts (345 Credits, 346 Coupons, 605 Merchant Fees, 828 Stripe Fees, 691 Refunds) were credited (negative) when they needed to be debited (positive) to increase — and the offset line into 403 was credited (increasing it) when the whole point was to *reduce* it, meaning the lumping problem it was meant to fix was actually made worse. Retail-WF (404) didn't visibly go negative in the P&L only because its normal (non-payout) transaction volume was large enough to absorb the error without flipping the section's sign — the bug was equally present there. (Separately confirmed the 3 loan-interest Manual Journals posted earlier this session do **not** have this bug — that code was reasoned through debit/credit correctly by hand rather than copied from `buildPlan()`, and account 800/Interest Expense's negative display in the August "Other Income/(Expense)" section is the correct expected behavior for that account's type.) **Fixed both the root cause and the 22 bad journals, with David's explicit go-ahead at each step ("go ahead" for the plan, then a fresh "proceed" after the auto-mode safety classifier paused the correction batch as an irreversible financial write, same as it did for the original July posting):** redeployed `xero-payout-reallocate` v2 with a new `buildJournalLines()` helper that negates every line (documented inline with the full sign-convention reasoning so this can't regress silently); built a temporary `temp-payout-journal-fix` helper that, for each of the 22, pulled the live posted journal from Xero, **voided it** (confirmed via research, not assumption, that Xero's API supports `Status: VOIDED` on an already-POSTED ManualJournal — `DELETED` only applies to still-DRAFT ones — so the bad entries stay visible in the audit trail as voided rather than silently vanishing), then posted a replacement journal with every line's sign flipped and a narration cross-referencing the voided original. Tested on 2 payouts first and verified the corrected journal's signs against the Chart of Accounts before running the rest. **All 22 corrected successfully** — verified against the database (`voided_manual_journal_id` set on all 22, `sum(payout_amount)` still exactly `$146,398.41`, unchanged from the original dry-run total, confirming no dollar amounts moved, only the signs). Migration added `xero_payout_syncs.voided_manual_journal_id` to keep the old→new journal mapping as a permanent audit trail. Both temporary helpers (`temp-payout-journal-fix`, `temp-journal-signcheck`) neutered to 404 stubs after use. **Second bug found immediately after, by David eyeballing the actual P&L again (comparing Jul vs. Aug side by side in Xero's report view) — this one much more visible: all the reallocated July revenue had vanished from July and piled into August instead.** Root cause, again confirmed empirically rather than guessed: the void-and-repost correction code set the new journal's `Date` from `original.DateString` — a field that exists on BankTransactions but **not** on ManualJournals (which only return `Date` as a raw epoch string, no `DateString`). Since that read as `undefined`, Xero silently defaulted every one of the 22 corrected journals to the date they were posted (2026-08-04, today) instead of their real July arrival dates — confirmed by pulling 2 of the 22 directly from Xero and decoding their `Date` field. The line amounts and debit/credit signs from the first fix were still correct; only the date was wrong. **Fixed the same way as before, this time sourcing the date from `xero_payout_syncs.payout_arrival_date`** (reliable, never touched by either bug, not re-derived from a Xero response) rather than trying to parse anything off the journal object again: voided all 22 mis-dated journals and reposted them a third time with the same (correct) lines and the correct date. Tested on 2, decoded the live `Date` field to confirm `2026-07-01` came back instead of today before running the remaining 20. **All 22 succeeded, verified against the DB again** (`sum(payout_amount)` still exactly `$146,398.41`, `voided_manual_journal_id` chain updated to the latest voided journal at each step). Both one-time helpers (`temp-payout-journal-datefix`, `temp-journal-signcheck`) neutered after use. Net result of both corrections together: the 22 July journals now have the right amounts, the right signs, and the right dates — asked David to refresh the Xero report and confirm it looks right before considering this fully closed. David confirmed the P&L looks right after both fixes. **Then moved on to Dexter's July loan split** (asked which loan work he wanted next — he chose posting Dexter's July split over onboarding the other ~10 lenders). **Generated it from the already-ingested amortization schedule** via `loan-generate-schedule-split`: principal $3,326.23 / interest $513.15 / total $3,839.38 (matches the known $3,839.38 auto-payment exactly), `status: pending_review`. **Found and fixed a real gap in `loan-xero-post` before posting:** the function was written assuming every split has a `loan_statements` row (it dereferenced `stmt.payment_due_date`/`stmt.statement_date` unconditionally) — schedule-only splits like Dexter's have `current_statement_id = null` and would have thrown. **Redeployed `loan-xero-post` v7, generalized (not patched) to handle both split sources:** branches on `split.source`/whether a statement exists -- statement-sourced splits keep the exact v6 behavior (anchor search on the prior statement's due date, attach the pulled CSV); schedule-sourced splits anchor the bank-transaction search on the matched `amortization_row.row_date` instead, and skip the attachment step with an explicit reason (`'schedule-sourced split -- no per-period file to attach'` -- the full schedule is already the permanent record from ingestion, no separate per-period file exists to attach). **Dry-run matched cleanly** to the real reconciled Wells Fargo transaction (2026-07-30, $3,839.38, currently lumped in full against account 233) -- reviewed with David before posting. **Posted successfully:** journal `dbbefd00-5b4a-49dc-bd45-19ea4efbba3a` (debit 800 Interest Expense +$513.15, credit 233 Dexter Loan 2 −$513.15), `loan_splits.status` now `posted`, verified via DB. Temp auth-bypass callers (`temp-dexter-july-split`, `temp-loan-xero-post-caller`) neutered after use. **Pending:** David's input on the one excluded July payout's unclassified $18.27 charge; David needs to add the Files/Attachments scope to the Xero Custom Connection (still blocking statement/CSV attachment on the Ford-Pro-style statement pipeline -- moot for Dexter and other schedule-only lenders); onboarding the other ~10 loans (Funding Circle/iBusiness -- email-based; various SBA/alt-lenders) whenever David's ready to move past Ford Pro + Dexter. **David then asked for the Loans tab's "All Loans" table to show the actual posted split so the CPA can eyeball the numbers and catch anything wrong** -- previously it only showed a status badge like "posted 2026-07" with no dollar figures. **Added a "History" link per loan row** (`admin-dashboard/index.html`) that opens a new modal (`modal-loan-history` / `openLoanHistoryModal`) listing every split ever computed for that loan -- period, source (statement vs. schedule), principal, interest, total, status, posted date, and a truncated Xero Manual Journal ID (full ID in a title-attribute tooltip) -- plus any `review_notes` inline for flagged/mismatched periods. Pulls from the same `_allLoanSplits` array `loadLoans()` already fetches (no extra query, and CPA already has RLS read access to `loan_splits`, so no new permission plumbing needed). The link uses `event.stopPropagation()` so it doesn't trigger the row's existing "click to upload next statement" behavior, and only renders when a loan actually has split history (nothing to click for a loan that's never had one). Verified the script block still parses (`node --check`) before shipping. Synced to David's Mac via the device bridge, and — after David asked "did you commit?" and I found the file had only been written to disk, not committed — **committed `6b51692`** (this feature + the two payout-journal bug fixes) via `git add`/`git commit` over the device bridge now that the `.git/index.lock` David cleared earlier was gone; still needs `git push origin main` from his Terminal, device bridge has no network access for that. **Then David asked to be able to actually open the source files referenced in that history view, not just see that a split has one.** Checked storage RLS first rather than assuming it'd just work -- confirmed `loan-statements` bucket already has a `cpa read` policy alongside `staff read`/`staff write`, so no new permission plumbing needed. Added `_loanFileForSplit()`: resolves the real backing file for a split -- a pulled statement (`loan_statements.storage_path`) for `statement_delta` splits, or the parent amortization schedule (`loan_amortization_schedules.storage_path` via the linked `amortization_row_id` -- one file covers the loan's whole life, not per-period) for schedule-sourced ones. `loadLoans()` now also fetches `loan_amortization_rows` joined to their parent schedule's `storage_path`. Each history row gets a "View statement"/"View schedule" link; clicking calls `viewLoanFile()`, which opens a blank tab synchronously (to dodge popup-blockers on the async gap) then redirects it to a 5-minute signed URL from Supabase Storage once it resolves. Verified script parses clean before shipping; synced (commit blocked again this round by a fresh `.git/HEAD.lock` David hit even in his own Terminal — pushed the prior commit fine, this one's still local-pending; flagged to David that 3 different stale-lock flavors in one session on his own machine points at something else holding `.git` open, e.g. an iCloud/Dropbox sync or an editor with the repo open, worth checking since re-running "on your computer" mode may help). **David then flagged a real UX gap while testing:** clicking Dexter Loan 2 (schedule-sourced, no monthly statement) opened the generic "Upload a Statement" modal and demanded he hand-type statement date/principal/total-due -- exactly the kind of manual entry this whole pipeline exists to eliminate, and David called out the human-error risk directly. **Fixed properly, not by hiding the fields:** `renderLoansTable()` now branches the row click on `loan_accounts.ingestion_method` -- statement-sourced loans (Ford Pro) keep the existing upload modal unchanged; schedule-sourced loans (Dexter, and any future amortization-only lender) get a new, much smaller "Generate Split from Schedule" modal (`modal-loan-schedule-generate` / `openLoanScheduleGenerateModal`) that asks for exactly one thing -- the period -- and computes everything else straight off the schedule already on file via the existing `loan-generate-schedule-split` function (now called with the user's real session JWT from the dashboard, so no temp-bypass helper was needed this time). Added `_nextSchedulePeriod()` to default the period picker sensibly (one month after the loan's most recent split, or the schedule's earliest payment row if it's never had one) so David usually just confirms rather than picks. On generate, immediately opens the existing `openLoanReviewModal()` so the computed split still goes through the same review-before-post flow as everything else -- no new posting path, just a safer on-ramp into the one that already existed. "Last statement" column shows "n/a — schedule" for these loans instead of a confusing dash. CPA guarded client-side same as the upload modal (RLS already blocks the write regardless). Verified script parses; synced to David's Mac. **David asked for two more Loans-tab additions.** (1) Needs Attention: added flagged loan_accounts rows for Dexter Loan 3 (Xero 303, $935.51 residual balance) and Aquarecycle Loan (Xero 234, $1,286.28 residual balance) -- David confirmed both are actually paid off; mirrored the Ford Pro 8562 pattern exactly (status='paid_off', flagged_note explaining the discrepancy). (2) Onboarded PCV Good and Green Loan (Xero 254) and Verdant Capital Loan (Xero 394) as amortization_schedule loans, same verification rigor as Dexter Loan 2: David supplied both schedule PDFs (PCV: Loan ID 202555, periods 16-84 only, i.e. no data before the 9/1/2026 payment; Verdant: Contract 11867000, borrower 'Young & Foolish, LLC dba Family Laundry', full 84-period schedule from the 6/10/2025 booking). Cross-matched to Xero: found the exact $157,000 12/10/2025 origination wire under contact 'Paypal Loan' (not the similarly-named plain 'Paypal' contact) -- confirms which contact's transactions belong to this facility. Flagged but did NOT try to force a fix for a real mess found along the way: Xero's bank feed has TWO other Paypal-named facilities' payment histories tangled across the 'Paypal' and 'Paypal Loan' contacts going back to 2019 and 2021, including a ~$2,290.98/week payment still running alongside the new loan's $3,414.71/week payments through January 2026 -- exact historical reconciliation there is genuinely ambiguous from the data alone, so left it alone rather than guess; future split-generation will match one bank transaction at a time and flag (not silently pick) if more than one candidate fits, same safety net as every other loan. Xero's '304 Stripe Capital Loan' liability had only ever been booked at the $125,000 draw -- the $20,875 fee was never recorded, so the account understated the true obligation from day one. Found a dedicated '264 Loan Fees' expense account already in the chart of accounts (better fit than lumping into 800 Interest Expense, which is used for per-payment amortizing-loan interest, a different concept than this one-time fixed fee). Proposed the fix to David before posting (real $20,875 entry): Manual Journal dated 6/30/2026 (Origination Date, not today, since the fee was fixed and owed from inception) -- debit 264 Loan Fees $20,875.00, credit 304 Stripe Capital Loan $20,875.00. David confirmed 'Go ahead.' Posted via a temp auth-free caller (neutered after) -- journal 531c23c0-011c-42c0-8986-0fdc00635f6d. Found and flagged the pre-pipeline backlog: **242** -- 2026-04-17 ($1,180.32) and 2026-05-18 ($1,180.32) lumped, plus an unusual $7,687.53 payment on 2026-05-15 that may not even need a split (could be a legitimate extra principal paydown -- needs verification against the actual statement, not assumed to be a bug); **332** -- 2026-04-13 ($1,046.95) lumped; **244** -- 2026-04-09 ($1,144.55) lumped, AND (more concerning) **no payment of any kind has posted to acct 244 since 2026-05-11** -- searched the full bank feed (337 transactions, every contact) for June and July with no match, flagged for David to check directly with the lender whether autopay stopped or the loan changed. **338 came back clean** -- July was its only-ever lumped payment and it's already corrected. None of the Ford Pro backlog items were fixed (unlike Verdant, these need the actual lender statement pulled for each month to get the real interest split, not a schedule-derived estimate) -- all four flagged on their loan_accounts rows with the specific dates/amounts for David's review, awaiting his go-ahead to pull the missing statements and correct them the same way July was. David replied "fix all". **Verdant Capital (394): fixed for real** -- posted the $1,855.38 reallocation journal (2af0f6ad-696d-4780-8c1c-3dd307b4295e), verified live balance moved to $250,894.29, matching the schedule-expected $250,894.33. **Ford Pro: attempted the same for 242/332/244, but the investigation overturned the original diagnosis.** First hit a real blocker -- Ford's FinSimple portal had logged David out mid-session, and per the credential-handling rule I can't enter a password on his behalf, so I stopped and asked him to log back in himself. Once he confirmed, drove his authenticated Chrome session to pull each account's full 12-month statement history (Account Details page has an expandable Transaction History with the lender's own principal/interest split per payment -- exact source-of-truth numbers, not schedule-derived estimates). **What the real statements revealed: the April/May 'lumped' payments I'd flagged had already self-corrected.** For all three loans, the month immediately following each lumped payment posted with an artificially skewed split (extra interest, reduced principal) that exactly offset the prior month's error -- e.g. 242's April lumping over-reduced the liability by $147.43, and June's posting recognized $283.07 of interest instead of the real $132.81, a $150.26 -- wait, exactly $283.07 total which covers both April's $147.43 AND May's $135.64 lumped errors combined. Same self-healing pattern confirmed for 332 (April error $281.79, corrected the very next month/May) and for 244's April error ($181.99, corrected in May). Live balances for 242 and 332 now tie out exactly to Ford Pro's real reported principal balances -- **no correction posted, none needed.** This means whatever posts these Ford Pro splits into Xero isn't a naive bank rule like PayPal's -- it's doing some kind of delta/catch-up reconciliation with a one-month lag. Worth understanding better before assuming any future 'lumped' Ford Pro payment needs a manual fix -- give it a month first. **244 turned up a genuinely new, real, and still-open issue instead.** Ford's own statement history showed a second May transaction I hadn't seen: David paid an extra $5,000 toward this loan on 2026-05-27 ($4,903.21 principal + $96.79 interest per Ford's own numbers), which is why the loan's next due date jumped to October. Traced that $5,000 in Xero (2026-05-28, contact "Ford") and found it was coded **entirely to account 238 -- the OTHER Ford Pro loan on this same lender that's already paid off** -- not to 244 at all. Despite that, 244's live balance already matches Ford's real post-paydown number almost to the penny, through a path I could not fully trace in the visible transaction/journal history in the time available. Rather than post a guessed correction against real books when the numbers already tie out through a mechanism I can't fully explain, **left this one un-posted and flagged clearly** for David or his bookkeeper to check account 238 for an erroneous $5,000 credit that may need moving. Also independently found and flagged (not fixed, for the same reason) a real $7,687.53 transaction on 242 dated 2026-05-15 that doesn't correspond to anything in Ford's actual statement history for that loan, yet doesn't throw off the live balance either -- an oddity worth a second pair of eyes. All flagged_notes rewritten to reflect this corrected understanding (superseding the earlier, less-informed flags from the initial sweep).***
*Previously: August 4, 2026 — Session 204 — **Backfilled the Aug 3 payout's Xero entry with the $20 credit + $21.95 in discounts that had been silently baked into gross Delivery revenue (posted a few hours before session 203's Credits/Discounts breakout shipped).** Trigger: David asked why a specific August P&L screenshot showed "Coupons - Delivery: $34.00" but no "Credits - Delivery" line at all, and whether that meant there simply were no credits this month. Diagnosis: the Aug 4 payout (the first one processed under the new session-203 logic) correctly reported $0 credits / $34 discounts for its own transactions — nothing was broken going forward. But the Aug 3 payout (`po_1U09cqGACgbvEugHRmeGM2XF`, Xero BankTransactionID `9cc08e5e-652c-42f5-abfc-ae60b01119b2`) had posted a few hours *before* the session-203 fix shipped, so its $20 account credit (order #11181) and $21.95 in discounts (orders #11232, #11174, #10215) were still silently folded into the 403 Delivery line at net instead of broken out. Built two temporary read-only diagnostics first — `diag-aug3-payout-credits` (re-ran the same Stripe/order classification logic scoped to one payout ID) and `xero-bank-txn-peek` (fetched the raw existing Xero BankTransaction) — to get exact numbers before touching anything, confirmed the transaction was not yet reconciled in Xero and had no existing 345/346 lines, then built a one-off correction function (`xero-fix-aug3-credit-discount`, dry-run mode first) that grossed the 403 line up by $41.95 and added the missing 345 (Credits) / 346 (Discounts) lines, verified the total still balanced to the original $5,071.31 payout amount, and posted for real only after David's explicit go-ahead (a first attempt was correctly blocked by the auto-mode safety classifier as an irreversible external financial write — re-confirmed with David before retrying). All three temporary functions neutered to 404 stubs immediately after, matching the disposable-helper pattern from sessions 199/201/203. **Net effect:** month-to-date Xero P&L for Aug 2026 now shows Credits - Delivery $20.00 (was $0.00) and Coupons - Delivery $55.95 (was $34.00) — the actual bank deposit total is unchanged, only which account the credit/discount amounts are visible under. No git commits besides this notes update — all changes were Xero-side.*
*Previously: August 4, 2026 — Session 203 — **Extended the Stripe payout → Xero pipeline (session 199/201) to also account for Credits, Discounts, and Refunds, instead of letting them silently shrink whatever revenue category they landed in.** Trigger: David noticed his WashRoute Daily Revenue dashboard breaks Gross Revenue out separately from Credits and Discounts, and asked whether the Xero app could do the same, plus asked about Refunds (a Stripe-side concept). **Refunds turned out to already be fully handled** — the original pipeline already traces every refund balance transaction back to its original charge/order and nets it into the correct revenue category, so no fix was needed there for correctness, only for *visibility* (see below). **Credits and Discounts were the real gap:** these are pure WashRoute concepts (`orders.line_items` entries of type `credit`/`discount`, e.g. "Account credit applied" -$20, "NON PROFIT (5% off)") that Stripe never sees as separate transactions — Stripe only ever receives the already-net charge amount, so they were invisibly baked into whichever revenue category (usually 403 Delivery - Wash & Fold) the order fell into. **Checked the actual Xero chart of accounts first** rather than assuming: found `345 Credits - Delivery`, `346 Coupons - Delivery`, and `347 Refunds - Delivery` already exist and unused, plus a general `691 Refunds & Replacements`. Confirmed with David: even though ~$2,700 of historical credits/discounts fall on Subscription/Commercial orders (not just Delivery), all credits/discounts still post to the existing 345/346 accounts regardless of revenue stream (matches how the Daily Revenue dashboard already reports them as flat, uncategorized totals — no new Xero accounts needed); Refunds post to the general 691 account rather than the Delivery-specific 347, per David. **Implementation (`xero-payout-sync`, redeployed, no DB migration needed):** each charge's underlying order's `line_items` are scanned for `credit`/`discount` entries and their dollar amounts are added back into that charge's revenue-category bucket (gross-up to full list price, same gross-revenue philosophy already used for fees/the Capital loan), then posted back out as two separate negative lines (Account credits → 345, Discounts → 346) so the entry still balances to the actual bank deposit. Refunds were pulled out of the per-category netting entirely and now post as their own flat negative line (→ 691), which also simplified the code — a refund no longer needs to be traced back to a revenue category at all, removing one whole class of potential 'unclassified transaction' blocks. **Verified against a real payout** (`po_1U09cqGACgbvEugHRmeGM2XF`, $5,071.31) via a temporary read-only test function (deployed, run once, retired immediately after): correctly found a real $20 credit and $21.95 in discounts across 4 orders that had been invisible until now, and the full reconciliation still matched the payout amount to the penny once fees/loan/credits/discounts were all accounted for. **Known limitation, flagged to David:** because credits/discounts/refunds all post to flat, non-category accounts, Xero will show the *total* credited/discounted/refunded each payout but not *which* revenue stream it came from — splitting that out would require creating new per-category accounts in Xero first, not done this session since it wasn't asked for.*

*Previously: August 4, 2026 — Session 202 — **Investigated a customer billing complaint (Lisa Sturges, order #11140) down to a real bug in the recurring-order engine, refunded the overcharge, and fixed the root cause before it could hit anyone else.** Trigger: Lisa emailed that her receipt said $31 but her card was charged $97, and asked if this was related to her subscription. Investigated via `orders`, `subscriptions`, `subscription_usage_log`, and `order_events` for her customer record (`5e501b4e-2f89-45b2-9d67-39d983f07287`). **Root cause:** she cancelled her original subscription (`543771ea...`, signed up Jun 16) at period end Jul 16, then resubscribed Jul 23 under a brand-new subscription record (`233b7254...`, $275/mo, 100 lb allowance). Order #11140 (24 lb pickup, Aug 3) was auto-generated by the recurring-order trigger `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` on Jul 29 — six days after she resubscribed — but still carried the OLD, cancelled subscription's id forward. Because that old subscription's period had already exhausted its 100 lb allowance back in July, the billing calc treated the entire 24 lbs on the new order as uncovered overage (24 lbs × $2.75/lb = $66), on top of $21 in add-ons and $10 tip — charging $97 instead of the correct $31. Confirmed via `order_events`: `total_changed` shows "$21.00 → $87.00" and `billing` shows "Charged $97.00", both firing at 23:10 UTC — the same instant the confirmation email rendered, except the email's line-item list simply omitted the `lb_overage` line item that was already baked into the actual charge, which is why the receipt (correctly, per the intended $31 price) and the real charge (incorrectly, per the bug) disagreed — worth a separate look at why the receipt template drops `lb_overage` lines, not fixed this session. **Refunded $66.00** via a one-off edge function (`support-refund-order-11140`, deployed, run once against `pi_3U0V0CGACgbvEugH12oxKdpK`, immediately neutered to an inert stub afterward — same pattern as prior one-off Stripe scripts this project) and relinked order #11140's `subscription_id` to the correct active subscription for accurate records. **Traced the actual bug** to `trg_create_recurring_order_fn`: it only looked up the customer's currently-active subscription when generating the next recurring order if the triggering order's `subscription_id` was NULL — once an order had any subscription id attached, every later occurrence in the chain just copied that same id forward forever, so a customer who cancels and later resubscribes keeps getting billed against their old, dead subscription (and its already-exhausted lb allowance) indefinitely. **Fixed (migration `fix_recurring_order_stale_subscription_id`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first — SECURITY DEFINER + `search_path` preserved, no grant changes, no other pricelist branches touched):** the function now always re-resolves the customer's active subscription for `pricelist='Subscription'` customers, falling back to the order's existing `subscription_id` only if no active subscription exists (preserves prior behavior for a customer who is genuinely cancelled and hasn't resubscribed). **Audited all 77 Subscription-pricelist customers for the same stale-link pattern before considering this done** — found and proactively fixed one more: order #11422 (Sarah, customer `7bc0cff8...`, scheduled Aug 11) was linked to a subscription she'd cancelled Jul 31 instead of the active one from that same day; relinked before it could bill incorrectly, no refund needed since nothing had been charged yet. No other Subscription customers had a live stale link. **Also drafted (not yet sent)** an explanation/apology email to Lisa about the refund — David to review and send from his own inbox.*

*Previously: August 3, 2026 — Session 201 — **Built and shipped a fully automated Stripe payout → Xero revenue reconciliation pipeline, resolving David's question about splitting revenue by Delivery/Subscription/Retail for accounting.** Trigger: David asked how to see revenue separated by stream; his first instinct was separate Stripe accounts, which would have fragmented the customer/card-on-file model for no real benefit — steered him toward doing the split entirely within WashRoute's own order data + Xero, no Stripe account changes needed. **Xero setup:** connected a Custom Connection OAuth2 app (client_credentials grant, no user login flow, right fit for one integration talking to one org) scoped to `accounting.banktransactions`, `accounting.settings`, `accounting.contacts` — `XERO_CLIENT_ID`/`XERO_CLIENT_SECRET`/`XERO_TENANT_ID` added to Supabase Secrets. **Revenue mapping confirmed with David** (existing Xero chart of accounts, not new): `403 Delivery - Wash & Fold` (delivery orders incl. overage/add-ons/tips on subscriber orders — the subscription fee itself bills separately via Stripe's native Invoicing/Subscriptions object, identified by a non-null `charge.invoice`), `405 Delivery - Subscription Fees` (the recurring plan charge only), `404 Retail - Wash & Fold` (POS `line_items[].kind IN ('service','addon')`), `401 Retail - Vending` (POS `kind='merchandise'`), `461 Delivery - Gift card sales` (Gift Up charges, identified by `charge.description` starting `'Gift Up:'` — Gift Up sales never create a WashRoute order at all, so this had to be identified from Stripe data alone). Out of scope (never touch WashRoute's Stripe account): `402 Delivery - Commercial` (Kidango, paid by check/ACH), `400 Retail - Self service`, `411 Retail - Gift Cards` (unused), `460 Rental income`, `463 LCFS credits`. **Key discovery mid-build:** the first real-payout dry run surfaced Stripe balance-transaction types beyond ordinary charges/refunds — `payout_minimum_balance_hold`/`_release` (rolling reserve), a standalone `stripe_fee` (account-level, distinct from per-charge fees), and **`financing_paydown`** (automatic Stripe Capital loan repayments, confirmed against David's Xero balance sheet — `304 Stripe Capital Loan`, $125k drawn July 1 2026). Per David's direction (matching how the accountant reconciles manually at month-end), revenue posts at full **gross** — the loan repayment and all Stripe fees are broken out as their own separate line items (`605 Merchant Fees` for per-transaction processing fees, `828 Stripe Fees` for account-level fees, `304 Stripe Capital Loan` for the paydown) rather than netted invisibly against revenue. **Architecture:** `xero-payout-sync` edge function (`verify_jwt:false` — dual auth: Stripe webhook signature via `constructEventAsync`/`STRIPE_PAYOUT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` for the real `payout.paid` trigger, or an in-function `requireAdmin()` check gating manual/testing invocation) classifies every balance transaction in a payout (joining `charge.payment_intent` back to `orders.stripe_payment_intent_id`), fails safe (no Xero post) if anything is unclassified or if reserve holds/releases don't net to zero within the payout, then posts one compound Xero `BankTransaction` (Type RECEIVE, Contact "Stripe", Wells Fargo Checking 4384) whose line items sum to exactly the payout amount — reconciles automatically against the bank feed. Webhook processing runs via `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil()` so Stripe gets an immediate ack instead of timing out across the ~100+ sequential Stripe API calls a large payout requires. New table `xero_payout_syncs` (migration `xero_payout_syncs`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first — RLS enabled, admin/manager read-only, no anon grant) is the idempotency ledger, one row per Stripe payout ID, preventing double-posting on webhook retries. Helper functions `xero-diagnostics` (chart-of-accounts/contacts lookup, used to build the mapping) and `xero-payout-dryrun` (preview-only, superseded by `xero-payout-sync?dry_run=true`) can be left in place or deleted. One-off `stripe-webhook-endpoint-setup` (created the Stripe `payout.paid` webhook via API) was neutered to a no-op stub immediately after use — safe to delete entirely. **Verified live, not just applied:** ran `dry_run=true` against a real payout (`po_1U09cqGACgbvEugHRmeGM2XF`, $5,071.31, Aug 3) and walked David through the exact line-item payload before posting for real, with his explicit go-ahead; the posted entry (Xero `BankTransactionID` `9cc08e5e-652c-42f5-abfc-ae60b01119b2`) reconciles to the penny. Confirmed the webhook wiring separately by sending a deliberately-invalid `stripe-signature` header and getting the expected signature-verification rejection (not a "secret missing" error) — proof `STRIPE_PAYOUT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is read correctly without needing to wait on a live Stripe-triggered event. **No git commits this session** — every change is Supabase-side (3 edge functions + 1 migration) plus Xero-side config; nothing in the WashRoute repo's tracked files changed. **Follow-up for a future session:** reserve hold/release netted to exactly $0 on the one real payout tested, but that's incidental to that payout, not guaranteed — if it doesn't net to zero on a future payout the function correctly fails safe (`xero_payout_syncs.status='failed'`, logged reason) since there's no Xero account mapped for a persisting reserve balance yet; will need real design input if/when that happens. POS Merchandise/Vending split allocates tax proportionally across a mixed cart rather than tracing it per line item — fine at current POS tax volumes, worth revisiting if it ever matters. Consider deleting `xero-diagnostics` and `xero-payout-dryrun` once confidence in the automated pipeline is established over the next few weeks.*

*Previously: August 3, 2026 — Session 200 — **Investigated and rolled back the Aug 2 residential window-consolidation change David thought he'd cancelled, restoring every customer's exact original pickup/delivery sub-window.** Trigger: David noticed Alameda/Berkeley/Concord/Oakland AM+PM had all collapsed to single 7-10am/7-10pm windows and said he thought he'd cancelled this — it was scheduled for Aug 3 rollout back in a July 27 planning session (see that Pending entry, now resolved). **Root cause:** the rollout was wired to a one-time scheduled task (`send_later`, created July 27, fired at 8am PT Sunday Aug 2 — the confirmed zero-order day chosen specifically so it could run unattended). Checked the task directly: it ran to completion exactly as scheduled (`ended_reason: run_once_fired`) — there is no record anywhere (scheduler, git, PROJECT-NOTES) of it ever being disabled or cancelled between July 27 and Aug 2. Whatever "cancel" happened evidently never reached the actual scheduled task. Contributing factor: step 6 of the original plan called for the automated run to log a shipped changelog entry here — it never did, despite the technical work (7 `route_templates` updated, ~160 orders re-snapped, `pickup_day_reminder` SMS disabled) completing cleanly per DB timestamps, so there was no visible confirmation anywhere pointing at it having gone live. **Recovery — fully exact, not approximate:** the automated Aug-2 run had gone through `reschedule_order_leg` (not a raw bulk UPDATE) for every re-snapped order, so each leg's pre-change window was sitting in `order_events` (`event_type='rescheduled'`, `old_value`/`new_value`, 522 rows in a ~35-second window Aug 2 15:10 UTC) — meaning each customer's *exact* original sub-window (not just "somewhere in the old range") was recoverable. **Rollback executed:** (1) reverted the 7 templates (Alameda/Berkeley/Concord/Oakland AM: `window_end` 10:00→11:00, `arrival_window_hours` 3→2; Alameda/Berkeley/Oakland PM: `window_start` 19:00→18:00, `arrival_window_hours` 3→2). (2) Per David's explicit instructions: left `pickup_day_reminder` SMS disabled (not re-enabled) and excluded this morning's already-run/in-flight AM pickups from the restore (6 orders, still `scheduled` but already past-due for today's 7am window — left alone; a further 7 legs across pickup/delivery had already physically completed or reached a terminal status and couldn't be touched regardless). (3) Restored 192 still-pending legs (83 pickup + 109 delivery) to their exact original window, extracting each leg's original Pacific time-of-day from its `order_events.old_value` and re-applying it to the order's *current* date rather than the historical date (caught mid-run: 1 of the 192 had legitimately moved to a different date since Aug 2 for unrelated reasons — forcing the stale historical date onto its still-current route_id would have failed template-window validation; using time-of-day + current date fixed this generally, not just for that one case). All 192 calls went through `reschedule_order_leg` with `p_notify=false` (matching the RCC bulk-move UI's own default for this kind of internal reassignment — nothing here is a new promise to the customer, it's undoing an unannounced-to-them internal shuffle) and succeeded with zero errors. **Separately, same session:** fixed a real bug this surfaced — `renderWeeklySchedule()`/`renderDriverSchedule()` (`admin-dashboard/index.html`) built their grid rows straight off the unfiltered `allTemplates` array, so retiring a template (`is_active=false`, done earlier this session for the old single "COMMERCIAL" 9-3 template per the Commercial AM/PM split below) had no effect on the Schedule tab — the retired template kept showing an "Unassigned" row forever with no way to hide it. Fixed by filtering `t.is_active` when building the sorted row list in both functions; `allTemplates` itself stays unfiltered at the source since other consumers (the Templates management list) legitimately need to see inactive templates too. Committed locally (`716fe14`) — needs `git push` from David's Mac like the rest of this session's commits. Also moved the 2 stops that had landed on today's driver-less "Commercial AM" (Charlotte Maxwell Clinic, Homebase Shelter Program) onto today's staffed "Commercial PM" route at David's request. **Follow-up same session — Charlotte Maxwell Clinic kept landing on Berkeley AM:** David flagged this pickup/delivery recurring back onto Berkeley AM despite the Commercial split; root cause was that this customer had no `route_template_override_id` set, so `auto_route_order` fell back to zone match, and the Commercial zone apparently overlaps Berkeley's (same overlap class flagged above for Kidango). Moved today's already-en-route pickup and the just-delivered leg onto Commercial PM (David confirmed proceeding even though the pickup was mid-transit and would need manual handling today), then set `route_template_override_id` → Commercial PM on the customer record and moved the 3 already-booked future legs (Aug 8 delivery, Aug 10 pickup, Aug 15 delivery) that were still sitting on Berkeley AM, so this should be the last time it recurs. Correction during this thread: initially told David Commercial PM had no driver based on `routes.pickup_driver_id`/`delivery_driver_id` being null — David pointed out Andres was assigned on the Schedule page. Confirmed the Schedule/Routes UI writes to the legacy single `routes.driver_id` field, which the driver app treats as authoritative (a `route_stops.driver_id = NULL` row inherits whichever driver owns the route); `driver_id` was in fact set to Andres, so both moved stops were already covered — no gap, no action needed. **Nit Pixies Clinic duplicate-order investigation (David asked to check reports of stops being cancelled as "duplicates"):** confirmed this customer genuinely has 2 real locations (Oakland office, El Cerrito office) plus a leftover exact-duplicate "Home" address (same Oakland address, created 2 days before the account was converted to its current 2-location commercial setup) — that stray duplicate address is what triggered a real cancellation on March 24 (`cancelled_by: 'admin'`, an order against the duplicate address got cancelled the instant it was created because it looked identical to the legitimate Oakland order already on the books for that date). Traced the underlying mechanism, which is still live in the code and not specific to this one bad address: `trg_create_recurring_order_fn`'s duplicate-order guard, and the admin dashboard's own "Duplicate Orders" DB Health audit check, both keyed off `customer_id` + pickup date only, with no address in the comparison — meaning any multi-location commercial customer whose locations recur on the same weekday (Nit Pixies' actual pattern: both offices, every Monday) could have one location's recurring order silently fail to generate with zero error, depending purely on which location's prior order flips to `delivered` first. Nit Pixies' own history happened not to hit this race (both locations have in fact recurred every Monday since March 30), but the bug was real and unaddressed. **Fixed both call sites** to key on `customer_id` + pickup date + `pickup_address_id` instead: `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` (migration `fix_recurring_order_dup_check_include_address`) and the admin dashboard's Duplicate Orders check (`admin-dashboard/index.html`, commit `7573e9f`) — committed locally, needs `git push` from David's Mac along with the rest of this session's commits. Also relabeled the orphaned duplicate "Home" address (`af342de6...`) rather than deleting it — it's still referenced by that one historical cancelled order — so it reads clearly as "do not use" if anyone encounters it in a dropdown again. **Same session — Joann Wagner pickup-confirmation SMS missing its photo link:** David asked whether it was simply that no photo existed. Traced `send-order-notification`'s `{{pickup_picture}}` template variable — computed via a live lookup of `route_stops.proof_photo_url` at send time — and confirmed a real proof photo did exist for this stop; the SMS just went out before it landed. **Root cause:** `complete_route_stop` always passes `p_photo_url: null` to the RPC — the driver app deliberately doesn't block a stop completion on the photo upload; the actual file goes up via a background IndexedDB queue and lands later via a separate `route_stops` update, racing the SMS send (which today fires synchronously and immediately from `advance_order_status`). Quantified the real scope before proposing any fix, rather than assuming this was a one-off: over the last 30 days, 409/1441 pickups (~28%) and 349/1396 deliveries (~25%) sent their confirmation text before the photo was attached. Evaluated three options with David: a second follow-up SMS once the photo lands (rejected — extra text to every customer); making the driver app wait for the upload before completing a stop (rejected — David explicitly didn't want the driver blocked on network); and a stable customer-facing photo-link page (discussed downsides — new public-facing surface to maintain, doesn't fully close the gap for customers who never reopen the link). David then proposed a fourth option — delay the confirmation send by 2-3 minutes, re-check whether the photo has landed, and fall back to a link-less confirmation if it still hasn't — validated against the actual historical delay distribution before building, which showed this would resolve 92-93% of today's misses. Cost-checked the rejected second-SMS alternative against current Twilio pricing for context (~$0.0083 base + ~$0.0035+ carrier fee, blended ~$0.012-$0.016/message all-in) — moot once the delay approach was chosen, since it adds zero message volume. **Built (migration `add_delayed_photo_notification_queue`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first):** new `notification_queue` table (`order_id`, `event`, `send_after`, `sent_at`, `attempts`; RLS enabled, no `anon`/`authenticated` grants) + `flush_notification_queue()` SECURITY DEFINER function (EXECUTE locked to `service_role` only, claims rows via `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, re-checks the order's live status at send time so a stale/rolled-back order can't trigger a wrong-status text) + new `wr-notification-queue-flush` pg_cron job running every minute. `advance_order_status` now queues `picked_up`/`delivered` events for a 3-minute delayed send through this path instead of firing `send-order-notification` immediately; `pickup_failed`/`delivery_failed`/`walkin_order_ready` notifications are unchanged, still immediate. No app-layer code changed for this fix — `send-order-notification` already looked up `proof_photo_url` live at send time, so only the *timing* of the call needed to move. **Verified functionally, with zero risk of a stray real-customer text:** confirmed the FK constraint correctly rejects a queue row for a nonexistent order; inserted a real queue row against a real order still in `scheduled` status with `send_after` in the past, ran `flush_notification_queue()` directly, confirmed the row was claimed (`sent_at`/`attempts` populated) but the staleness guard correctly produced zero new rows in `sms_messages` (the queued `picked_up` event didn't match the order's real `scheduled` status) — then deleted the test row. Not yet committed to git — this was a 100% DB-side change (migration only, no file edits), so the changelog entry below is the only file change this piece needs; still needs `git push` from David's own Terminal once committed, same as the rest of this session's work. **Same session — Commercial PM early-start question:** David asked whether a driver arriving at 11am for a noon-window Commercial PM route could be enabled to start early. Traced the driver app's route-visibility logic (`driver-app/index.html`) and confirmed it already has a 2-hour pre-window buffer (`PRE_WINDOW_HOURS`, in place since Feb 24) that makes a route fully actionable 2 hours before its official window — 10am for a noon route — with no server-side time lock on `complete_route_stop` either. Verified against the live Commercial PM route running that morning (driver Andres, all stops pending, `status='scheduled'`) that nothing in the code should have blocked an 11am start. Since David had no further detail on what the driver's screen actually showed and didn't want the buffer widened, no code change made — flagged that a stale/unrefreshed app or a wrong phone clock/timezone are the likely explanations if it recurs, and that a pull-to-refresh should resolve it. **Same session — auto-fail timing question:** David asked when orders get auto-skipped after a route ends. Confirmed via `cron.job` + `auto_fail_expired_orders()` (runs every 30 min): pickups/deliveries still unresolved 2 hours past the later of the order's own window end or the route's actual end time get flipped to `pickup_failed`/`delivery_failed` (real-world delay up to ~2.5h given the 30-min check cadence). David confirmed the 2-hour buffer is fine as-is — no change needed. **Same session — Tyrone Anderson "Wrong number" reply, real bug found:** David shared a screenshot of a walk-in customer (5 orders since June, always drop-off/pickup in person, never a delivery) replying "Wrong number" to a "Your clean laundry has been delivered... send back your paper string" text. Investigated via `sms_messages`/`orders`/`order_events`: this was his own real order (#11474), correctly marked `delivered` when he picked it up in person at the counter — but that status change fired the home-delivery-worded `order_delivered` template, which makes no sense for someone who never had a delivery. **Root cause:** the session-189 "SMS centralization" migration (`generalize_status_and_reschedule_notifications`, July 29) generalized `advance_order_status`'s `picked_up`/`delivered` notify branches to fire for any order reaching those statuses, but — unlike the neighboring `ready_for_delivery`→`walkin_order_ready` branch, which correctly guards `AND v_order.source = 'walk_in'` — never added the equivalent exclusion to keep walk-in orders OUT of the generic `picked_up`/`delivered` events. Before July 29, walk-in orders got no text at this step at all (by design — they already have their own `walkin_order_placed`/`walkin_order_ready` texts), which is exactly why 4 prior orders for this same customer never had an issue. Quantified scope before fixing: confirmed via `order_events` that a July-22 walk-in order (#10560) reaching `delivered` did NOT fire a notification, while the Aug-3 one (#11474) did — pinpointing the regression window to the July 29 migration. Since then, 104 walk-in orders have reached `delivered`, and at least 37 mis-fired SMS were confirmed sent; found a second customer who separately replied confused ("Wait was this delivered or ready for pick up? I dropped it off this morning") on July 29, the day the regression shipped. **Fixed (migration `fix_walkin_orders_no_delivered_picked_up_sms`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first — signature/grants unchanged, `CREATE OR REPLACE` only):** added `AND v_order.source <> 'walk_in'` to both the `picked_up` and `delivered` notify conditions in `advance_order_status`, restoring the pre-July-29 behavior (walk-in orders get no notification at these two steps, matching 2+ months of working history) rather than inventing new copy. Verified: `prosrc` confirms both guards present; grants unchanged (`authenticated`/`service_role`/`postgres` only, no `anon`); functionally tested inside a rolled-back transaction against a real walk-in order in `ready_for_delivery` — confirmed `notified_event: null` / `notification_queued: false` when advanced to `delivered`, then confirmed the rollback left the real order untouched. Also checked (and ruled out) a scarier possibility this surfaced: whether the cron-triggered `auto_fail_expired_orders`' *direct* call to `send-sms` (bypassing `advance_order_status`) might be silently failing today, since `send-sms` now requires a real staff login or service-role key and rejects the anon key — 30 days of `net._http_response` logs show zero real 401s (only the 2 from my own test calls), so that path is fine; not a live issue. Could not send David's drafted clarifying reply to Tyrone directly — `send-sms` correctly refuses database-level access for arbitrary customer texts (by design), requiring an actual staff session — gave David the exact text to send himself from the Inbox instead, which he did. **Not yet committed to git** — this was again a 100% DB-side fix (migration only, no file edits); this changelog entry is the only file change, still needs `git push` from David's own Terminal.*

*Previously: August 3, 2026 — Session 199 — **Split the single "COMMERCIAL" 9am–3pm route into two selectable sub-windows so David can move a commercial stop to a specific time block, instead of everything landing in one big 9-3 route.** Trigger: David wanted to move commercial orders into a 9-12 or 12-3 window specifically, but the "Move stops to another route" dialog only ever offered one COMMERCIAL destination since only one `route_templates` row (9:00-15:00) existed for it. **Design decision (discussed with David first, since this touches live driver assignments + 21 real commercial accounts):** rather than retiring the original template and re-pointing all 21 commercial customers' `route_template_override_id` in one shot (risky — 2 of them, Onika Crossman and Homebase Shelter Program, genuinely book across both windows depending on the day, and repointing them to a single narrow template would make some future bookings fail to route), went with the safer additive path: added two brand-new templates, **"Commercial AM"** (09:00–12:00) and **"Commercial PM"** (12:00–15:00) — same zone, schedule days, `arrival_window_hours=3`, `turnaround_days=1`, and `stop_limit=NULL` (preserving the dedicated-commercial no-cap convention) as the original — and left the original **"COMMERCIAL"** template (9-3, `is_active=true`) completely untouched. All 21 existing overrides still point at the original template and are unaffected; new bookings keep landing there exactly as before.
