Ran `generate_route_runs(14)` to materialize the two new templates into actual `routes` rows for today through the next two weeks (24 rows created), so "Commercial AM"/"Commercial PM" now show up immediately as destinations in the Move-stops dialog — David can hand-move any commercial stop into the correct window today. **Driver staffing, per David's explicit choice:** left both new templates with no default/weekly driver (`route_driver_schedule` has no rows for them yet) rather than auto-mirroring the existing weekday drivers (Francisco Cobos Mon-Thu / Luis Lemus Sun / Eve Piñon Fri, who currently run the single 9-3 block solo) — **a stop moved onto Commercial AM/PM won't show up in any driver's app until a driver is assigned to that template in the Routes tab.** Flagged, not fixed: the Commercial zone already overlaps Kidango's zone/window (both share `zone_id 914d20f4...`, windows overlap 9am-1pm) for any customer routed by zone instead of override — pre-existing, not introduced by this change, worth a look eventually. **Separately this session:** fixed a broken git state on David's Mac — a prior `git pull` had aborted because 11 files existed locally as untracked copies of content that had *also* been added upstream (verified byte-identical against `origin/main` first, so nothing was at risk), plus that aborted attempt left two stale `.git/*.lock` files that the device-bridge shell couldn't `unlink` (a known sandbox limitation — bridge can `mv` but not `rm`/`unlink` on the mounted folder, so leftover lock files have to be moved out of `.git/` by hand instead of deleted). Moved the 11 duplicates into a new `_to_delete/` folder and moved the stale locks out of `.git/`; David then ran `git pull` cleanly from his own Terminal. `_to_delete/` is now redundant (git recreated all 11 files fresh from the pull) — safe for David to trash himself whenever.*

*Previously: August 1, 2026 — Session 198 — **POS pay screen: Card/Cash are now hidden by default for on_account customers, instead of just being deprioritized next to a "Charge to Account" button.** Trigger: David's reaction to the session-196/197 writeup — "the system should tell attendants not to charge, not the other way around." He's right: the system already knows a customer is on_account from their profile (`customers.billing_type`), so it shouldn't be on staff to remember to pick the correct payment method every time. **Before:** the pay modal (session 168) showed a "Charge to Account" button as the visually primary option, but Card and Cash sat right below it as fully clickable equal-weight alternatives — nothing stopped a cashier from tapping Card out of habit. **Fix (`pos/index.html`, `openPay()`):** for `billing_type='on_account'` customers, the `#payCardCashMethods` row is now `display:none` by default; only the Account button and banner show. A small "Needs to pay by card or cash instead?" link (`#payAccountOverrideRow`) reveals Card/Cash via `revealCardCashOverride()` for the rare legitimate exception (e.g. a one-off personal purchase unrelated to the business account) — but it takes a deliberate second tap, not a stray one. State resets closed every time `openPay()` runs, so an override can never silently carry over into the next sale. **Blast radius:** checked whether the admin dashboard's own billing surface (the Rack panel) has the same equal-weight-buttons problem — it doesn't. `openRackPanel`/`renderRackPanel` never expose a manual card/cash/account choice at all; the `rack_order` RPC decides the billing branch server-side purely from the customer's `billing_type`, so there was nothing to fix there. The POS pay modal was the only surface in either app where a human picked the payment method by hand next to an on-account option. **Verified:** `node --check` on the full extracted script passes; diffed against the previously-committed file to confirm the change is scoped to just the pay-modal markup + `openPay()`/`revealCardCashOverride()`. Commit `bfa8b07` on `main`, **committed locally, not yet pushed** (same device-bridge network limitation as sessions 196/197 — needs `git push` from David's own Terminal).*

*Previously: July 31, 2026 — Session 197 — **Ran a full on-account audit after the session-196 fix, to check whether any other customers were slipping through the same crack.** Trigger: David asked for an audit of on-account customers to confirm none were missing from the On Account report after the pickup_window_start fix. **Method:** queried every on-account order directly against the live DB rather than trusting the app-level fix alone — grouped by status/source/pickup_window_start-null to find any pattern the session-196 fix might not cover, checked for duplicate/shadow customer records that could split one account's orders across a visible and an invisible profile (matched by shared phone/email), checked RLS on `orders` to confirm admin/manager/laundry_tech have unrestricted read access (they do, via `is_admin()`), and simulated the fixed report's exact query (Pacific-day boundaries, today = 2026-07-31) directly in SQL to confirm the fix is live and working. **Findings:** the session-196 fix is confirmed working live — City of Oakland's order and 4 other on-account orders correctly surface under today's default report view. No other on-account customer has an order with a missing pickup date outside the counter-drop pattern already fixed (zero remaining cases across all 62 on-account customers). No shadow-duplicate customer records found (2 pairs of on-account customers share a phone number — e.g. Homebase Shelter Program / Soul Sanctuary 1888 MLK — but both sides of each pair are already correctly tagged on_account, almost certainly a shared front-desk line across program sites, not a data bug). On-account orders sitting in skipped/cancelled/pickup_failed status with a nonzero total_amount were double-checked and confirmed to all have `weight_lbs IS NULL` — i.e. laundry was never actually intake'd before the order fell out of the pipeline, so that dollar figure is a stale pre-service booking estimate, not real uncollected revenue; correctly excluded from AR, not a gap. **Real finding — historical missing AR, now surfaced:** before the session-196 fix, two on-account customers had been dropping laundry at the Foothill counter regularly and every one of those orders was invisible to the On Account report: **City of Oakland** — 10 prior orders since June 5, $550.25, all still `billing_status IS NULL` (unpaid) — plus the $49.00 corrected order from today, **$598.25 total**. **OAKLAND ROOTS & SOUL** — 1 order from July 9, **$160.00**, also unpaid. Combined **$710.25** that was real, completed service sitting outside AR reporting for weeks, now correctly visible in the report and Record Payment modal. **Decision (David, this session):** leave both as outstanding for now rather than mark paid — has not been confirmed whether either was already collected out-of-band (cash/check) and just never recorded. **Follow-up for a future session:** check back on whether the $710.25 has been collected or needs an invoice sent; consider whether Foothill counter staff need a lighter-weight way to flag "this is an on-account commercial drop-off" at intake so these route through the dedicated intake panel instead of the walk-in cart in the first place (would prevent the root pattern behind both session 196 and this audit's finding from recurring).*

*Previously: July 31, 2026 — Session 196 — **Root-caused and fixed a double-charge on a City of Oakland on-account order (#11365, $98 charged for a single 28 lb bag instead of $49) AND why on-account counter drop-offs vanish from the On Account report + Record Payment modal.** Trigger: David reported a customer screenshot showing 2 identical "Wash & Fold (28.0 lb @ $1.75/lb)" line items on order #11365's receipt, plus that City of Oakland wasn't showing up in the On Account report. **Root cause #1 (double charge):** the order was rung up at the Foothill counter via the POS's normal per-lb weight-entry modal (`pos/index.html`, `addWeightToCart()`), not the dedicated commercial intake panel. That function had no double-tap guard — unlike the checkout button's `_chargeInFlight` lock — so a fast double-tap on the weight modal's Add button pushed the same 28 lb weigh-in into the cart twice, producing two identical `type:'service'` line items at checkout (`createPosOrder`). Confirmed by `total_bags=2`/`weight_lbs=56.00` on the order (computed from cart-line *count*, not a real second bag) and by comparing against sibling orders in the same queue, which all had a single line item at non-round weights. **Root cause #2 (missing from On Account report):** `loadOnAccountReport()` (and the near-identical `oaPaymentLoadOrders()` behind the "Record Payment" modal, found via blast-radius check) filters strictly by `pickup_window_start`, which is always NULL for counter drop-offs (`source='walk_in'`) since they never have a scheduled pickup. The Reports page also defaults its date pickers to today-only on first load, so the order was invisible in every dated view — City of Oakland's on-account balance silently under-reported. **Fix:** (1) `pos/index.html` — added an `_addWeightInFlight` lock around `addWeightToCart()`, reset on each `openWeightModal()` call, mirroring the existing checkout guard. (2) `admin-dashboard/index.html` — `loadOnAccountReport()` and `oaPaymentLoadOrders()` now OR the `pickup_window_start` date filter with a `source='walk_in'` + `created_at` fallback (PostgREST ANDs repeated `.or()` calls, used here to combine with the existing unpaid-status OR in the payment modal); AR aging math and the 4 invoice-sort/date-display call sites got the same `created_at` fallback so counter orders age into 30/60/90-day buckets correctly and no longer print blank dates or sort to epoch 0 on invoices. **Data correction:** order #11365 fixed directly in the DB — duplicate line item removed, `total_amount` $98.00 → $49.00, `weight_lbs` 56 → 28, `total_bags` 2 → 1, with an `order_events` row documenting the correction. **Verified:** both files pass a full `node --check` syntax pass; diffed against the originals to confirm no unintended changes; QA blast-radius pass (per `washroute-qa`) caught the identical Record Payment modal bug before shipping. Commits `2273848` and `8692680` on `main`, **committed locally on David's Mac but not yet pushed** — the cloud sandbox's device-bridge shell has no network access, so `git push origin main` must be run from David's own Terminal (or ask Claude to do it next time he's chatting from that machine) before this ships to production via the Vercel auto-deploy.*

*Previously: July 31, 2026 — Session 195 — **Root-caused and fixed a stale/self-referencing `moved_from_route_id` badge on route stops — a stop already sitting on Hayward PM was showing a red "↩ Hayward" badge claiming it had been moved FROM Hayward.** Trigger: David flagged a screenshot of the Route Command Center showing this on order `c0375499-b21b-4716-b2af-46a37c6cdb84` (Annaamalaii Palani, 4271 Dali Street, Hayward PM route, driver Eve Piñon). **Root cause:** `reconcile_order_stops`'s "reuse a skipped/failed stop" branch (the ELSE arm, used when no pending/en_route stop exists to reroute and a previously skipped/failed stop can be reused instead) repoints the reused stop's `route_id` to the new target route but never touched `moved_from_route_id` — unlike the sibling "keeper" branch, which correctly sets/clears it. A stop that had genuinely been moved away from route A, then skipped, then later reused and reassigned back onto route A, ended up with `route_id = A` and a stale `moved_from_route_id = A` left over from a much earlier move — a self-referencing value the admin UI's badge renderer (`admin-dashboard/index.html`, ~line 24106) has no reason to expect, so it rendered "moved from Hayward" on a stop that was never actually moved away this time. Confirmed via `SELECT count(*) FROM route_stops WHERE moved_from_route_id = route_id` — 7 affected rows, all dating back to May 20, 2026. **Fix (migration `session_195_fix_reconcile_order_stops_stale_moved_from`, no schema change, grants/ownership unaffected, reversible):** the reuse branch now captures the reused stop's prior `route_id` into `v_reusable_route` and sets `moved_from_route_id = CASE WHEN v_reusable_route IS DISTINCT FROM v_run_id THEN v_reusable_route ELSE NULL END` — mirroring the keeper branch's logic exactly. Paired one-time cleanup: `UPDATE route_stops SET moved_from_route_id = NULL WHERE moved_from_route_id = route_id` fixed all 7 historical rows. **Verified live:** re-ran the self-reference count query post-migration (0, down from 7) and re-fetched the live function definition to confirm the new logic is deployed; confirmed the exact order from David's screenshot now shows `moved_from_route_id: null` on both its route_stops.*

*Previously: July 31, 2026 — Session 194 — **Root-caused and fixed a system-wide outage of the "driver picked up your laundry" / "delivered" confirmation texts — 0 of 89 pickups sent between 2026-07-30 14:54 UTC and the fix.** Trigger: David relayed that Alice Baldridge and Mary Hayes complained they never got a pickup confirmation text; investigation found it wasn't 2 customers, it was everyone. **Root cause:** `complete_route_stop` marks the route_stop `'complete'` *before* calling `advance_order_status` to advance the order + fire the SMS. But marking the stop complete fires trigger `trg_sync_order_status` → `sync_order_status_from_stops()`, which — for the common single-stop-per-leg case — writes `orders.status` directly via a raw `UPDATE`, in the same transaction, before `advance_order_status` ever runs. By the time `advance_order_status` executed, `orders.status` already equaled the target status, so its "already in target status" guard silently swallowed the call: no real transition detected, so the SMS notify block never fired (and when an event *was* logged via the `log_order_change` fallback, it was mis-attributed to `'System'` instead of the real driver). This race has existed since `sync_order_status_from_stops`'s `picked_up`/`delivered` branches were added around session 137 (April), but was invisible for 3+ months because driver-app/admin-dashboard/customer-app used to fire the customer SMS via their **own client-side `fetch()`** call to `send-order-notification`, independent of this DB logic. Commit `91ebb29` (2026-07-29 17:27 UTC, "Finish SMS centralization", paired with DB migrations `session_189_generalize_status_and_reschedule_notifications` and `session_189b_reschedule_notify_opt_out`) correctly moved SMS-sending server-side for reliability — a dropped client network call used to silently lose the text with no way to detect it — but that removed the one thing that had been masking the race, and the race then broke the SMS outright once drivers' apps picked up the new client build (gradual cutover through the evening of 7/29 as open driver sessions kept running cached old JS, total outage by the morning of 7/30). Ruled out along the way: the session_191-era bag-check migrations (7/27–7/28) — inspected in full, none touch `complete_route_stop`, `advance_order_status`, or `sync_order_status_from_stops`. Also ruled out: template misconfiguration (`message_templates.order_picked_up` unchanged since March), edge function regression (`send-order-notification` last deployed 32 days prior), and a systemic `pg_net`/Twilio failure (other message types were still sending fine; `pickup_failed`/`delivery_failed` — which route through `skip_route_stop`, never through the racing trigger — kept working throughout). **Fix (migration `session_194_fix_pickup_delivery_sms_notify_race`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first — no signature/schema change, grants unaffected, fully reversible):** two layers. (1) `complete_route_stop` now calls `advance_order_status` *before* marking the stop `'complete'`, so it always sees the real pre-transition status — correct actor attribution and correct SMS, every time. (2) `sync_order_status_from_stops` now routes through `advance_order_status` instead of writing `orders.status` directly, so it's a pure safety net for any future/other path that marks a stop complete without going through `complete_route_stop` — `advance_order_status`'s own no-op guard makes this harmless when the order was already advanced normally. **Verified live, not just applied:** ran `advance_order_status` and the reordered `complete_route_stop` against a real pending pickup stop (order `011018d7-2d21-4332-a482-a6c43c6c377b`) inside a transaction rolled back at the end — confirmed `notified_event: "picked_up"` in the RPC's own return, then confirmed the rollback left the stop/order untouched (`pending`/`scheduled`). **Remediation decision (discussed with David):** fix going forward only — no backfill text to the ~89 already-affected customers, since most of those orders have already moved on to processing/ready/delivered and a day-late "picked up" text would likely read as more confusing than helpful.*

*Previously: July 30, 2026 — Session 191 — **Root-caused why Kidango - Mayfair Too's order silently lost its route on reopen, fixed `advance_order_status` at the source, verified the fix live, and shipped press-and-hold "solo route" on the Route Command Center eye icon.** Trigger: David reported Kidango - Mayfair Too wasn't showing up on today's (7/30) Kidango route. Forced the order onto today's and tomorrow's (7/31) Kidango pickup+delivery via `reschedule_order_leg`, then — per David's request once he'd manually moved it to the commercial route to catch up on a behind-schedule day — changed its window from the auto-assigned 9am-12pm to 12-3pm with a second `reschedule_order_leg` call. **Root cause (the real question David asked: "why did the system allow it?"):** the order had at some point been rolled back to `on_hold` — `rollback_order_to_on_hold` correctly nulled both `pickup_run_id`/`delivery_run_id`, deleted its `route_stops`, and set a descriptive `routing_error`. But when it was later advanced back to `scheduled`, `advance_order_status`'s generic "forward transition" cleanup blindly set `routing_error = NULL` as a side effect — without ever calling the routing engine — so the order landed back in `scheduled` with no route_stop AND no warning flag: invisible to both the Route Command Center and the Issues tab. **Fix (migration `session_191_advance_order_status_reroute_on_reopen`, reviewed via `washroute-migration-review` first — no schema change, grants/ownership guard preserved, reversible):** added `v_needs_reroute` (true when reopening to `scheduled` with either run_id still null, captured before the UPDATE) and `PERFORM public.auto_route_order(p_order_id)` right after it; enriched the RPC's return jsonb with `rerouted`/`routing_error`/`pickup_run_id`/`delivery_run_id` reflecting the real post-reroute state instead of assuming success. **Verified live, not just applied:** blast-radius query turned up a second order already sitting in the identical broken state, `42fd28ff-6254-418c-8fa0-2dd32837e504` (scheduled, both run_ids null, routing_error null, zero active route_stops). Re-ran the exact bug path on it — `rollback_order_to_on_hold` then `advance_order_status(..., 'scheduled')` — and the RPC returned `rerouted:true` with both run_ids populated and a clean `routing_error:null`; a follow-up SELECT confirmed 2 active route_stops. Same action doubled as the fix's live test AND the paired one-time remediation (Root-Cause Rule). **New feature — press-and-hold solo view on the RCC eye icon:** click still toggles just that route's visibility (unchanged); holding the icon ~450ms now hides every *other* open route so only that one shows (`rccEyeDown`/`rccEyeUp`/`rccEyeCancel`/`_rccIsRouteSolo`/`rccSoloRoute` in `admin-dashboard/index.html`), holding again restores them all — same single icon, no new button, per David's explicit "click = hide this one, hold = solo this one" spec. **Deployment incident + branch consolidation:** shipped the feature but it never appeared live; traced via direct curl to `admin.familylaundry.com` (Vercel MCP tools were 403'ing) to Vercel's Production Branch being `main` while this and other recent laptop-connected sessions had been committing to a separate `laptop-untracked-2026-07-24` branch that Vercel only ever built as Preview. Per David's call ("having two branches competing sounds messy"), consolidated onto `main` as the single going-forward branch: pushed the laptop branch's unique commits onto `main` from the cloud side via git plumbing (hash-object/read-tree/commit-tree, since a direct `git checkout main` through the device-bridge corrupted the index with false D/MM entries — recovered cleanly with `git reset HEAD`, see Known Issues), then had David run the actual branch switch (`git checkout main`, `git reset --hard origin/main`) in his own Terminal. Confirmed live at commit `64e677b` via direct curl. **Known follow-up, not yet fixed:** the pickup_failed/delivery_failed/skipped/cancelled → scheduled reopen path can still leave a *stale non-null* `pickup_run_id`/`delivery_run_id` pointing at a dead stop — `sync_stops_on_order_terminal` marks those route_stops failed/skipped but never nulls the run_id, so this fix's `IS NULL` check won't catch it. Logged as tech debt (Known Issues #29), intentionally out of scope for this fix.*

*Previously: July 30, 2026 — Session 190 pt 3 — **Found and fixed the real bug behind Olivia's scheduled Aug 1 subscription showing wrong on refresh: `invoice.payment_succeeded` was silently clobbering 'trialing' back to 'active'.** After the pt 2 button fix deployed, David used the new Feature 2 date picker to schedule Olivia onto the Monthly Subscription starting Aug 1 — but refreshing her Customer panel showed the empty "Assign Plan" form again instead of a "Starts Aug 1" scheduled card. Diagnosed with a temporary read-only debug edge function (`debug-stripe-sub`, deployed then retired/neutralized same session — no delete-function tool available from this environment, so it's a 410-stub now; safe to actually delete via the Supabase dashboard whenever convenient) that queried Stripe directly: **Stripe's live subscription was correctly `trialing`** with `trial_end`/`current_period_end` = Aug 1 07:00 UTC, and there was exactly ONE `customer.subscription.created` event on record, whose payload correctly said `status: "trialing"`. But our `subscriptions` table had `status='active'`. Root cause: `stripe-webhook`'s `invoice.payment_succeeded` handler (pre-dates session 190 — originally written for the past_due→active dunning-recovery case) **unconditionally wrote `status: 'active'` on ANY successful invoice**, with no check for what kind of invoice it was. Stripe auto-generates a $0 "trial-start" invoice the instant a `trial_end` subscription is created and immediately marks it `paid` (nothing was due) — which fires this same `invoice.payment_succeeded` event and, ~0.5s after `customer.subscription.created` correctly wrote `'trialing'`, this handler stomped it back to `'active'`. Customer-facing billing was NOT affected (the pricelist-flip gate added earlier in session 190 correctly stayed on `'Delivery'` since it derives from the created/updated handlers, not this one), but every scheduled subscription's *status* silently reverted to full-active immediately on creation — Reports would show her as a paying subscriber on day zero, and `audit_subscriptions_missing_invoice` would have false-positived on her in ~2 hours (status='active' + no real subscription_invoice transaction, since the $0 trial invoice records `amount_paid=0` and skips the transaction insert). **Fix (`stripe-webhook` v51):** the handler now fetches the subscription's live status from Stripe (`stripe.subscriptions.retrieve` → `mapStripeStatus`) instead of hardcoding `'active'` — correctly leaves a trialing subscription alone while still correctly flipping a real dunning-recovery to `'active'`. **Second, smaller bug found + fixed in the same investigation:** admin's `_confirmOptInCharge` re-rendered the Customer panel immediately after `create-subscription` returned, with no wait for `stripe-webhook` to actually write the `subscriptions` row (which is fully async) — a pure race that produced the "empty Assign Plan form" flash David saw the first time, independent of the status bug above. Fixed in `admin-dashboard/index.html` by adding the same 1500ms wait before re-render that customer-app's `confirmSubscribe` already uses for this identical race. **One-time data fix:** Olivia's `subscriptions` row corrected back to `status='trialing'` to match Stripe truth (root-cause code fix above prevents recurrence — this satisfies the Root-Cause Rule, the DB patch and the code fix are paired). **Lesson banked:** when adding a new subscription status value (`'trialing'`) to an existing state machine, audit EVERY handler that writes `subscriptions.status` — not just the ones the new feature obviously touches. `customer.subscription.created` and `.updated` were updated for trialing awareness in the original session-190 pass; `invoice.payment_succeeded` writes the same column and was missed because on the surface it looks unrelated to a "created" flow.*


*Previously: July 30, 2026 — Session 190 pt 2 — **Fixed a gap in the session-190 cancel-choice feature: an already-scheduled-to-cancel subscription had no "Cancel now" button at all.** Found immediately after deploy, on the exact account that motivated the feature: Olivia Rosaldo-Pratt's subscription is `status='active'` + `cancel_at_period_end=true` (staff placed a graceful cancel on Jul 28), which routes `renderCpSubscriptionActions` into the pre-existing `scheduling` branch (session 178) — and that branch only showed "Cancel now" when `status==='past_due'`, otherwise just "Reactivate". So the one scenario Feature 1 was built to fix (force an already-scheduled cancellation to end immediately) had no button for it. **Fix (`admin-dashboard/index.html`, `renderCpSubscriptionActions`):** the `scheduling` branch now always pairs "↺ Reactivate" with "✕ Cancel now" (`adminCancelSubscription(id, true)`), regardless of past_due — no functional risk since `cancel-subscription` v11 already forces immediate for past_due and this just extends the same button to the non-past-due scheduled case. **Immediate workaround used to unblock Olivia same-day** (before this fix deployed): Reactivate → re-render flips her into the "healthy" branch (both buttons) → Cancel immediately. **Lesson for QA:** when a new status-gated UI branch is added, audit for *every* pre-existing branch that could also match the same record — a session-178 branch shipped fine on its own but wasn't revisited when session-190 added a sibling branch below it in the same if/else chain, and the combination left one real production account with no working button. Committed + pushed to `main` via the `laptop-untracked-2026-07-24` local branch (`git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` — this Mac's checkout has been sitting on that branch, not `main`, since a prior session's git-lock recovery; that refspec push is the safe way to land commits on `main` from here without switching branches).*


*Previously: July 30, 2026 — Session 190 — **Subscription cancel-choice (immediate vs. end-of-term) + scheduled start date (up to 30 days out), prompted by the Olivia Rosaldo-Pratt incident.** Olivia opted into Subscription on Jul 22 wanting an Aug 1 start; staff refunded her $275 on Jul 28 believing the subscription was cancelled immediately, but `cancel-subscription` had exactly one behavior for a paid-up subscription — always `cancel_at_period_end=true` — so she stayed listed "active" through her Aug 22 term end. Two features shipped to close this gap for good. **Feature 1 (`cancel-subscription` v11):** on an otherwise-healthy (active/paused) subscription, admin now gets an explicit choice — "Cancel immediately" or "Cancel at end of term" — instead of always silently taking the period-end path. New `immediate` request param, gated to staff (`assertOwnership` extended to recognize `manager` as staff, returns `{ok, isStaff}`); past_due and trialing subscriptions remain forced-immediate regardless of the flag (no unpaid invoice or not-yet-started subscription should ever "run out a term"). Response now carries a `reason` (`past_due` / `trialing_scheduled` / `staff_immediate`) for logging clarity. **Feature 2 — scheduled subscription start date, customer + admin, ≤30 days out:** implemented with **zero DB migration** by reusing Stripe's native `trial_end`/`trialing` mechanism — `'trialing'` was already an allowed value in the `subscriptions.status` CHECK constraint, and `current_period_end` while trialing doubles as the scheduled-start date, so no new column was needed. `resolveTrialEndEpoch(startDate)` (Pacific-anchored, BIZ_TZ) validates the date is today..+30 and converts to a Stripe trial_end epoch; duplicated identically in `create-subscription` (v7 — customer-app saved-card + new-card paths, admin opt-in-with-card path) and `create-checkout` (v36 — admin "Start Checkout" fallback for a customer with no saved card). **`stripe-webhook` v50:** the pricelist-flip-to-'Subscription' on `customer.subscription.created` is now gated behind `subStatus === 'active'` (skipped while trialing, so a scheduled subscriber keeps normal pay-per-order pricing/booking until their chosen date); since Stripe doesn't fire a distinct "trial ended" event, a new block in `customer.subscription.updated` does the flip the moment status flows trialing→active. **UI (`admin-dashboard/index.html`, `customer-app/index.html`):** `'trialing'` recognized everywhere subscription status is read — badges ("SCHEDULED" / "Starts on {date}"), Reports subscriber table + status-color map, renewal-label logic; admin Customer panel gained a date input (`#cpb-start-date`, min=today/max=+30) threaded through both the opt-in and Start Checkout paths with scheduled-vs-immediate confirm-modal copy; customer-app subscribe modals (saved-card and new-card) gained the same date picker, with a dedicated "scheduled" home-card state and cancel-copy branch. **QA (washroute-qa) — clean, no issues found:** confirmed RLS on `subscriptions` has no status restriction (customer can read their own trialing row fine); confirmed `audit_subscription_pricelist_orphans`, `audit_subscriptions_missing_invoice`, `enforce_sub_not_past_due`, and `link_subscription_on_order_fn` all filter on `status IN ('active','past_due','paused')` and so correctly exclude `'trialing'` — a scheduled subscriber won't false-positive the daily audit, won't get subscription $0-delivery pricing early, and won't get their normal pay-per-order pickups auto-linked to the not-yet-started subscription; confirmed every `create-subscription`/`create-checkout` call site threads `startDate` correctly; confirmed customer-app only calls `showSubscriptionPickupPicker()` for an immediate-start subscription (a scheduled one correctly skips it and keeps normal booking, per David's confirmed requirement). All 4 edge functions deployed and live (`cancel-subscription` v11, `create-subscription` v7, `create-checkout` v36, `stripe-webhook` v50). ⚠️ **DEPLOY PENDING — UI not yet live:** `admin-dashboard/index.html` + `customer-app/index.html` are updated and written to disk in the project folder, but hit the sandbox's chronic `.git/index.lock` issue (see many prior session notes — a stale/regenerating lock the sandbox cannot remove) before the commit could go through here. **David needs to commit + push from his Mac terminal** — exact paste-block is in this session's chat. The 4 edge functions are backward-compatible, so nothing is broken live in the meantime; the two new UI affordances (cancel-choice buttons, start-date pickers) simply aren't visible yet. ⚠️ **Olivia's own subscription record is still out of sync** — Stripe + the `subscriptions` row both still show `status='active'`, `cancel_at_period_end=true`, `current_period_end='2026-08-22'` (her `customers.pricelist` was already separately reverted to Delivery/paygo by staff, so she isn't being mis-priced, but the subscription record itself is wrong). **Fix once this deploy is live:** open her Customer panel → Billing and click the new "Cancel immediately" button — this is the correct root-cause-consistent fix (syncs Stripe + DB together in one transaction, proper audit trail) rather than a raw DB patch, and is literally the mechanism Feature 1 was built to provide.*


*Previously: July 29, 2026 — Session 189 — **Finished SMS centralization: every order-status/reschedule text now fires from inside the RPC that changes the record, not from the UI button that called it** (migrations `session_189_generalize_status_and_reschedule_notifications` + `session_189b_reschedule_notify_opt_out`; `driver-app/index.html`, `admin-dashboard/index.html`, `customer-app/index.html`). Follow-up to session 188's walk-in-ready fix: while researching what architectural gap to close next, found the exact same fragility pattern still present everywhere else. Driver-app's `completeStop()`/`cantCompleteStop()` called `complete_route_stop`/`skip_route_stop` to change the order's status, then made a **separate, independent** `fetch()` to `send-order-notification` right after — if that second call failed (dropped network, backgrounded tab, killed app) the DB said "delivered" but the customer never got a text, with nothing surfacing the miss. Admin-dashboard had a 7-call-site helper (`_maybeNotifyReschedule`) re-implementing "did the window actually move?" after every `reschedule_order_leg` call, and customer-app duplicated that logic independently — less carefully, firing off the RPC's raw `pickup_changed`/`delivery_changed` flags (true for *any* requested change, including a route-only reassignment where the time never moved). **Root-cause fix:** generalized `advance_order_status`'s existing walk-in-ready notify block (session 183/188) into a full transition→event map (`picked_up`, `delivered`, `pickup_failed`, `delivery_failed`, plus the existing `walkin_order_ready`), added a `p_notify_sms boolean DEFAULT true` param so an admin override can suppress it, and moved the "only text on a real window change" gate into `reschedule_order` itself (comparing before/after values it already has), with a `p_notify boolean DEFAULT true` param for the one UI path (`rccConfirmMove`'s "Move stops" modal) that has its own explicit "text customers?" checkbox. Both signature changes required `DROP FUNCTION` + `CREATE FUNCTION` (confirmed empirically: `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION` with a different argument count silently creates a **second overload** instead of erroring or replacing — verified with a throwaway test function before touching the real RPCs — which would have left every existing 6-arg caller of `advance_order_status` silently resolving to the old, unmodified function). Regranted `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC/anon` + `GRANT ... TO authenticated, service_role` on all three functions per the SECURITY DEFINER checklist. **Also fixed a real inconsistency found along the way:** admin's `opSetOrderStatus`/`setSingleOrderStatus`/`batchSetStatus`/`batchAdvanceStatus` deliberately skip SMS on manual overrides (session 167 rule — the real text should come from the driver's actual action, not an admin dropdown), but `updateOrderStatus` (the Orders-tab "Advance" button) fired it anyway, contradicting that rule; confirmed with David and aligned `updateOrderStatus` with the rule by passing `p_notify_sms:false` like every other override path, removing its manual SMS block. **Client-side removals:** driver-app's two manual SMS blocks (now dead code — the RPC fires it); admin's `_maybeNotifyReschedule` helper and all 7 call sites (`opSaveRouteAndSlot`, `selectSpSlot`, `confirmReassignRun`, `rccDrop`, `rccConfirmMove`, `rccConfirmReschedule`, one more `rccDrop`-adjacent site) — `rccConfirmMove` now passes its checkbox value through as `p_notify` instead; customer-app's duplicate reschedule-notify block plus the now-unused `_pickupRescheduled`/`_deliveryRescheduled` tracking vars. **Verified via direct RPC calls** (mocked `service_role` JWT via `SET LOCAL request.jwt.claim.role`), not just reasoning: created disposable test orders, drove `advance_order_status` through `picked_up`/`pickup_failed`/`delivery_failed`/`walkin_order_ready` (each correctly returned `notified_event`), confirmed `p_notify_sms:false` suppressed it (`notified_event:null`), confirmed `reschedule_order` fired on a real window change and stayed silent on a re-applied identical window (the exact no-op gate `_maybeNotifyReschedule` used to enforce client-side), and confirmed `reschedule_order_leg`'s new `p_notify:false` suppresses even a real window change (the `rccConfirmMove` case). Cross-checked `net._http_response` — all 6 `net.http_post` calls made during testing returned HTTP 200 from `send-order-notification`, confirming the edge function was actually invoked each time. All test orders/customers deleted after. `node --check` clean on all three edited app files; reviewed the full `git diff` on all three files line-by-line before committing — matched intent exactly. **Explicitly out of scope** (unchanged): the `confirmed` event (single call site per app, low risk); `out_for_delivery`/driver-app's `sendOnMyWay()` — investigated and confirmed `notify-on-my-way` already does the `route_stops` write and the SMS send atomically inside one edge function call, with its own stale-stop guard and opt-out check, so it isn't part of this fragility class. **Skill flag:** the `washroute` skill's "Pending RPCs" section (item 3, `complete_route_stop`) is stale — that RPC has been shipped and wired since session 136; the skill's static pending-list wasn't updated when it shipped, which is what sent this session investigating a dead lead before the actual scope (this SMS-centralization work) was found through fresh code reading. Worth a skill refresh next time someone's in there.*

*Last updated: July 29, 2026 — Session 188 — **Fixed: POS/retail customers not always getting the "your laundry is ready for pickup" text** (migration `session_183_advance_order_status_walkin_ready_sms`, `pos/index.html`, `admin-dashboard/index.html`; no code commit yet — see DEPLOY note). David reported a POS customer complained they never got a ready-for-pickup SMS even though "we thought we had this set up." Investigation confirmed the `walkin_order_ready` template in `message_templates` was correctly configured and the `send-order-notification` edge function handled it correctly — the notification **worked, but only from one single call site**: POS's own Retail Queue "Mark Ready" button (`advanceQueueOrder()` in `pos/index.html`), which explicitly calls `sendPosTemplateSms('walkin_order_ready', ...)` after advancing status. Every OTHER admin-dashboard path that can advance a walk-in order to `ready_for_delivery` — the order detail panel (`opSetOrderStatus`), the Orders-table row advance button (`updateOrderStatus`), the single-order Set-Status modal (`setSingleOrderStatus`), and both batch actions (`batchSetStatus`/`batchAdvanceStatus`) — call the same `advance_order_status` RPC but never fire any customer notification for that transition, so a walk-in order advanced from the admin Orders tab instead of the POS queue silently sent nothing. Separately found (and fixed) a second, independent bug in the same area: admin's Folding/Rack panel (`saveRacking()`) had its own OLD hardcoded walk-in SMS block that predates the `walkin_order_ready` template — it called `send-sms` directly with a hand-written message (so editing the template in the admin Notifications tab wouldn't have changed what this path sent) and, worse, `send-sms`'s `STAFF_SMS_ROLES` allowlist excludes the `laundry_tech` role, so if the one `laundry_tech` staff account in the system ever racked a walk-in order, the text would 403 silently (caught via `console.warn` only, invisible in the UI). Confirmed via live query this specific path was never actually hit in practice (`orders.racked_at` is NULL for every `source='walk_in'` row — walk-in orders never flow through the physical rack panel, only the POS queue) but removed it anyway as dead/duplicate risk. **Real-world evidence pulled from `order_events` + `sms_messages`:** of 457 walk-in `ready_for_delivery` transitions logged historically, 444 had a matching SMS (the POS-queue path works correctly ~97% of the time); the 13 misses were mostly a placeholder commercial phone number (`(666) 666-6666`, "City of Oakland" test/muni account) but ~5 were real-looking customer phone numbers spread across May–July — consistent with staff occasionally advancing a walk-in order through the admin dashboard instead of the POS queue, exactly the gap this fix closes. **Root-cause fix (not a per-button patch):** moved the notification into `advance_order_status` itself — the one RPC every call site (current and future, in any of the four apps) already goes through — via a fire-and-forget `net.http_post` call to `send-order-notification` whenever a transition lands on `ready_for_delivery` for a `source='walk_in'` order coming from any other status (mirrors the existing `notify_customer_registered` trigger + the `wr-reminder-evening`/`wr-klaviyo-nightly-sync` cron jobs, which already call edge functions the same way via `net.http_post` — established, working pattern in this codebase, not a new one). Removed the now-redundant client-side calls in both `pos/index.html` (`advanceQueueOrder`) and `admin-dashboard/index.html` (`saveRacking`) to avoid double-texting the customer. **Verified live, not just reasoned through:** created a real test order (`source='walk_in'`, tied to the existing `Matt SuRefferalTest` test-customer account with its fake phone), called `advance_order_status` as `service_role`, confirmed the RPC's response included `notified_walkin_ready:true`, and confirmed via `net._http_response` + edge function logs that `send-order-notification` was actually invoked, found the `walkin_order_ready` template, and attempted the send (rejected only by Twilio's phone-format check, since the test number is fake) — all test data cleaned up after (`order_events` + `orders` rows deleted). `node --check` clean on both edited files. **Note on session numbering:** in-code comments in the migration and both files refer to this as "session 183" — an assumption made mid-session that wasn't checked against this file before writing code, the same mistake flagged in the session 187 pt 2 entry below. Left as-is (no functional effect) rather than re-touching ~6 scattered comments; the actual session number is 188. **Not fixed here (flagged as follow-up):** `send-sms`'s `STAFF_SMS_ROLES` role-allowlist gap (excludes `laundry_tech`) is now moot for walk-in-ready specifically (that path no longer calls `send-sms` at all), but the same gap could still silently swallow any OTHER manually-triggered staff SMS if a `laundry_tech`-role user ever triggers one — worth auditing if a similar "SMS didn't send" report ever traces back to that role. Also, the migration hardcodes the Supabase anon key inline (matching the existing pattern already used by every `net.http_post` cron job in `cron.job`, e.g. `wr-reminder-evening`) rather than pulling it from `vault` — consistent with current practice here, not a new gap, but worth revisiting project-wide someday. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** the DB migration is already LIVE (Postgres functions apply immediately, no separate deploy step). The two `pos/index.html` / `admin-dashboard/index.html` edits (plus this note) are committed locally to `laptop-untracked-2026-07-24` but **not yet pushed** — from David's Mac: `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` (same two-branch push this project always needs — see session 187 pt 3 above for why skipping the explicit `origin/main` push has silently left features undeployed before).*
