That gap is exactly what let a fully-built, DB-tested feature sit invisible to every driver for a full day while this file said DEPLOYED.*

*Previously: July 28, 2026 — Session 187 — **Mandatory bag-check gate + missing-bag reassignment** (commit `61af298`, `driver-app/index.html` + `admin-dashboard/index.html` + migrations `session_bag_check_gate_and_reassignment`, `session_193_fix_authenticated_grants`, `session_193_fix_null_bag_exception_flag`). David noticed the driver-app's Bag Checklist (session 191) was effectively unused — it was an optional collapsible preview that self-hid around 5pm, so drivers never had to interact with it. Redesigned it as a **mandatory, full-screen gate** shown once per shift period (am/pm, split by route if a driver has multiple routes in one shift) before "Start my shift" unlocks — driver must tap On Rack/Missing on every delivery bag; pickup-only stops need no check; missing bags notify dispatch but do NOT block the driver from starting. **New DB layer:** `driver_shift_bag_checks` (one row per driver/date/shift_period once cleared, via new RPC `clear_shift_bag_check`) and `driver_alerts` (in-app banner queue, realtime-enabled) tables; 3 new `route_stops` columns (`bag_check_flagged_driver_id`, `bag_check_resolved_at`, `bag_check_resolved_by`) plus a widened `bag_check_status` CHECK to allow `'found_elsewhere'`. **The reassignment-resolves-the-mixup flow** (a real scenario David described: drivers sometimes end up with each other's bags, dispatch calls around, then reassigns the delivery): admin's reassign-driver modal (`openReassignDriver`/`confirmReassignDriver` — existed as unwired dead code before this session, now wired up from a new "Reassign" button in the Bags Not On Rack Overview widget) now routes explicit reassignments through a new SECURITY DEFINER RPC `resolve_missing_bag_and_reassign`, which atomically clears the missing flag, alerts the newly-assigned driver (red "bag exception" banner if this was a real exception, calmer "routine" banner for an ordinary mid-shift reassignment — David explicitly pushed back on overusing red and this was the agreed balance), notifies the original flagged driver it's resolved ("Found — you may already have this bag" → "Found. Francisco has it, nothing more you need to do."), and keeps a resolved-history record the Bags Not On Rack widget now surfaces. Driver-app gained a global alert-banner stack (`loadPendingDriverAlerts`/`subscribeToDriverAlerts`/`renderDriverAlertStack`/`acknowledgeDriverAlert`) driven by `driver_alerts`. In-app only, no SMS fallback (David's call — dispatch already calls drivers directly). Orders → Map view deliberately left untouched (David: "we are moving orders from one route to another — the driver assigned to that route can change," so the map should track routes/geography, not transient per-stop overrides). **QA catches (self-found, fixed before commit):** (1) the bottom nav was visible+clickable while the mandatory gate was up, letting a driver tap away and bypass it entirely — fixed by deferring nav visibility until the gate clears and adding a `_bagGateActive` guard inside `goTo()` as defense-in-depth; (2) `renderBagGate`/`_bagGateRow` called an `esc()` helper that doesn't exist in driver-app (only in admin-dashboard) — would have silently no-op'd the entire gate forever via the fail-open catch block, never crashing but never showing either; removed (driver-app has no HTML-escaping convention anywhere, matches existing rack-check code); (3) added a periodic re-check (`refreshStopsQuietly`) so a driver on a genuine split AM/PM shift who never logs out between them still gets gated for the second period. **Live-tested against `umjpbuxrdydwejqtensq`** (schema/RLS/grants/publication verified read-only, then both RPCs exercised end-to-end with real driver/admin rows and a terminal-status test stop, all test data cleaned up after): caught and fixed a real grant-hygiene gap — `authenticated` had inherited the still-active pre-Oct-30-2026 Supabase default (auto-grant ALL on new `public` tables), so it had DELETE/TRUNCATE/unrestricted UPDATE on both new tables despite the migration's narrower intended GRANTs (the initial migration REVOKEd the default from `anon` but never REVOKEd it from `authenticated` before layering the narrow GRANT on top) — RLS backstopped the actual exposure (no INSERT/DELETE policy existed, and the UPDATE policy is row-scoped) but closed it to match intent. Also tightened `resolve_missing_bag_and_reassign`'s `was_bag_exception` return field, which resolved to SQL NULL instead of FALSE for non-exception reassignments (three-valued-logic artifact of `bag_check_status = 'missing'` when the column is NULL) — harmless in JS (both falsy) but cleaned up for a correct boolean contract. **Note on session numbering:** in-code comments throughout both files and the three migrations refer to this as "session 193," inherited from an earlier assumption in this session's conversation that wasn't checked against this file — the actual next number was 187. Left as-is rather than churning ~30 scattered comment references for a label with no functional effect; flagging here for anyone cross-referencing "session 193" against this log. ✅ **DEPLOY:** commit `61af298` was pushed successfully by David from his own Mac (confirmed via `git log origin/laptop-untracked-2026-07-24..HEAD` showing 0 unpushed commits). ⚠️ **NOT visually tested in the actual driver-app/admin-dashboard UI from here** — DB layer and RPC logic are live-tested and correct; verify in-app once pushed: (a) driver logs in during a route's pre-window → sees the full-screen gate, taps through, bottom nav stays hidden until cleared and no tab can be tapped away to; (b) admin's Bags Not On Rack widget → Reassign on a missing bag → picks a driver → toast + resolved-history row appears; (c) the picked driver's app shows the red banner, the original flagged driver's app shows the calm "found" banner, both dismissable via "Got it".* **Session 187 pt 2 (same day) — QA + security review, all High/Medium fixed:** David asked for a deep QA and security pass on the above feature. Found and fixed 5 real issues (2 High, 3 Medium), all independently verified against live code/DB before and after the fix, not just agent-reported. **(1, HIGH)** `clear_shift_bag_check` had no bound on `p_run_date` — a driver could open devtools and loop-call it across every future date/period once, permanently pre-clearing the gate and defeating the entire feature; fixed by restricting `p_run_date` to yesterday/today/tomorrow in `America/Los_Angeles` time (narrow window chosen to tolerate client-clock skew around midnight, same TZ convention as `auto_fail_expired_orders` etc). **(2, HIGH)** Stored XSS: `resolve_missing_bag_and_reassign` built `driver_alerts.body`/`title` from raw `customers.first_name_cache`/`last_name_cache` with no escaping, and `renderDriverAlertStack()` in driver-app inserted them into `innerHTML` with no escaping either — two unescaped layers, no safety net; fixed by adding a new `_escHtml()` helper (driver-app previously had none at all) and escaping at the single render choke-point so it covers every current/future writer of `driver_alerts`. **(3, MEDIUM)** `resolve_missing_bag_and_reassign` never checked `drivers.is_active` for the new driver or `route_stops.status` for terminal states (`complete`/`skipped`/`failed`) before reassigning — fixed with explicit checks, both live-tested to confirm they reject correctly. **(4, MEDIUM)** the `_bagGateActive` guard only lived in `goTo()`, so 4 direct `showScreen('route')` calls inside realtime `postgres_changes` handlers could tear the mandatory gate off-screen in a narrow race (driver has a stop open when a new shift period triggers the gate, then a realtime event fires for that stop) — fixed by moving the guard into `showScreen()` itself (exempting `'bag-gate'` and `'auth'` so the gate and sign-out always still work), closing every direct call site at once instead of patching four individually. **(5, MEDIUM)** `_bagGateActive`/`_bagGatePeriod`/`_bagGateStops` were never reset on sign-out or session-loss, and being module-level in a no-reload SPA meant a driver who signed out mid-gate could come back to a silently blank/stuck home screen — fixed by resetting all three in both `doSignOut()` and the auth listener's no-session branch. Two Low-severity items also fixed as part of the same pass: admin-dashboard's `loadBagCheckIssues()` was silently swallowing the resolved-history query's error (now logged via `console.error`, matching house convention); `openReassignDriver()`'s driver-name dropdown label was interpolated unescaped (now wrapped in the existing `esc()` helper). DB changes applied via migration `session_187_qa_security_fixes_bag_check_rpcs` and live-tested end-to-end (out-of-range date, inactive driver, and terminal-status stop all correctly rejected; the valid/happy path still succeeds) before committing — all test calls wrapped in `BEGIN…ROLLBACK` so zero residual data. Code changes committed as `ab0e7d3` on `laptop-untracked-2026-07-24`; **not yet pushed — David needs to `git push` from his Mac to deploy this fix.** ⚠️ **NOT visually re-tested in the actual driver-app/admin-dashboard UI** — same caveat as pt 1: DB layer and the specific guard logic are live-tested and correct, but the UI flows (gate can't be torn off by a realtime event, sign-out/sign-in no longer soft-locks, reassign rejects an inactive driver in the actual modal) have not been clicked through in a browser.

*Previously: July 27, 2026 — Session 186 — **Order Schedule: per-day driver override — "Just this day" vs "Every &lt;Day&gt;, going forward"** (commit `5e824df`, `admin-dashboard/index.html` + migration `session_186_driver_override_single_day`). David remembered discussing a way to swap a route's driver for one specific date without changing the recurring default — investigation found the feature had never actually been built (checked git stash, 3 temp branches, draft-migrations folders — nothing there), but the **DB half already existed and was unused**: `route_driver_overrides` (exact `override_date`, NOT `route_driver_schedule`'s `day_of_week`) + `resolve_route_driver(template_id, date, driver_type)` (precedence: exact-date override → weekly schedule → template default) were built at some earlier point but **only consulted by `generate_route_runs`** (the nightly 14-day-ahead route pre-generation cron) — `route_driver_overrides` had 0 rows in production, meaning nothing ever wrote to it. **Root-cause fix (not just a UI patch):** `auto_route_order`, `sync_pickup_stop_on_window_change`, and `sync_delivery_stop_on_window_change` — the three functions that create a route on the fly (new order booked, or a reschedule lands on a date with no route yet) — all queried `route_driver_schedule` directly, so a one-day override set on a date more than ~14 days out (before the nightly cron would have created that route) would've been silently ignored the moment an order forced early creation. Migration re-points all three through `resolve_route_driver()`, called with `driver_type='pickup'` at every site (not `'delivery'` — `route_driver_schedule` has zero `'delivery'`-typed rows today; the surrounding code has always applied one driver to `driver_id`/`pickup_driver_id`/`delivery_driver_id` alike, so filtering the delivery leg by `driver_type='delivery'` would've returned NULL for every delivery route — caught in migration review before applying, not after). **Admin UI:** the driver popover (Orders → Order Schedule → Driver Schedule grid, function `openDriverPopover`/`assignDriverOverride`) gains a segmented toggle — **"Just this day"** (new default) writes `route_driver_overrides` and, if a route already exists for that exact date, updates it immediately (no waiting on the cron); **"Every &lt;Day&gt;, going forward"** is the pre-existing behavior unchanged (writes `route_driver_schedule`, propagates to every current+future route matching that day-of-week). Refactored the propagate-and-cascade logic into a shared `cascadeDriverToRoutes()` helper used by both scopes so pending/en_route-only stop reassignment stays identical either way. **Blast radius audit found + fixed 2 more call sites** that resolve a driver from `route_driver_schedule` alone: `opCreateRouteAndSelect` (admin's manual "create route for this order" path in the order panel) now checks a new `_opDriverOverrides` cache first; the Route Command Center's null-driver fallback (`_rccDriverSched`) now checks a same-date `_rccDriverOverride` map first. Both writers (`assignDriverForSingleDay`/`assignDriverGoingForward`) now check `.error` on their Supabase calls and skip the success toast on failure — the pre-existing code showed "Driver updated!" unconditionally with no error handling at all. **⚠️ Known limitation (documented in-code):** `route_driver_overrides.driver_id` is `NOT NULL`, so "Unassigned" + "Just this day" can only clear a *previously-set* one-day override — it can't record "explicitly no driver just for this day" on a date more than ~14 days out where no route has materialized yet and a weekly default exists (it'll fall through to that default once the route is created). Once a route exists for the date — true for every near-term day — "Unassigned" always works correctly because it updates that route's `driver_id` directly. **QA:** `node --check` clean on the full inline script both before and after the blast-radius fixes; RLS confirmed identical (`is_admin()`) between `route_driver_schedule` and `route_driver_overrides`, no gap; explicit `.select()` columns everywhere, no `select('*')`. **Tested live against production Supabase** (not a staging env — none exists yet, see the staging thin-slice pending item): simulated the full flow on Oakland PM's real Monday route (`0fe884ef…`, 2026-08-03, 7 real pending stops) — override written, route + all 7 stops reassigned to a second driver, weekly default (`route_driver_schedule`) confirmed untouched, an unrelated Monday one week later confirmed still resolving to the original weekly-default driver, then a second test proved `resolve_route_driver()` returns the override driver for a date **28 days out with no `routes` row yet** (the exact gap the migration closes) — **all test writes fully reverted**, `route_driver_overrides` confirmed back to 0 rows after cleanup. ⚠️ **DEPLOY: commit `5e824df` needs `git push` from David's Mac** (this environment's device bridge has no network access to push directly) — DB migration is already LIVE and behavior-preserving on its own (the table was empty, so nothing changes until the new UI actually writes to it). **Post-deploy fix `4f2eb7f`:** the popover's "Every &lt;Day&gt;" scope toggle silently closed the whole popover instead of switching scope — clicking it called `setDriverPopoverScope`, which re-renders `#driver-popover-scope`'s innerHTML synchronously, detaching the clicked div from the DOM before the click finished bubbling to `document`. The "click outside closes popover" listener then saw a detached `e.target` (no longer `contains()`-ed by the popover, since it had just been removed from the tree) and closed it — even though the click was inside. "Just this day" never showed the bug because it's the default scope, so nobody had to click it; clicking "Every &lt;Day&gt;" always hit it. Fixed with `event.stopPropagation()` on the toggle's onclick, matching the pattern already used in `openDriverPopover`. Verified live in Chrome against admin.familylaundry.com (confirmed broken before, confirmed fixed after — toggle switches and popover stays open). **Blast radius checked:** the file's two other outside-click-via-`contains()` listeners (archive-pattern popover, SMS compose dropdown) don't re-render their own container on an item click before hiding/removing it, so neither is exposed to the same failure mode. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `4f2eb7f` needs `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac (this environment's device bridge has no network access to push directly). **Follow-up `c3bd904` — wrong driver shown as "selected" on the Every &lt;Day&gt; tab:** David tested the toggle fix, then noticed switching to "Every Saturday" showed a driver already checked (blue highlight) even though nothing had been saved for that scope — he proposed adding a Save button. Investigated instead of taking the patch: the popover's one-click-to-assign pattern is used everywhere else in this dashboard, so a second commit step would be a real UX inconsistency, not a fix. **Actual root cause:** the driver list's "selected" highlight was computed once from `currentDriverId` (always the tier-1 exact-date override) when the popover opened, and never recomputed on scope-tab switch — so the "forward" tab kept showing the day's override driver as selected regardless of the real weekly default. Confirmed via DB: Concord AM has **zero** `route_driver_schedule` rows for Saturday — no forward default exists at all — yet David was shown checked. Nothing was broken about saving; the checkbox was lying about current state. **Fix:** both grid-render functions already compute `overMap[template-day-pickup]` (the tier-3 weekly value) to draw the cell; reused that same already-fetched value as `weeklyDrvId`, threaded through `openDriverPopover` as `weeklyDriverId` onto `popoverCtx`. Hoisted the driver-list renderer out of `openDriverPopover`'s closure into a shared `renderDriverPopoverOptions()` that picks `currentDriverId` vs `weeklyDriverId` based on `popoverCtx.scope`, called on every tab switch from `setDriverPopoverScope()`. No extra network call — reuses data already loaded for the grid. `node --check` clean. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `c3bd904` needs the same  **Follow-up `3bbf6f7` — uniform driver-chip width on the weekly grid:** David flagged the chips on Orders > Schedule as messy since `.sched-cell` had no fixed width (`display:inline-flex` + `white-space:nowrap` sized each pill to its own text), so columns didn't line up. Measured the live rendered width of "Unassigned" (79.7px — the widest label in the current driver/status set) and set a fixed `width:84px` + `overflow:hidden;text-overflow:ellipsis` (inert safety net for any future longer driver name). Locked-route chips share the class so they're fixed for free; the `—` no-route placeholder is a plain span, left untouched by design. Verified visually in Chrome by injecting the rule live before committing. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `3bbf6f7` needs the same `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac. **Follow-up `25638c6` — RCC quick-reschedule offered closed days:** **New `dae4072` — orders no longer deliver while unpaid; Overview widget renamed:** David flagged that Overview > "Unpaid Delivered Orders" showed 3 orders that had already gone out to customers with a failed card charge (billing_status='failed') — asked that going forward, no order should reach the customer unpaid, and that the widget be renamed to "Unpaid Orders" (it already covered ready_for_delivery/out_for_delivery, not just delivered — the old title undersold its own scope). Root cause: `chargeOnReady` (session 178, admin-dashboard) deliberately still advances an order to `ready_for_delivery` on a declined charge — by design, so it surfaces in Issues — but nothing downstream ever re-checked `billing_status` before a driver could complete the delivery stop. Fix: extended driver-app's existing `isGhostDelivery` gate (already hides delivery stops for orders stuck in picked_up/processing/folding — the "ghost delivery" case) to also hide any delivery stop where `billing_status='failed'`, with an on_account exemption (invoiced customers are never card-charged via `chargeOnReady`, so a stray 'failed' flag on one of their orders is stale/unrelated — found + confirmed exactly 2 such historical rows via live query, both already delivered, zero live impact). No RPC changes needed — both `get_driver_route_stops` and `get_driver_override_stops` already return the full `orders`+`customers` rows via `to_jsonb()`. Applies at all 3 existing `isGhostDelivery` call sites (main stops, reassigned/override stops, upcoming preview). As soon as the card is updated and the order re-charged, the stop reappears automatically — no manual "release" step. Admin's Route Command Center map view is unchanged by design (still shows these stops via the existing "not ready" badge pattern) since admin needs full visibility to work the case. **Verified live:** order #10914 (Donald Chu, the exact Ready-for-Delivery/FAILED case from the report) has a pending delivery stop scheduled for 2026-07-28 (tomorrow) and `billing_type=null` (not on_account) — confirmed it will now correctly stay hidden from the driver until paid. **Suggested follow-up (not built, offered to David):** mirror this with a "💳 payment failed" badge on the admin Route Command Center map card (next to the existing "not ready" 🧺 badge) so admin can see at a glance which planned stops are blocked on payment vs. still processing. David confirmed the push — commit `dae4072` is now live on `main` (deployed via Vercel). **New `0e8d42b` — Driver View tab (read-only admin mirror of the driver app):** David asked for a way to see what a driver sees on their app, framed as a "Driver View tab that lets us switch between accounts." Asked two design questions first: read-only mirror vs. full impersonation (David: read-only — much lower risk, no chance of accidentally taking an action as the driver), and new admin tab vs. opening the real driver-app logged in as them (David: new tab in admin-dashboard). Landed as a new sub-tab on the existing Drivers page (which already had a tab-bar mechanism, down to one visible tab since Messages moved to Slack in session 147) rather than a new top-level nav item. Un-hid the tab bar, retired the now-redundant standalone "+ New Driver" header row, and made `showPage` preserve the last-active driver sub-tab (`_activeDriverTab`) instead of hardcoding 'manage' every visit. Pick a driver + date → queries `route_stops` for that date joined to `routes`, keeps stops owned by that driver or individually overridden to them (same ownership rule as the `get_driver_route_stops` RPC), resolves orders/customers/addresses (same fallback pattern as the Route Command Center), and renders two lists: what's actually visible on their app, and what's hidden and why. `dvHiddenReason()` is a direct port of driver-app's `isGhostDelivery` (both the "not processed yet" and the "payment failed" cases from the last two sessions) — commented that it must be kept in sync if that logic changes again. **V1 scope, not yet mirrored (called out in code + noted here for later):** multi-day carry-forward of missed stops, and the upcoming-later-today-route preview. **Verified by hand:** ran the equivalent SQL against production for Donald Chu's driver/date — 8 stops total, 7 land in "visible", #10914 lands in "hidden" with "Payment failed", matching what the real driver app shows. **Could not live-browser-test** (new, not-yet-deployed feature — no live-but-broken version to test against like the last two fixes); `node --check` clean on the full inline script. ⚠️ **DEPLOY + REVIEW:** commit `0e8d42b` needs `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac, and since this couldn't be live-tested pre-ship, David should click through Drivers → Driver View after deploying and report back anything that looks off. **Follow-up `75f8634` — fixed the embed error + added a Map-page shortcut:** David hit "Could not embed because more than one relationship was found for route_stops and routes" opening Driver View. Root cause: `route_stops` has two FKs to `routes` — `route_id` and `moved_from_route_id` (pre-existing, tracks stops moved between routes) — so PostgREST can't infer which one an unqualified `routes(...)` embed means. Fixed with an explicit FK hint: `routes!route_stops_route_id_fkey!inner(...)`. **Blast radius found 2 more hits of the identical bug** (grepped for the same embed pattern): `loadRouteAssignments()` (admin-dashboard customer panel's "Route Assignments" section) — this one is LIVE and, since the ambiguity is a hard schema fact not a conditional one, has almost certainly been silently broken in production for as long as `moved_from_route_id` has existed; and `runDailyCheck()`'s check #2 — dead code (explicitly "disabled — WIP, re-enable when ready," never called) fixed anyway so it's not a landmine later. Also shipped the second ask: a button on each route's header in Route > Map (next to the existing pin-visibility eye icon) that jumps straight into Driver View pre-loaded with that route's driver and the Map page's current date (`rccOpenDriverView`) — threads the already-resolved `driverId` through `_rccRouteData` so no extra query is needed, and explicitly awaits `loadDrivers()` before switching tabs so the dropdown is populated even on a cold first visit. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `75f8634` needs the same push from David's Mac; worth re-testing Driver View plus the customer panel's Route Assignments section (which may not have visibly "worked" before either — good to confirm it's now showing routes). **Follow-up `c403565` — Map-page shortcut opens in a new tab:** David wanted the Route > Map view to stay put instead of being navigated away from when jumping to Driver View. `rccOpenDriverView` now calls `window.open('#drivers/driverview/<id>/<date>', '_blank')` instead of driving `showPage`/`switchDriverTab` in place — reuses the app's existing `#page/subtab` hash-restore mechanism (the one that already reopens e.g. `#orders/map` after a refresh) rather than inventing a new one, just extended with two extra hash segments for driver + date on this one route. Kept the click handler synchronous (no `await` before `window.open`) so it fires as a direct result of the click and isn't popup-blocked. **Follow-up `f0ea023` — hid the login-form flash on fresh loads:** David noticed the new tab briefly shows the login page before landing on Driver View. Root cause is sitewide, not specific to this feature: `#login-screen` is `display:flex` by default in CSS, so the raw sign-in form is the very first thing painted on ANY fresh load, before JS has had a chance to check localStorage for an existing session — just rarely seen before since staff mostly keep one long-lived tab open. Fixed by starting the login *card* itself at `display:none` behind a plain spinner (`#login-boot-spinner`, same background as the login screen), revealed the instant `onAuthStateChange` fires with an answer either way — placed at the very top of that callback so it can't be skipped by the `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` early-return or any other branch. Already-logged-in loads now show a brief spinner instead of a flash of the sign-in form; genuinely-logged-out loads see the real form a beat later instead of a flash-then-reappear. Existing 10s null-session grace timer unaffected — it runs after this reveal, not instead of it. David flagged that the map-page “Reschedule N pickups/deliveries” quick action listed Sunday as a pickable target date even though WashRoute runs zero routes on Sundays. Root cause: this was the one date-picker in the app that never checked `route_templates.schedule_days` — the other three (new-order pickup day, new-order delivery day, single-order reschedule day) already gray out/disable days outside a template's `schedule_days`. Fixed by adding `schedule_days` to the `_dsTemplates` fetch and intersecting the `schedule_days` of every route template represented in the current selection, disabling any day not valid for all of them (falls back to excluding just Sunday if no template resolves). Confirmed via live query that every real `route_templates` row (Alameda/Berkeley/Concord/Oakland AM+PM, Hayward AM+PM, San Francisco, COMMERCIAL) is `[0,1,2,3,4,5]` (Mon–Sat) and Kidango is `[0,3,4]` — so Sunday is correctly excluded everywhere; verified with a standalone date-math check that Sun Aug 2 and Sun Aug 9 both disable correctly across the 14-day window. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `25638c6` needs the same `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac. **New `352db60` — driver-app Map view ("always understand where they are on their route"):** new 5th bottom-nav tab (Home / Route / **Map** / Messages / Profile) opens a Leaflet map (same library + tile source already used in admin-dashboard's route map) plotting every stop on the driver's active route as a numbered, color-coded pin (blue=pickup, green=delivery, gray=done, amber=skipped/failed, dark blue=en route) plus a pulsing “you are here” dot. Verified the load-bearing assumption directly against `pg_proc` before building on it: `get_driver_stop_addresses` returns `a.lat, a.lng` from the `addresses` table (not `latitude`/`longitude` or a nested shape), and confirmed all 5,228 rows in `addresses` have coordinates populated — no stop should ever be missing a pin. Reuses the always-current `_driverLat`/`_driverLng` globals (already updated on every GPS fix by the existing `startGpsTracking()`, independent of its throttled DB-write interval) — no new geolocation watch added. Tapping a pin opens that stop's detail via the existing `openStop()`, matching the Route list's tap behavior. A `setInterval` paced to the existing `GPS_SEND_INTERVAL` moves just the “you are here” marker while the Map tab is open, without rebuilding every pin each tick; pins themselves (new stops, status changes) refresh on tab entry or the header's refresh button. **Known gap, intentionally left open:** `renderRoute()`'s ~15 realtime call sites were NOT all wired to also call `renderRouteMap()`, so a stop reassigned/added by realtime while a driver is already on the Map tab won't appear until they refresh or re-enter the tab — scoped this way to keep the change low-risk; can be revisited if it turns out to matter in practice. `node --check` clean on both driver-app script blocks; `git diff --stat` confirmed only the 164 intended lines changed (no CRLF corruption — driver-app/index.html is CRLF and was edited with `newline=""` per the standing convention for this file). Bumped `BUILD_TS` to `20260727_s102` so driver iPads pick up the new tab on next load. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `352db60` needs `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac — this is new functionality with no live-but-broken prior version to test against, so David should open the Map tab on a real route after deploying and confirm pins line up with real stops and the “you are here” dot tracks correctly. **New `4bfafff` — Driver View now shows a live map too:** David couldn't assess the new driver-app Map tab without a driver's device, since admin's Driver View (session 189's read-only mirror) only showed a text list. Added a Leaflet map (`#dv-map`) above that list, rendered by a new `renderDriverViewMap()` called from the SAME `loadDriverViewData()` that builds the list — so the two are guaranteed to show the same “visible” stop set the driver's own app would compute for that driver/date, not an approximation of it. Reuses driver-app's exact pin-color convention (`_dvPinColor` mirrors `_dmapPinColor`) and adds a live “you are here” dot from `driver_locations`, shown only when previewing today's date and only if the last GPS ping is under 30 minutes old (a “current” position plotted against a past/future date's route would be meaningless). Added `lat, lng` to both address lookups in `loadDriverViewData()` (exact address + customer-default fallback) since the text-only list had never needed them. **Caught before shipping:** the panel's 3 early-return paths (no driver picked, query error, zero stops for the date) would have left a PREVIOUS driver's pins on screen under a “No routes or stops” message — added `_dvClearMap()`, called on all three, so the map can never show a stale route. `node --check` clean; confirmed no naming collisions before adding `_dvMap`/`_dvPinColor`/`renderDriverViewMap`; `driver_locations` access already exercised elsewhere in this file (live drivers map, driver messages online-status) so no new RLS surface. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commit `4bfafff` needs `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac — not yet tested against a real in-progress route from here; once deployed, open Drivers > Driver View for a driver actively out on a route and confirm the pins match their driver-app Map tab and the live dot tracks their real position. **New `a6f5777` — Driver View: embed the REAL driver app (read-only) instead of an admin-side recreation:** After shipping 4bfafff's admin-side map+list mirror, David asked to go further — click through the actual driver-app UI (all 5 tabs: Home, Route, Map, Messages, Profile) from admin, since that's what actually surfaces driver-reported UI bugs (a parallel admin reconstruction can drift from what drivers really see, as already happened once this session with pin-color/ghost-stop duplication). Presented the trade-off explicitly (recreate the 5 tabs admin-side vs. embed the real app) via AskUserQuestion — David chose embedding the real app. admin-dashboard's Driver View tab now points a phone-frame `<iframe>` at `https://driver.familylaundry.com/?preview=1&...`; **three independent safety layers, so no single missed check can leak data or take a real action:** (1) server-side — `get_driver_route_stops`, `get_driver_override_stops`, `get_driver_stop_addresses` (all SECURITY DEFINER) gained an optional `p_admin_preview_driver_id uuid DEFAULT NULL`; identity resolution becomes `CASE WHEN p_admin_preview_driver_id IS NOT NULL AND is_admin() THEN p_admin_preview_driver_id ELSE current_driver_id() END` — a non-admin passing the param is silently ignored (falls back to their own id), so no escalation path exists; shipped as 3 migrations (`session_190_admin_preview_driver_override`, `session_190_drop_old_rpc_overloads`, `session_190_fix_rpc_grants` — the last two needed because `CREATE OR REPLACE` with a new parameter list creates a second overload rather than replacing the old one, and because recreating the function re-grants EXECUTE to PUBLIC/anon by Postgres default; both caught via direct `pg_proc`/`has_function_privilege` queries and fixed before considering the migration done, per this project's SECURITY DEFINER checklist) — **all 3 already applied and verified live in production.** (2) client-side, the real boundary — driver-app's Supabase client now takes `global: { fetch: _wrGuardedFetch } }` (the official supabase-js construction option, since a bare `window.fetch` reassignment risked being a no-op if supabase-js captured its own fetch reference at construction time); `_wrGuardedFetch` blocks every non-GET call to `/rest/v1/` except a POST to one of the 3 whitelisted read-only RPC names above, and blocks every non-GET call to `/auth/v1/` except `/auth/v1/token` (needed once, for the session handoff) — blocked calls get a synthetic 403 and never touch the network. (3) UX guard (not the boundary, just cleaner feedback) — `doSignOut`, `sendOnMyWay`, `arrivedAtStop`, `handlePhoto`, `completeStop`, `cantCompleteStop`, `sendMessageInThread` all early-return via a new `_previewBlock()` toast. **Identity handoff:** admin-dashboard reads its OWN session (`db.auth.getSession()`) and `postMessage`s `{accessToken, refreshToken, driverId, date}` into the iframe (targeted at `https://driver.familylaundry.com` only); driver-app calls `db.auth.setSession(...)` — this is a genuinely valid session AS ADMIN, never as the driver, no driver password ever known or used; verified via direct `pg_policies` queries that admin's existing `is_admin()`-gated RLS on `drivers`/`profiles`/`routes`/`driver_locations`/`driver_messages`/`route_templates` already covers every read the preview needs, zero RLS changes required. driver-app does a local-only sign-out (`scope: 'local'`, no network call) at preview boot so no stale session bleeds in. Non-today dates skip the real-time route-visibility window (meaningless for a historical/future preview) and show that date's routes directly. The live “you are here” dot is a single freshness-gated read (<30 min) of the driver's last `driver_locations` row, only for a TODAY preview — `startGpsTracking()` (which would mislabel admin's own device location as the driver's) is never called in preview mode. **`vercel.json`:** the pre-existing global `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` would have silently blocked the iframe from rendering at all (different subdomains); added a `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'self' https://admin.familylaundry.com` header scoped only to `driver.familylaundry.com` (modern browsers prefer CSP over the legacy header, which is left untouched for every other host). Removed the now-unnecessary `loadDriverViewData` list/map rendering, `_dvMap`/`_dvPinColor`/`_dvClearMap`/`renderDriverViewMap`/`dvHiddenReason`/`DV_NOT_READY` from 4bfafff/session 189 — the iframe makes that duplication obsolete. `node --check` clean on both files; `git diff --stat` confirmed exact expected line counts (driver-app +207/-16, admin-dashboard +66/-249, vercel.json +7/-0) with no CRLF corruption. ⚠️ **This is the biggest, highest-risk change shipped this session** — it embeds the real driver app cross-origin, hands a real admin auth token across origins, and relies on a network-level write-block that could not be end-to-end tested from this sandboxed dev environment (no real browser, no real cross-origin postMessage round-trip). **DEPLOY + TEST CAREFULLY:** commit `a6f5777` needs `git push origin laptop-untracked-2026-07-24:main` from David's Mac — the 3 Supabase migrations are already live regardless of push, but none of this UI exists in production until pushed and redeployed. After deploying, David should pick a real driver/today's date in Driver View and confirm: the phone-frame iframe actually renders (a blank/broken frame means the CSP fix isn't taking effect); all 5 tabs (Home, Route, Map, Messages, Profile) show sensible real data; the amber “🔒 ADMIN PREVIEW — READ ONLY” banner is visible at the top; and — most importantly — tapping Sign Out, Complete a stop, Skip/Can't Complete, and sending a driver message all show the “Preview mode is read-only” toast and do NOT change any real data (check the actual order/route afterward to be sure). Also worth trying a past date to confirm it shows that full day's stops rather than an empty view. **New `8d4aba8` — Bag Check is now persisted to the DB and visible in admin (David caught this session's Driver View work surfaced a checklist he thought was disabled):** While clicking through the new real-driver-app preview, David noticed the pre-departure “Bag Check” checklist (session 31) was live but had ALWAYS been localStorage-only — a driver's ✓ On Rack / Missing taps never left that one phone, so admin had zero visibility into a missing bag. He asked for it to become actionable: a “bags not on rack” alert plus a green-check/red-cross indicator per stop on the map view (he screenshotted a route-list layout that turned out to be, structurally, admin's own existing Orders → Map / rcc-map-pane view — confirmed by matching route header / numbered stop rows / P-D badges / bag counts / time columns exactly). **DB (migration `session_191_bag_check_status_column`, already live):** added `route_stops.bag_check_status` (`'ok' | 'missing' | NULL`, CHECK-constrained) and `route_stops.bag_checked_at`. Blast-radius check (per the migration-review checklist) found two functions that repoint an EXISTING route_stops row to a different `route_id` without touching the new columns: `reconcile_order_stops` (admin-reschedule keeper-repoint path) and `reconcile_stop_route_on_run_change` (trigger firing when an order's pickup/delivery run_id changes) — both now reset `bag_check_status`/`bag_checked_at` to NULL whenever a stop's `route_id` changes, since a check made against one physical route's rack is meaningless once the stop moves to a different route and nothing would otherwise have cleared the stale flag. `get_driver_route_stops`/`get_driver_override_stops` needed NO changes — both serialize the whole row via `to_jsonb(rs)`, so the new columns flow to driver-app automatically. Verified via a live `curl` round-trip against `/rest/v1/route_stops` that PostgREST's schema cache actually picked up the new columns before writing any app code against them (the session 176/177 stale-cache trap only bites when column + dependent code ship in the same push, so this checks it explicitly instead of trusting `NOTIFY`). **driver-app (`BUILD_TS` → `s104`):** `rackCheckToggle` is now async — optimistically flips the tapped stop's status/timestamp in memory and re-renders immediately (same snappy feel), then writes through via a plain `db.from('route_stops').update()` (the existing `driver_update_assigned_stops` RLS policy already covers this, no RLS change needed); rolls back and toasts on write failure so the UI can never show a check that didn't actually save. Added the standard `_previewBlock()` guard since this is now a real write — without it, a tap from inside admin's read-only Driver View preview (previous commit) would attempt a genuine DB change (the network-level fetch guard from that same commit would have silently blocked it either way; this just makes the failure an intentional, friendly toast instead of a silent no-op). Removed the old `_rackCheckState`/`_rackCheckKey`/`_rackCheckLoad`/`_rackCheckSave` localStorage plumbing entirely — state now lives on the stop object, sourced from the server every load, consistent across devices instead of trapped on one phone. **admin-dashboard:** new “Bags Not On Rack” card on the Overview page (between Requires Rescheduling and Unpaid Orders), mirroring those two panels exactly — collapsible, red count badge, “View →” table rows, green-checkmark empty state; lists every pending/en_route delivery stop flagged `'missing'`; wired into `loadOverview()` and into the existing `admin-route-stops-live` realtime subscription so it updates the instant a driver taps Missing, no polling. Orders → Map now shows a small ✓ On rack / ✗ Bag missing badge per delivery stop row alongside the existing moved/en-route/photo-skipped/not-ready tags — added `bag_check_status`/`bag_checked_at` to BOTH places that fetch route_stops for this view (the initial per-route load AND `rccRefreshRoute`, the realtime-triggered refetch) since missing the second would have shown the badge on first load but silently dropped it on every live update after. **Caught before shipping:** the new Overview query's `routes(route_templates(name))` embed was ambiguous — `route_stops` has two FKs to `routes` (`route_id` AND `moved_from_route_id`) — and would have failed at runtime with a `PGRST201` error despite passing a syntax check; found by test-querying the exact shape against the live REST endpoint before wiring it in, fixed via `routes!route_stops_route_id_fkey(...)`. **Not built, deliberately scoped out:** no push/SMS alert when a bag goes missing, and no explicit “resolve” action other than the driver un-flagging it or the stop completing — both additions here are visibility surfaces, not new automation; worth a follow-up if David wants to be proactively pinged rather than checking the page. **New `15a3eb7` — fixed admin Driver View showing “No Stops Today” until manually tapping Refresh:** David reported the preview loaded fine (banner, tabs, greeting) but always showed empty Home/Route until he tapped the Route tab's own Refresh button, after which the real route appeared. Screenshots showed two tells at once: the greeting read “Good afternoon, Driver.” (the generic fallback name, meaning `currentProfile.first_name` was missing) alongside zero stops for a driver who genuinely had a route that evening. Root cause: the very first data read immediately after `db.auth.setSession()` resolves into `loadDriverData()` can come back incomplete — tapping Refresh calls the exact same function a second time and it works immediately, with no data having changed in between, pointing at a one-time settling race on the freshly-handed-off session rather than an RLS/RPC design problem (those are unchanged from the previous commit and work correctly on every later call). Fix: the preview branch now automatically re-fires `loadDriverData()` 900ms after the first load, silently in the background, and re-renders Home + Route — deliberately “do what Refresh does, automatically” rather than a guess at the exact internal cause, since it self-heals regardless of which layer the race is actually in (this sandboxed dev environment has no real browser to reproduce/inspect it directly), and is a harmless no-op if the first load already succeeded. `BUILD_TS` → `s105`. **New `28c54a3` — fixed completed DELIVERY stops vanishing from driver-app while completed PICKUP stops stayed visible:** David used the new admin Driver View preview and noticed that completed pickups still show as “Done” on the app, but completed deliveries disappear entirely. Root cause: `_loadDriverDataInner()` had three near-identical filters (the override-stops list, the main route-stops list, and the upcoming-routes list) that all excluded any stop whose parent order was `'delivered'`/`'cancelled'`, on the assumption that a delivered/cancelled order has nothing left to do. But completing a DELIVERY stop is usually exactly what flips the parent order to `'delivered'` a moment later — so that stop would vanish from the driver's own list on the very next render/refresh, right after the driver just finished it. Completing a PICKUP stop, by contrast, leaves the order in an intermediate status (`picked_up`/`processing`/`folding`/etc.) for the rest of the day, so it stayed visible for hours — same filter, wildly different practical effect depending on stop type. Fix: all three filters now exempt any stop that is itself already `status === 'complete'` from both the delivered/cancelled-order check and the ghost-delivery check (the latter is only meaningful for not-yet-done stops) — a stop the driver has already finished always stays visible as “Done” going forward, regardless of what happens to the order afterward. The skipped/failed exclusion is untouched, since admin already handled those. Confirmed via blast-radius grep that admin-dashboard's Orders → Map view uses a deliberately different “hide completed stops” design (a live “what's left to do” planning surface, not a completed-stops log) and does not need the same fix. `BUILD_TS` → `s106`. **New `8fe147a` — Orders → Map PM/AM window summary no longer counts skipped stops:** David noticed the “PM window” header (Pickups/Deliveries counts above the route list) showed 46 pickups, while the 5 visible route cards only summed to 36 — the cards intentionally show remaining work only. Traced with live data: the gap was two groups the header counted but nothing on screen surfaced. First, 5 already-completed pickups, all on a route called “COMMERCIAL” that runs 9am–3pm — not one of the 5 routes shown, but its window_end (3pm) is after noon so the existing AM/PM slot logic (`window_end hour > 12`) correctly classifies it as PM by design; confirmed with David this classification should stay as-is. Second, 5 pickups on orders marked `'skipped'` (1 on Berkeley, 2 on Hayward, 2 on Oakland) that were invisible everywhere: the route cards already hide skipped stops (by design), and the “View completed” panel only lists `status='complete'`, never `'skipped'` — so there was no view anywhere that showed what got skipped. David's call: skipped stops shouldn't count toward the window total at all, only active work should. Fix: `rccRenderDaySummary()` now also excludes any stop with `status` `'skipped'` or `'failed'`, on top of the pre-existing cancelled-order exclusion — completed stops still count (real work today), only skipped/failed stops are dropped from the tally. Blast-radius grep for the same “only truly cancelled” exclusion pattern came back clean — this was the only spot. **Not built, deliberately scoped out:** no dedicated “skipped stops” view was added (David explicitly declined this in favor of just excluding them from the count); worth a follow-up if he later wants visibility into what got skipped rather than just a cleaner total.*

*Previously: July 22, 2026 — Session 185 — **Orders → Map (Route Command Center): 3 operator-requested improvements** (commit `12c6b78`, `admin-dashboard/index.html`). Feedback on the session-184k RCC rebuild. **(1) Select-then-assign move to route** (David's pick over drag): the bulk-select batch bar (`#rcc-batch-bar`, the same select mode used for SMS/reschedule) gains a **"Move to route"** button → `rccOpenMoveModal`/`rccConfirmMove`. Pick a destination route (same day, current AM/PM slot) and all selected stops are reassigned via the **`reschedule_order_leg` RPC** per stop (same trusted path as drag-drop `rccDrop` + `confirmMoveStop`: repoints route_stops, clears driver override, appends stop_number, recounts `routes.total_stops`, `autoOptimizeRoute` on every affected route). **Defaults to NO customer SMS** — this is a dispatch/driver reassignment, not a promised-time change; an opt-in checkbox ("Text customers if their time changes") calls `_maybeNotifyReschedule` only when checked (which itself only sends if the window start actually moved). This avoids the bulk-move mass-SMS footgun. Works for mixed pickup/delivery selections (each stop moves its own leg). **(2) Completed-history slide-in panel** (Luis's idea): clicking the **Pickups/Deliveries shift summary** (`.rcc-summary`, now `.rcc-summary-clickable` with a "View completed →" affordance) opens `#rcc-completed-panel` → `rccOpenCompletedPanel`. Lists every `route_stops.status='complete'` stop for the selected date + AM/PM window across all slot routes: P/D badge, customer (click → panel), address, order #, **time completed** (`completed_at`), and **driver** (stop `driver_id`, falling back to the route's driver — verified ~15% of completed stops lack a stop-level driver so the fallback matters). Close via × or Escape. Read-only; no mutations. **(3) Removed the top route-name chip strip** (redundant with the right-hand accordion). `rccRenderChips` now renders ONLY the AM/PM toggle into the Date bar (the chip-building + `#rcc-chips` container are gone; dead `.rcc-chip*` CSS left harmless per 184k precedent). All slot routes still auto-open (`rccAutoOpenSlotRoutes`), so the accordion shows them all by default. Since the chips were the only way to re-add a route, the route-header **× was replaced by a reversible eye toggle** (`rccToggleRoutePins` + `_rccHiddenPins` Set, cleared on date/slot change) that hides/shows just that route's pins on the map while keeping it in the list — `rccRenderMap` skips hidden routes for both pins and legend. **QA:** inline script `node --check` clean; the completed-panel query validated against live data (2026-07-21: 87 deliveries + 45 pickups, all with `completed_at`/address); all 6 new handlers defined+wired; no payment-status/RLS surface touched; blast radius contained (the only route-deactivate paths — chips + × — were both removed/repurposed; realtime refresh uses `_rccActiveRoutes` which still populates via auto-open). ⚠️ **NOT visually tested in-app from here** — verify on Orders → Map: (a) select stops on one/several routes → Move to route → lands on target + both routes re-render, (b) click the P/D summary → completed panel content + driver names, (c) eye toggle hides/shows pins, (d) chip row is gone but AM/PM toggle + Optimize/Maximize still work. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** commits `12c6b78` + `d39f325` — `git push` from David's Mac. **Post-deploy fix `d39f325`:** the completed-history panel first errored "Could not embed because more than one relationship was found for 'route_stops' and 'routes'" — `route_stops` has TWO FKs to `routes` (`route_id` + `moved_from_route_id`), so the `routes(driver_id)` embed was ambiguous. Dropped the embed; driver resolves from stop `driver_id` → `_dsRoutes` cache fallback. **Lesson:** never embed `routes` on a `route_stops` PostgREST query without a disambiguating FK hint (`routes!route_stops_route_id_fkey(...)`); prefer resolving the route from the already-loaded cache. **Follow-up `6de1028`:** disabled drag-to-move on Orders → Map now that "Move to route" exists — RCC rows are no longer `draggable`, the `.rcc-body`/`.rcc-col` drop-zone handlers + drop hint are removed; the four `rccDrag*`/`rccDrop` functions remain as unreferenced dead code (harmless). Processing-page kanban drag (`dropToIntake`/`Cleaning`/`Folding`/`Rack`) is a separate feature and untouched. **Follow-up `24c8cd2` — global "Select stops" toggle:** the per-route ☐ square was undiscoverable; replaced with one labeled "Select stops" button in the panel toolbar (`rccToggleGlobalSelect`) that enables selection across ALL shown routes at once (expands them so rows are tickable), reads "Done selecting" + accent-filled when active. Per-route select state (`_rccSelMode` Set) collapsed to a global `_rccGlobalSelect` boolean; `rccToggleStopSelection`/`rccBuildCard`/`rccRenderColumns` now gate on it; cleared on date/slot change + `rccClearSelection`. The batch bar (Move to route / Send SMS / Reschedule) is unchanged and reads the same `_rccSelStops` set. **Follow-up `cf8eb98` — reorder routes ▲▼:** each route header gains up/down chevron buttons to move the route within the accordion (for putting two routes adjacent to compare). New `_rccRouteOrder` array holds display order; `rccRenderColumns` iterates it. Order resets on date/slot change, appended on route activate, filtered on deactivate. Map pins/legend still iterate `_rccActiveRoutes` (pin order irrelevant). **Superseded by `4b73b17` — drag-to-reorder:** per David's preference the ▲▼ arrows were replaced with drag: the `.rcc-col-hdr` is `draggable` (grab cursor), `.rcc-col` is the drop target (`rccRouteDragStart/Over/Leave/Drop/End` + `_rccRouteDragId`), dropping above/below the target's midpoint splices `_rccRouteOrder`; accent inset-top indicator shows the drop line; click-to-expand still works (drag suppresses click). `rccMoveRoute` removed. Purely visual, no data change. NOTE: this is the ONLY drag interaction on the Map — stop-drag remains disabled; the two don't conflict (`rccRouteDragOver` early-returns unless a route drag is in progress). **Follow-up `b53758a`:** (a) each route header shows its template **time window** next to the name (`winLabel` from `tpl.window_start/window_end` → "6–10 PM" / "9 AM–3 PM", muted `.rcc-d-win`); (b) **"Select stops" no longer auto-expands all routes** — `rccToggleGlobalSelect` keeps expansion state as-is so the admin opens routes case-by-case and ticks stops only in expanded ones; toolbar shows an "Open a route to pick its stops" hint while select mode is active. **Follow-up `280df01` — column alignment:** stop rows were misaligned because `.rcc-row-meta` was shrink-wrapped + right-floating (P/D shifted per row by badge count). Pinned meta to a fixed 200px with fixed sub-columns — `.rcc-row-type` 20px (P/D), `.rcc-row-bags` 44px, `.rcc-row-tags` flex/truncate (moved/en-route/photo/not-ready), `.rcc-row-time` 52px right (tabular-nums); `.rcc-row-name` fixed 104px so addresses align too. Badges lost their leading `rcc-card-dot` separators (tags column spaces them); ETA moved out of the badge cluster into the time column. Row cursor grab→pointer. **Follow-up `7a9dfdd` — route-loading fixes (2 regressions from removing the chip strip):** (1) routes loaded one-by-one with lag — `rccAutoOpenSlotRoutes` awaited each `rccToggleRoute` sequentially; now `Promise.all` fetches them in parallel (order preserved because the synchronous optimistic-add runs in `toOpen` order before any fetch resolves). (2) panel stayed EMPTY after an AM↔PM toggle — `rccSetSlot` cleared routes but never re-opened them (the chips used to); now it calls `rccAutoOpenSlotRoutes()`. Rapid-toggle races handled by `rccToggleRoute`'s existing post-fetch guard (`if (!_rccActiveRoutes.has(routeId)) return`). **Follow-up `a53c1bb` — report speedups (Delivery KPIs, Daily Revenue, Launderers):** root cause was uncached slow EXTERNAL API calls per load + unindexed date filters. (1) **Client caches** (session-lifetime `Map`, successful results only): `_squareLaborCache` wraps `fetchSquareLaborHours` (Square timesheet API — Delivery KPIs calls it 2× for current+previous, Launderers 1×) and `_stripeFeesCache` wraps the Revenue `get-stripe-fees` fetch (its own comment notes ~50s for long ranges, 12s abort). Re-loads / tab-switches on the same window are now near-instant; the shared current window is reused across KPIs↔Launderers. First load of a new window still hits the API (unavoidable). (2) **Indexes** (migration `session_185_report_date_range_indexes`): `orders(actual_delivery_at, billed_at, racked_at, delivery_window_start)`, all range-filtered but previously unindexed (full seq scans of ~9.7k orders). Verified via EXPLAIN: Revenue racked_at query 868→440 buffers (5.8→2.1ms, Bitmap Index Scan); Launderers delivery_window_start now Index Scan (2.7ms). `orders.created_at` was already indexed. The `delivery_kpis` RPC (~119ms, unchanged) was never the perceived bottleneck (Square was); its ~52k-buffer cost is `customers`/`routes` scans inside `_dk_window`/`avg_ltv`, left for a future pass if needed. ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** indexes are LIVE; client cache in commit `a53c1bb` needs `git push`. **Follow-up `6d59d52` — caching extended to ALL reports:** the Square cache already covered every `fetchSquareLaborHours` caller (Delivery KPIs, Launderers, Drivers) since it's inside the function; the only remaining uncached external report call was **POS Sales** `get-stripe-fees` — now wrapped with the same `_stripeFeesCache` keyed by the same `{from,to}` window as Daily Revenue, so the two reports share ONE Stripe call per window. Every report's slow external call is now cached. The other `functions/v1/*` calls in the reports area are user-triggered send-email/send-sms actions, not load-path bottlenecks. Reports with no external call (Items, On Account, Registrations, Subscriptions) are DB-only and covered by the existing/new `orders` indexes. **Follow-up `addc332` — persist caches to localStorage.** Measured actual latency via edge-function logs: **`get-stripe-fees` ~2.5s**, **`square-labor` 0.6–1.9s** per call. The in-memory Map caches were wiped on every page reload, so first load of a window still paid it. Added a localStorage layer (`_reportCacheGet/Set`, `_reportTtl`, `_stripeFeesLoad/Store`) keyed by window: closed windows (`end < today` Pacific) hold long (Stripe 30d — settled balance-txns are immutable; Square 24h — timesheets editable), live windows short (1h/6h). Survives reloads; shared per-window across reports. **Still open (offered, NOT built): the true first-ever-load and cross-user/cross-browser fix is a SERVER-SIDE mirror — incrementally sync Stripe balance-transactions (and Square hours) into a DB table so `get-stripe-fees`/`square-labor` answer any window from Postgres instead of the external API. Bigger change (edge-function rewrite + new table + one-time backfill sync + touches money-reporting) — waiting on David's go-ahead.** ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** `addc332` needs `git push`. **Follow-up `b57b9c0` — Stripe fee mirror BUILT & LIVE (DB side).** New tables `stripe_fee_cache` (pi PK, fee/net/charge cents, bt_created) + `stripe_fee_sync` (singleton cursor row) — migration `session_185_stripe_fee_mirror_tables`, RLS on, `service_role` only, anon/authenticated REVOKED (financial data, Data-API-invisible). **`get-stripe-fees` v11 (LIVE):** on each call does an incremental forward sync (new balance-txns since cursor — tiny once caught up) + one bounded backward backfill chunk (`BACKWARD_PAGES=15` ≈ 1500 txns), upserts into the mirror, then serves the requested window from a fast Postgres read. Settled balance transactions are immutable → once mirrored, a window is instant + permanent + shared across all users/browsers. **SAFETY: entire mirror path is wrapped in try/catch → on ANY error falls back to the original live-Stripe pagination (`source:'stripe-fallback'`), so reports can NEVER break.** Same request/response contract (client unchanged; extra `source`/`count` fields ignored; the report still estimates any PI not yet mirrored, so it's accurate throughout backfill). **The mirror is EMPTY now — it self-populates over the first several Daily Revenue / POS Sales loads (one backfill chunk each), converging to <1s steady state.** Could not exercise a full admin-auth run from the session; verify by opening Daily Revenue 3–4×, then check `SELECT count(*) FROM stripe_fee_cache` climbs + edge-fn logs show `source:mirror`. **Square hours mirror NOT built** (timesheets are editable → needs per-shift updated-since sync; lower latency 0.6–1.9s; left on the localStorage cache). ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** tables + edge fn are LIVE; repo copies in `b57b9c0` need `git push` (source-of-truth only — no runtime effect).*

*Previously: July 22, 2026 — Session 185 — **Launderers report: Issues column + required order-link on issues** (commit `a4924e9`, `admin-dashboard/index.html` + migration `session_185_enforce_issue_order_link`). Added a quality dimension next to efficiency on the Launderers report. **Design premise (verified against live data):** the chain issue→order→launderer already exists — every order records its folder (`orders.folded_by_id`, 6811 orders, 0 orphans) — so attributing an issue to a launderer is really just linking the issue to an order; the launderer is derived automatically. The real gap was the linking habit: only **1 of 158** `cs_issues` had an `order_id`, so the trend starts fresh from now. **(1) Both admin issue forms got an order picker** — the Create Issue modal (`submitCreateIssue`, new `ciLoadOrders`/`ciOnOrderChange`) and the inbox quick-add (`saveIssue`, new `ifLoadOrders`/`ifOnOrderChange`, populated from `currentConv.customer_id`). Picking an order shows "Folded by <name>" so the attribution is visible before saving. The **modal requires an order for every category** (David's "all types"); the **inbox requires it only for `ORDER_REQUIRED_CATS = pickup/delivery/damaged`** so an issue can still be logged for an unmatched SMS number. **Lost & Found create is untouched** (already order-optional; found items may have no known owner). Both insert `order_id`. **(2) Report — new "Issues" column** (`loadLaundererReport` + `sortLaundererReport` + `exportLaundererCSV`): raw count + prior-period **trend arrow** (▲ red = more/worse, ▼ green = fewer) + **hover breakdown by category** (title attr). Attributed via `cs_issues.order_id → orders.folded_by_id` (**primary folder only**; split orders — rare, 176+43 vs 3872 — are NOT split for issues); counted by `created_at` over the report's date range; prior window = same length immediately before (`addDaysPacific`/`pacificDayStart`/`pacificDayEnd`). Rows now carry `id`; unassigned + total rows include issues; column is sortable and in the CSV. Uses PostgREST embed `cs_issues.select('category, orders!inner(folded_by_id)')` (FK `cs_issues.order_id→orders.id` confirmed). **(3) DB backstop — migration `session_185_enforce_issue_order_link` (LIVE, migration-reviewed + tested):** `trg_enforce_issue_order_link` BEFORE INSERT on `cs_issues` raises when `order_id IS NULL AND lower(category) IN (pickup,delivery,damaged) AND created_by NOT IN (migration,system)`. `lost_found` + general CS categories (billing/schedule/complaint/other) stay order-optional → doesn't break the inbox unmatched-number flow, the L&F found-item flow, or the stripe-webhook grace-expiry `Billing`/`system` insert. **BEFORE INSERT only** — the 157 existing order-less rows are untouched (a CHECK constraint would have failed on them). Added indexes `idx_cs_issues_created_at` + `idx_cs_issues_order_id`. Live-tested: pickup-no-order BLOCKED with a friendly message; damaged+order / billing-no-order / system-Billing all ALLOWED; test rows cleaned up. **QA:** inline script `node --check` clean; blast radius — all 3 `cs_issues` insert paths accounted for; customer app inserts none; no DB fn/other trigger inserts `cs_issues`; `currentConv` confirmed a real global (shape `{key,customer_id,phone,channel,conv_id,status}`). ⚠️ **DEPLOY SEQUENCING:** the **DB trigger is LIVE but the matching frontend is committed-not-pushed** — until `git push` from David's Mac deploys the new admin UI, creating a **pickup/delivery/damaged** issue via the CURRENT live dashboard (no order picker) will be REJECTED by the trigger ("Issues of type … must be linked to an order"). note/other/billing/complaint/schedule + Lost & Found still work in the gap. **Push soon to close the gap.** **Fine-tune-later (per David):** modal requires an order for ALL types, so a customer with zero orders can't be issued via the modal (blocked at the dropdown); no dedicated `stain` category yet (stains file under `damaged`); "found" items counted like any issue. **Reversibility:** rollback SQL is in the migration file; `git revert a4924e9`.*

*Previously: July 16, 2026 — Session 184h — **Fixed false-active customers: "Active within N days" now measures COMPLETED (delivered) orders, not order creation.** David flagged that customers who skip every recurring order (e.g. Annalee Mintz — 16 orders, ALL skipped, 0 delivered, $0 billed) still showed the green "• 7 days" activity badge. **Root cause:** the `trg_update_customer_last_order_at` AFTER-INSERT trigger stamps `customers.last_order_at` on EVERY order created, regardless of status. The recurring engine auto-creates the next weekly order (and a skip immediately spawns the following occurrence), so `last_order_at` stayed fresh for anyone on a live recurring chain whether or not they ever completed one — and the "Active (7/30/90)" cards + activity badge/pill read only that field. Meanwhile `risk_status` reads delivered orders only, so the same card showed "churned" AND "active" — a visible contradiction. Blast radius: **24 customers** counted as Active-7d and **48** as Active-30d with zero lifetime deliveries. **Fix — migration `session_184b_last_delivered_order_at` (LIVE):** new nullable `customers.last_delivered_order_at`, maintained by `trg_update_customer_last_delivered_at` (AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OF status on orders; stamps `GREATEST(existing, COALESCE(actual_delivery_at, now()))` only on the transition INTO 'delivered', so unrelated updates don't re-stamp and it never moves backward). SECURITY DEFINER + `search_path=public`, REVOKE public/anon (trigger fn, not client-callable). Backfilled from history → **1216 customers** got a real delivery timestamp; NULL = never completed an order (Annalee correctly NULL). **Admin UI (commit `036711d`, `admin-dashboard/index.html`):** repointed to `last_delivered_order_at` — the Active 7/30/90 stat cards (`activeIn`), the 7d/30d/90d filter, `custActivityColor`/`custActivityBadge` (map dot + "N days" pill), the customer-list "Last Order" column + its sort/status sort, and the panel header "Last Order" stat; added the column to both customer SELECTs; and changed the `loadPanelOrders` delivered-date self-heal to write `last_delivered_order_at` (was polluting `last_order_at`). `last_order_at` is untouched (still "last touch"; kept for the billing-group member badge). Consistent with session 184f which had already made the panel's Last Order delivered-only. **Migration-reviewed** (additive, no drops, reversible; the one flagged hazard — PostgREST stale-cache trap since the admin `.select()` names the new column — was handled by applying + verifying data-API visibility via a REST round-trip BEFORE the HTML change). **Verified the session-184 skip auto-stop actually fires** (David's Q1): transactional DO-block test with guaranteed `RAISE EXCEPTION` rollback — a 2nd consecutive skip logged exactly 1 `recurring_stopped` event and generated 0 new orders; 0 residue after rollback. Also confirmed the auto-stop's decision branch evaluates TRUE for the live customers who currently have an open scheduled recurring order preceded by a skip (they'll stop on their next skip). ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** migration is LIVE (Supabase); commit `036711d` is committed locally and needs `git push` from David's Mac to deploy the admin UI (until then the live dashboard runs old code — still functional, just still shows the false-actives). **Reversibility:** `DROP TRIGGER trg_update_customer_last_delivered_at ON orders; DROP FUNCTION update_customer_last_delivered_at(); ALTER TABLE customers DROP COLUMN last_delivered_order_at;` + `git revert 036711d`.*

Fixed dashboard-wide: **corner radius 15 distinct values → 4** (4 tiny / 8 controls / 12 cards / 20·999 pills) — commits `b1f3917` (6/7/9→8), `b56312c` (3/5→4), `abe84ba` (10/14/16→12); **success green 3 shades → 1** (`--green` token + inline `#16a34a`/`#059669` all → `#059669`) `49af4ab`; **borders** — 77 awkward `1.5px`→`1px` `53925ad` + 30 heavy `2px` neutral-gray dividers→`1px` `0916db2` (2px accent/tab-underline/`#000`-print kept); **all `text-transform:uppercase` removed** (~107 inline labels + 6 data-table header rules → sentence/title-case everywhere) `943247f`; **label letter-spacing zeroed** (positive tracking that accompanied the removed uppercase; negative title tracking `-.01/-.02em` preserved) `b56312c`; **`font-weight:800`→`700`** (26×) `dd83e98`; **duplicate dark text-on-tint shades unified** (green `#15803d`→`#166534`, red `#b91c1c`→`#991b1b`, amber `#78350f`/`#854d0e`→`#92400e`) `0916db2`. **Deliberately NOT changed:** `#f59e0b` amber (it's the Recall-to-Route action color AND a `DRV_COLORS` driver-palette value — not a duplicate status shade). **Color tokenization (hardcoded status hex → `var(--token)`) investigated and DECLINED as unsafe:** token-value hexes (`#3b82f6` ×30, `#dc2626`, `#0ba25a`, etc.) are reused as literal DATA in `DRV_COLORS` (driver map colors), the template color-swatch picker (`data-color=`), and the `STATUS_COLORS` JS map — where `var()` doesn't resolve (JS/canvas) and a blanket replace would break driver colors + the picker, for zero visual gain. *Previously: July 16, 2026 — Session 184f — **Recurring auto-stop after 2 consecutive skips + Last Order = completed only.** **(1) DB rule — recurring chain auto-stops after 2 CONSECUTIVE skips.** Migration `session_184_recurring_autostop_two_consecutive_skips` modified `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` (the AFTER-UPDATE trigger `create_recurring_order_on_delivered` that creates the next occurrence on skip/deliver/pickup_failed). New guard, placed right after the `recurring_interval` check: when `NEW.status='skipped'` AND the customer's **prior** recurring occurrence (most recent terminal recurring order by `pickup_window_start`) was ALSO `'skipped'`, it does NOT create the next occurrence (recurrence ends; history preserved) and logs a `recurring_stopped` order_event. **Excludes Subscription customers** (`customers.pricelist='Subscription'`) AND any order with a `subscription_id` — they're billed via Stripe, handled manually. Only `status='skipped'` counts (pickup_failed = driver failure, doesn't count). **Migration-reviewed** (no column drops, SECURITY DEFINER + search_path preserved, trigger fn not client-callable so no grant changes, order_events insert satisfies NOT NULLs) and **tested live with guaranteed rollback** (`RAISE EXCEPTION` pattern): 2-consecutive-skip → after_scheduled=before−1 + stop_logged=1; single-skip-after-delivered → next occurrence still created + stop_logged=0. **(2) One-time cleanup of the existing 100.** Preflight-checked (LOW risk, 0 SMS — no DB trigger sends SMS, that's edge-function/app only). Cleared `recurring_interval=NULL` on **90** pending scheduled recurring orders for the 100 non-subscription customers whose 2 most recent terminal recurring occurrences were both skipped (excludes `subscription_id` orders), + logged a `recurring_stopped` event each. Snapshot: `_archive._backfill_recurring_autostop_20260716` (id, customer_id, recurring_interval, status). Verified: 90 cleared / 0 missed / **0 outbound SMS** in the 10 min after. **(2b) Then hard-deleted those 90 pending scheduled orders** (David's follow-up: "remove the currently scheduled orders as well") so drivers don't see phantom pickup stops for inactive customers. Preflight: **no DELETE triggers** on `orders`/`route_stops` (no SMS/side effects), **0** linked `cs_issues` (NO ACTION FK didn't block), CASCADE removed 180 `route_stops` + order_events/items/folding/notifications; SET-NULL children (customer_transactions/discount_redemptions/print_jobs/subscription_usage_log) didn't apply to never-charged scheduled orders. Full snapshots first: `_archive._deleted_orders_recurring_autostop_20260716` (SELECT * of the 90 orders) + `_archive._deleted_route_stops_recurring_autostop_20260716` (180 stops). Verified post-delete: 0 orders / 0 stops / 0 events remain, **0 outbound SMS**. (Restoring deleted orders would require re-inserting from both snapshot tables — best-effort; unlikely needed for inactive customers.) **(3) "Last Order" = completed only** (commit `6558d5d`, `admin-dashboard/index.html`): the customer-profile Last Order stat now resolves the most recent **delivered** order (by `actual_delivery_at`) — skips/cancels don't count; the `loadPanelOrders` `last_order_at` sync is delivered-only too. **New order_events `event_type` `recurring_stopped`** (History renders it with the default bullet icon; fine). ⚠️ **DEPLOY:** DB migration is LIVE (Supabase); commit `6558d5d` needs `git push` from David's Mac. **Reversibility:** restore the recurrence via `UPDATE orders o SET recurring_interval=s.recurring_interval FROM _archive._backfill_recurring_autostop_20260716 s WHERE o.id=s.id;` and the trigger via its prior definition.*

New `.cp-subhead` class replaced the inline uppercase mini-labels (Billing sections + Special Care Notes + New Address / Enter Card Details). **Globals (`.btn`/`.filter-tab`/`.modal-close`/`.side-panel`) were intentionally NOT touched** so the rest of the dashboard is unchanged. **Tabs 7→5:** Contact · Preferences · Billing · Orders · Issues — Engagement (marketing toggles + message history) folded into **Contact**; Transactions folded into **Billing → History** read-only sub-tab. `cpTab`/`openCustomerPanel` tab arrays + `cpBillingSubtab` updated; deep-links (`contact`/`billing`) still resolve. **Contact sub-tabs** (`cpContactSubtab`): Details / Marketing / Messages so the identity form isn't buried; Messages history lazy-loads. **Header:** status pill + Issue/Order buttons pinned to `height:28px` so they align; Last Order stat restored to 18px (had a leftover inline 13px). **Transaction history (Billing→History):** day filter restyled to match Orders→Completed (`cp-range-btn`, "Days: 7 30 60 120 All"); **Description column replaced by Order #** (linked to the order, number parsed from `description`, `—` for non-order rows); card `•••• last4` moved to a subline under the Type badge; friendly badges added for `subscription_invoice` ("🔁 Subscription charge") and `credit_use` ("🎁 Credit used"). **Orders → Completed:** now the **default** section when opening the Orders tab; each completed row expands an **inline Starchup-style quick summary** (Details: status, pickup/delivery + **drivers**, route, source, **processed-by/folder**, weight | Items: line items, subtotal, tip, grand total, billing) **+ pickup/delivery proof photos side by side**; row-click expands the summary, the blue order # (or "Open full order →") opens the full page. Driver names resolve via `route_stops.driver_id → allDriversCache` with **route-driver fallback** (`pickup_driver_id`/`delivery_driver_id`/legacy `driver_id`) for stops that inherited the route default; `loadPanelOrders` now ensures `allDriversCache` is loaded (was Routes-page-only). The per-row 🧾 Receipt button (added earlier this session) was **removed as redundant** once batch existed. **Marketing toggles fixed:** the `:checked` visual was losing to the knob/track **inline styles** (inline always wins) so they were stuck OFF — added `!important`, so opted-in = ON/green, opted-out = OFF. All steps QA'd with `node --check` on the extracted script; contact-pane div-balance verified. Commits: `9f6b03d`, `bf8d857`, `e86aa73`, `09dd922`, `5e29bf3`, `f0554d6`, `b7b4b4e`, `49d9c19`, `2954f6b`, `d8f37e4`, `f358387`, `9ef1b22`, `8edbe62`, `5758e98`, `85f7cc5`. ⚠️ **DEPLOY: all committed locally, clean fast-forwards — David pushes from his Mac (Vercel auto-deploy).** **PENDING (approved direction):** roll the airier styling across the rest of the dashboard (sidebar, orders list, routes, reports) and finish the global **purple→blue cleanup** (~30 leftover `rgba(99,91,255,…)` refs in the admin CSS: nav-active, selected-row highlights, hover glows).*

*Previously: July 9, 2026 — Session 183 — **Subscription cancel was broken for past-due subs — fixed in 3 layers (Maria Markison case).** Maria's $275/mo renewal failed Jul 8 → sub went `past_due` + dunning. She couldn't cancel because the customer-app's `past_due` branch (renderPlanCards) showed ONLY "Update Payment Method" — the Cancel button was hidden entirely. David's admin Cancel click actually SUCCEEDED (Stripe + DB set `cancel_at_period_end=true`) but the admin Billing tab ignored that flag — still said "Renews on Aug 8" with a live Cancel button, so it looked like nothing happened. Worse, "cancel at period end" is the wrong semantic for past-due: Stripe keeps dunning the failed renewal all month for a period the customer is cancelling out of. **Fixes:** (1) **cancel-subscription v10** — past-due subs (checked on BOTH the local row and live Stripe status, since they can drift) now cancel IMMEDIATELY (`stripe.subscriptions.cancel`, no proration/invoice_now) + ALL open invoices on the sub are VOIDED (card retries stop); paid-up subs keep the period-end path; response gained `immediate: true/false`. The `customer.subscription.deleted` webhook does the full bookkeeping (final overage invoice, pricelist restore, plan clear) — the function's local update is just an optimistic mirror. (2) **resume-subscription v3 (QA catch)** — was blindly writing `status='active'` on reactivate; now mirrors Stripe's real status so reactivating a past-due sub keeps the dunning state visible. (3) **Admin UI** — Billing tab shows a CANCELS AT PERIOD END badge + "Cancels on <date>", swaps Cancel→Reactivate when scheduled, and shows BOTH Reactivate + "Cancel now" for the past-due+scheduled state (Maria's exact state); `adminCancelSubscription(id, isPastDue)` confirm copy matches immediate vs period-end. (4) **Customer app** — past_due layout gets its Cancel button back (copy: ends right away, no charge); `cancelSubscription()` dialog/toast branch on past_due; QA fix: spinner now targets `button.danger` (old first-button selector hit "Update Payment Method"/"Schedule weekly pickup"). Blast radius: Reports→Subscriptions "Cancel pending" signal already read the flag (clean); no DB functions/cron read `cancel_at_period_end`. Both edge functions DEPLOYED and live. Commit `bc5a2d3` (admin + customer + both function sources). ⚠️ **DEPLOY PENDING: `git push` can't auth from this environment — David pushes from his Mac to deploy the UI via Vercel.** The deployed v10 works with the OLD admin UI's Cancel button meanwhile. **Maria's sub at session end: `cancel_at_period_end=true`, still `past_due` — David to click Cancel (now = immediate + void) in admin, then verify `status='cancelled'` + invoice voided.***
