*Previously: July 8, 2026 — Session 182 — **POS (Foothill) receipts now include delivery date, pickup date, bag count, and route — the delivery/intake bag-tag receipts printed at our retail counter were missing all of it.** Team feature-request triage day; started with the receipts. **Scope clarified with David:** the 23rd Ave / admin "Delivery" receipts are fine — the problem is delivery orders PROCESSED at Foothill (the POS/retail spot) came out with missing info. Both POS sites share one receipt builder, so this was never two different receipts — just fields the shared builder never printed. **Fix (`pos/index.html`, commit `98ab91a`):** (1) `loadWalkInQueue` SELECT now pulls `pickup_window_start, delivery_window_start, pickup_run_id, delivery_run_id`; a single batched `routes(id,name)` query hydrates `_routeName` on each queue order (DELIVERY route preferred — where the bag is headed — pickup route fallback). (2) `buildPosReceiptMarkup` (thermal) + `buildPosReceiptHtml` (browser fallback) both render a delivery-info block (Pickup / Delivery / Bags / Route) **gated on `source !== 'walk_in'`** so retail sales stay clean; each line only prints if its value exists (partial data degrades gracefully). **Dates formatted in `America/Los_Angeles`** — verified against 4 live orders that the Pacific-formatted `pickup_window_start`/`delivery_window_start` exactly matches each order's authoritative route `run_date` (no off-by-one). **QA (washroute-qa):** node parse-check clean; blast radius — 4 print call sites checked (intake auto-print + queue reprint pass hydrated delivery orders → block shows; both retail auto-print paths pass `source:'walk_in'` → block hidden); `routes` RLS "Auth read routes" (`true` for authenticated) admits the POS device → no RLS gap on the new read. Display-only, no message fan-out, no bulk write. ⚠️ **DEPLOY PENDING: commit `98ab91a` is committed locally but `git push` could not authenticate to GitHub from this environment — David must push from his Mac to trigger the Vercel auto-deploy.** Remaining team requests still to prioritize/build: Archived Orders tab fix, $30 POS minimum, new paid add-ons (Air Dry / Double Wash / Steam Iron), per-bag add-ons at Foothill, employee discount, one-time charges, order history + launderer, chips $3→$1.50, driver mid-delivery pickup scheduling, production +/− controls, launderer photo view.*

*Previously: July 7, 2026 — Session 181 — **Recurring reschedule now has a "just this once" vs "apply to all future" choice (root-cause fix for one-time edits silently drifting the whole subscription).** David reported that editing a single recurring order's pickup/delivery permanently shifted the cadence going forward, with no way to make a change one-time-only. **How it worked before:** recurring orders carried a hidden `orders.recurring_anchor_at` (intended pre-shift PICKUP date) that the generator `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` uses to compute the next occurrence — so holiday/Sunday bumps never drift. But it was applied inconsistently: 335 of 363 active recurring orders had the anchor set (edits reverted), 28 had NULL (edits drifted, fell back to actual window), and **delivery had NO anchor at all** — the generator derived next-delivery from the just-completed order's actual pickup→delivery gap, so any one-time delivery change silently stuck going forward. **Fix (3 layers):** (1) migration `session_178_add_recurring_delivery_anchor` — new `orders.recurring_delivery_anchor_at` (nullable, mirrors the pickup anchor for the delivery leg). (2) Backfilled ALL 363 active recurring orders — filled the 28 missing pickup anchors (= their current pickup) and set every delivery anchor = `pickup_anchor + actual turnaround` (correct even for the 21 currently holiday-shifted); prior values snapshotted in `_archive._backfill_recurring_anchors_20260707`. (3) migration `session_178_recurring_scope_engine` — `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` now derives next-delivery from the delivery anchor (intended turnaround measured anchor-to-anchor), and `reschedule_order` + `reschedule_order_leg` gained **`p_apply_to_future boolean DEFAULT false`**: when true on a recurring order it advances the pickup/delivery anchor(s) so the new day/time becomes permanent; when false (default) only this occurrence moves and the chain reverts next cycle. Both functions DROP+CREATE'd (signature change) with REVOKE public/anon + GRANT authenticated/service_role preserved; ownership guard `enforce_caller_owns_order` unchanged (admin/staff + own-order customers pass). **UI (commit `dcaff8f`):** customer edit-order modal shows an "Apply to all future pickups" checkbox (recurring orders only) wired into `reschedule_order`; admin order-panel Schedule tab shows an "Apply changes to all future occurrences" checkbox (recurring only, resets per order but persists across a pickup+delivery edit in one panel session) wired into `reschedule_order_leg`. **Deliberately left one-time (flag omitted → default false):** all route-logistics paths — admin drag/drop, batch reschedule, RCC moves, `confirmReassignRun`, `selectSpSlot`, and the same-day `_opShiftDeliveryWindow` toggle — because moving a stop between routes should never permanently reshape a customer's subscription. **Verified:** two guaranteed-rollback trigger simulations proved (a) a one-time Wed/Thu shift reverts to the Mon/Tue +7 cadence and (b) an apply-to-future edit sticks at the new day +7, for both legs. Migration-review (clear, additive/reversible) + preflight (LOW, 0 customer contact — anchor-only writes short-circuit every window/holiday/status guard) + QA (blast-radius clean — only these two functions read the anchors; no app SELECTs the new column) all run. **The DB changes are LIVE and behavior-preserving on their own** (backfilled anchors reproduce the existing next-occurrence, and the new RPC param defaults to false), so the DB being ahead of the UI is safe. ⚠️ **DEPLOY PENDING: commit `dcaff8f` is committed locally but `git push` could not authenticate to GitHub from this environment — David must push from his Mac to trigger the Vercel auto-deploy of the two checkboxes.** Also cleared stale `.git/index.lock` + `.git/HEAD.lock` (crashed-session leftovers from Jul 6) via the cowork file-delete tool before committing. Note: migrations are prefixed `session_178_*` (applied name) though this is session 181 — find them via `list_migrations`.*

*Previously: July 6, 2026 — Session 180 — **Subscription-renewal alert investigated → NOT webhook drift, just one genuine failed renewal; refined the smoke-test check so past_due subs stop false-alarming.** The `nightly-smoke-test` SMS fired "stripe seam — subscription invoice missing: 1 active subscription(s) have no recorded invoice for the current period — possible stripe-webhook signature/secret drift" for **Jenn Holloway** (sub `1fef18a9…`, cust `ffeda888…`, Monthly Subscription $275/mo, plan `73c7ae5d…`). **Diagnosis — the "signature/secret drift" hypothesis is WRONG.** Scope check across all 64 non-cancelled subscriptions: **63 recorded their current-period `subscription_invoice` normally** (several within hours, e.g. Elizabeth Gettinger + Sonny Grewal today) — if the webhook signing secret were broken, EVERY renewal would fail to record, not one. Jenn's own sub received a Stripe event at 03:11:47 today (`last_stripe_event_at`) that set `status='past_due'` + `dunning_started_at` — proving the webhook is delivering + verifying events fine. Her **July 6 $275 renewal genuinely FAILED** (card visa …2156 exp 07/2027 is valid, so likely insufficient funds / bank block); no July invoice was recorded simply because invoices only write on a SUCCESSFUL payment (they live as `customer_transactions` rows `type='subscription_invoice'` — there is no separate invoice table). Stripe's dunning auto-retry is in progress. **David's call: let Stripe auto-retry — NO data change made to Jenn's record.** **Root-cause fix (migration `smoke_test_exclude_past_due_from_missing_invoice`):** the `audit_subscriptions_missing_invoice()` RPC (which the smoke test calls) filtered `status IN ('active','past_due')`. Including `past_due` is self-contradictory — a past_due sub by definition has no paid invoice (Stripe told us the payment failed, which is itself proof the webhook works), so flagging it as "webhook drift" is a guaranteed false positive. Narrowed the filter to `status = 'active'` only. TRUE webhook drift = payment succeeded on Stripe's side but our DB never recorded it, which keeps the sub `active` — so genuine-drift detection is fully preserved. Body-only `CREATE OR REPLACE` (SQL, STABLE, SECURITY DEFINER); signature/return type/`search_path`/grants (service_role, authenticated, postgres — no anon) all unchanged. Ran washroute-migration-review (LOW/clear, trivially reversible — just replace `= 'active'` back with `IN ('active','past_due')`). **Verified:** `SELECT * FROM audit_subscriptions_missing_invoice()` now returns 0 rows (Jenn, being past_due, is correctly excluded). **Follow-up offered, not built:** if David wants visibility into customers stuck in dunning, that belongs in a SEPARATE business alert — this smoke test is now purely a webhook-health check. DB migration applied directly via MCP (this project tracks migrations by name; the smoke-test RPC lives only in the DB, not in a repo file). No edge-function or app code change needed.*

*Previously: July 3, 2026 — Session 179 (cont.) — **The bug recurred (2 more commercial orders unpaid) → found a SECOND charge-skipping path in the POS + shipped a server-side auto-charge safety net that covers EVERY path for good.** After the admin-side fix below, David flagged that Onika #8432 ($57.75) and Covenant House #8421 ($89.25) were unpaid again. Both reached Ready for Delivery AFTER the admin fix deployed, via a generic "Ready For Delivery" status_change with NO charge attempt (`billing_status=NULL`, not 'failed'), actor = POS attendants (Juana Olivar Casiano, Benita Juarez). **Root cause of the recurrence:** the admin fix only covered `admin-dashboard/index.html`. The **POS app** (`pos/index.html`) has its own path — `advanceQueueOrder` behind the Retail Queue "✅ Mark Ready" button — that calls `advance_order_status` with NO charge step. Commercial DELIVERY orders processed at Foothill (Covenant House, Onika, Kidango, Kasa, Extended Stay) are marked ready there, so they slipped the same way. Full audit confirmed the complete set of writers into `ready_for_delivery`: admin (5 status paths, now charge-gated) + admin `rack_order` (own billing) + **POS `advanceQueueOrder` (was uncovered)**; driver + customer apps only ever skip/cancel, never touch ready. **Data fix:** charged both — Onika #8432 PAID (visa 1248); **Covenant House #8421 card DECLINED** (genuine decline, now correctly stamped `failed` → shows "Update card" in the unpaid widget, NOT the bypass bug). **Permanent fix (David chose "server-side safety net" over per-button patching):** migration `session_179_autocharge_ready_sweep` — new SECURITY DEFINER function `sweep_autocharge_ready_orders()` + pg_cron job **`wr-autocharge-ready-sweep` every 5 min**. It charges (via `net.http_post` → `charge-order`) any order that is `ready_for_delivery`/`out_for_delivery`/`delivered`, `billing_status IS NULL`, `total_amount>0`, `source<>'walk_in'`, customer `billing_type<>'on_account'`, has a card on file, `archived_at IS NULL`, and `updated_at < now()-3min` (lets the client's own charge finish first). LIMIT 20/run. This enforces charge-on-ready in ONE server-side place, immune to any current OR future UI path. **Why it's safe (preflight LOW):** `billing_status IS NULL` excludes both paid AND failed (declines never retried in a loop); on-account + cardless excluded; charge-order's idempotency key (`co_<orderId>_<pm>`) prevents any double-charge with the client; no order trigger sends customer messages on a billing change; on success it fires the SAME `payment_received` SMS+email a normal charge sends (no new message type); on decline stripe-webhook only stamps `failed` for non-subscription orders (no dunning fan-out). **First-run blast radius = 0** (backlog already cleared); manual dry-run returned 0 charged, no error. EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC/anon/authenticated, granted service_role only (no app user can trigger a charge sweep). Ran preflight + migration-review before applying. ⚠️ **The admin-side commit `6009505` is already deployed (David pushed it); the DB migration is applied directly (no repo file — this project tracks migrations by name).** No POS code change was needed — the sweep covers it.*

*Previously: July 2, 2026 — Session 179 — **Commercial orders delivered UNPAID → root-caused to a status path that skipped charging; centralized the charge-on-ready invariant so no path can drift again.** David reported (screenshot) that some Commercial-pricelist customers (Nourish Osteo, Onika Crossman) weren't auto-charged when their order hit Ready for Delivery — he'd been manually pushing them through. **Not a pricelist problem:** both are `billing_type='automatic'` with valid cards; Onika has a long history of successful auto-charges. **Root cause:** SIX admin paths advance an order to `ready_for_delivery`, and the "charge the card" step was copy-pasted into FIVE of them and MISSING from one — `setSingleOrderStatus` (the single-order "Set Status" override) called `advance_order_status` with no charge. These two commercial orders (#8325 Nourish, #8327 Onika, $52.50 each) were pushed through via that override right after folding — reaching Ready for Delivery with `billing_status=NULL`, never charged. Confirmed via `order_events`: they had a generic "Ready For Delivery" status_change (advance_order_status's `initcap` label) and NO `racked` event, so they never went through the charging rack/`rack_order` path. Diagnostic query found EXACTLY these 2 orders system-wide in the silently-unbilled state ($105). **Data fix:** charged both via `charge-order` (mastercard 6778, visa 1248) — both now `billing_status='paid'`, `billed_at` stamped; re-ran the diagnostic → 0 unbilled remain. **Root-cause fix (commit `6009505`, admin-dashboard/index.html only):** added `chargeOnReady(order, newStatus)` — ONE helper that owns the charge-on-ready invariant (on-account skip, Stripe `charge-order` call, and stamping `billing_status='failed'`+`charge_failed_at`+`billing_notes` on decline so it surfaces in Issues + the unpaid widget). Routed ALL FIVE status paths through it: `opSetOrderStatus`, `batchSetStatus`, `batchAdvanceStatus`, `updateOrderStatus`, and the previously-broken `setSingleOrderStatus`. Side wins: `updateOrderStatus` now correctly SKIPS card-charging on-account customers (it used to try to charge everyone), and `batchSetStatus`/`opSetOrderStatus` now stamp failed charges consistently. The rack flow (`saveRacking`→`rack_order` RPC) is intentionally left alone — it owns its own credit/tip-aware billing server-side. **QA (washroute-qa):** blast-radius clean — no direct `status:'ready_for_delivery'` writes bypass the two RPCs in any of the 3 apps; no DB function/cron auto-advances an order INTO ready_for_delivery (`sync_order_status_from_stops` does not, so driver actions can't skip the charge); the only writers are `advance_order_status` (now fully charge-gated on the client) and `rack_order`. Full inline-script `node --check` passed. ⚠️ **Deploy pending:** committed locally but `git push` couldn't authenticate to GitHub from this environment — David needs to push from his machine to trigger the Vercel auto-deploy. **Follow-up worth considering (offered, not built):** a server-side backstop (pg_cron sweep that finds ready_for_delivery + unpaid + not-on-account + has-card orders older than N min and calls `charge-order`) would make this class of bug impossible even if a future non-charging writer appears.*

*Previously: June 28, 2026 — Session 178 — **SMS delivery-receipt tracking added (root-cause fix for "we were blind to delivery failures"); diagnosed John's "all notifications failing" report as NOT a real outage.** **Diagnosis first:** John reported all SMS failing (David relaying, secondhand). Evidence showed the opposite — messages are being generated, handed to Twilio with valid SIDs, inbound replies still arriving (166/3d), and Twilio's own Messaging Health Score was 87/Good with Sent-rate Good. The only failures in Twilio Insights (last 7d) were EXPECTED compliance bounces: 75× error 21610 (recipient texted STOP), 5× 21408 (region permission disabled), 2× 30007 (carrier filtered) — ~82 of thousands. The number David tested (415-608-5446) is not a customer and not opted-out. Conclusion: no system outage; "all failing" was either secondhand or specific opted-out recipients. **Root-cause gap found:** WashRoute never recorded delivery OUTCOMES — across all 47k+ outbound rows the only statuses that ever existed were sent/queued/accepted (the value Twilio returns at SEND time, i.e. "accepted from us", not "delivered to handset"). Every sender stored that initial status and nothing listened for Twilio's later delivery callback, so genuine delivery failures were invisible by design — which is exactly why this was hard to see. **Fix shipped — full Twilio StatusCallback pipeline:** (1) migration `session_178_sms_delivery_receipt_columns` — added nullable `error_code`, `error_message`, `delivered_at`, `status_updated_at` to `sms_messages`; partial index on `twilio_sid` (callback lookup — there was NONE, would've been a 47k-row scan per callback) + partial index on failed/undelivered. (2) migration `session_178_record_sms_delivery_status_rpc` — SECURITY DEFINER `record_sms_delivery_status(p_sid,p_status,p_error_code,p_error_message)` writes the columns server-side (immune to the session-176 PostgREST schema-cache trap) with a terminal-status guard so a late out-of-order 'sent' can't clobber 'delivered'/'failed'. REVOKE public+anon, GRANT authenticated/service_role. (3) NEW edge function `twilio-status-callback` (verify_jwt false) — parses Twilio's form/JSON callback, maps common Twilio error codes to readable text, calls the RPC; always 200s so Twilio doesn't retry-storm. (4) Wired `StatusCallback` into the 4 customer-facing senders: **send-sms v31, send-order-notification v42, notify-on-my-way v33, send-scheduled-reminders v27** (one added param each, `${SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1/twilio-status-callback`). **Intentionally NOT wired:** `health-monitor` + `nightly-smoke-test` (internal alert/diagnostic texts to staff/test numbers, not customer notifications). **Verified end-to-end in production:** synthetic callback test (insert temp row → delivered → undelivered+30007 → confirmed status/error_code/error_message/delivered_at → row deleted), AND a REAL outbound message recorded `status='delivered'` minutes after deploy — the FIRST EVER 'delivered' status in the system's history. Ran migration-review (additive/reversible), preflight (LOW — adds a callback param, cannot fan out / increase volume; redeploy doesn't fire the cron), and QA (blast-radius clean — no app code or DB trigger branches on `sms_messages.status`, so the new terminal values break nothing). Repo synced (4 senders + new function + 2 migration SQLs). **Admin 'SMS Delivery' view BUILT (same session):** new failures-first Analytics page in `admin-dashboard/index.html` (nav item under Analytics + `page-smsdelivery` container + `loadSmsDelivery`/`setSmsdFilter` + added to `ALL_PAGE_IDS`/`PAGE_LABELS`/`pageTitles`/`pageCTAs`) — 7-day Failed/Delivered/Awaiting summary cards, filter chips (Failures default), and a table of recent outbound texts with a status pill + failure reason + customer name, reading the new receipt columns. All DB-sourced strings `esc()`'d (XSS-safe), full inline-script `node --check` passed, FK (`sms_messages_customer_id_fkey`) + RLS (`admin_all_sms_messages`) verified, status-value blast-radius clean. Deploys via git push → Vercel. **Inline Inbox delivery badges BUILT (same session):** each outbound bubble in the SMS Inbox now shows a small indicator beside its timestamp — green ✓ Delivered, red ✗ Failed/Undelivered (hover shows the reason), grey ✓ Sent for awaiting/historical messages — via `_smsDeliveryBadge(m)` injected into the `msg-ts` line in `openSMSConversation` (thread query already `select('*')`, so no query change). **Follow-ups offered (not built):** a failure-spike alert; optional X-Twilio-Signature validation on the callback. Also this session: recovered a stuck `git pull` (178 commits behind, stashed/popped a local PROJECT-NOTES DB-perf note cleanly) and committed/pushed that note. Commit `<this>`.*

*Previously: June 15, 2026 — Session 177 (cont.) — **Delivery price-cut scenario model built (analysis only, no code/DB change): `$55/20 lb` vs May's `$59/25 lb`, with optional conversion + demand layers.** David explored lowering the Delivery Wash & Fold price. Modelled on ACTUAL May 2026 Delivery W&F volume (service_id `d97ba33a…`, delivered orders w/ weight: **1,571 orders / 2,280 bags, avg 22.8 lbs/bag**). **Pricing model reminder:** Delivery W&F is `per_bag`, base currently **$65** (was **$59** in May), `lbs_per_bag=25` included PER BAG, `$3/lb` overage beyond bags×included; line_items carry a `type:'base'`, a `type:'overage'` (e.g. "11 lbs overage × $3"), and discounts as negative `type:'discount'`. My formula `bags*base + max(0, weight − bags*incl)*3` reproduces May's actual line items exactly. **Findings (base+overage only, holding add-ons/fees/tips/discounts constant):** (1) **Price alone $55/20/$3 → +$7,902 (+5.5%)** vs May's $144,801 → $152,703. Counter-intuitive but right: dropping included weight 25→20 exposes up to 5 lbs × $3 = $15 more overage/bag, which beats the $4 base cut; **break-even ≈ 21.3 lbs/bag**, May avg 22.8 → most loads pay slightly more (915 up / 656 down). $55/**22** lbs ≈ revenue-neutral (−$150). (2) **+ Signup conversion 50%→60%** applies ONLY to ~150 monthly new signups (David's clarification — NOT a 20% lift on the whole book): 75→90 first orders = **+15 orders ≈ +$1,500/mo**. (3) **+ Overall demand +5%** (whole-book elasticity, ~79 orders) **≈ +$7,600**. **All three stacked ≈ $161,800 (+$17,000 / +11.7%) for May.** **Deliverable: `WashRoute-Delivery-Pricing-Model-May.xlsx`** (repo root) — fully editable model: Model tab (3 stacked effects off blue inputs), Threshold Data (overage-lbs by included-weight 15–28), Sensitivity (base×included grid + conversion table), By Weight Band. Built with formulas + recalc, 0 errors. **Caveats flagged to David:** one-month view (understates LTV of retained converts), revenue not margin (no variable cost per order), static (no behavioural weight-splitting). Offered an annual contribution-margin/LTV extension. No code, DB, or schema changes.*

*Previously: June 15, 2026 — Session 177 (cont.) — **Discounts page: % discounts showed "—"/$0 usage → now shows real Count + new Value column (admin-only, no backfill).** David flagged that percentage discounts (SENIORS, NON PROFIT, EDUCATORS, VETERANS) showed $0/no usage despite being applied to many orders. **Audit:** the Discounts list's usage came from `discount_redemptions`, which is a **CLAIM ledger** (a customer typed a code into the customer app → `redeem_discount_code` RPC inserts a row; also enforces single-use: 1/customer for %, 1 global for fixed). Staff apply SENIORS/NON PROFIT directly at intake — `record_order_intake` sets `orders.discount_id` but never creates a claim row — so those % codes had 0 redemptions (LOVELAUNDRY, a customer-app code, had 16 claims but 34 actual orders → even it was undercounted). ALL 22 existing `discount_redemptions` rows have `order_id IS NULL` — the table was never an order-usage ledger. **Decision: did NOT backfill** `discount_redemptions` (would conflate claims vs order-usage and could break the single-use `already_redeemed` checks that query it). Instead fixed the DISPLAY to read the real source of truth, `orders.discount_id`. **Shipped (admin-dashboard/index.html only, commits `0ae22de` + `f8b14e0`, pushed → Vercel):** `loadDiscounts` now also fetches orders w/ a discount_id (incl. line_items) and aggregates per-discount, skipping cancelled/skipped. Columns are now **Code · Created · Count · Value · Last Use** (renamed "Redeemed" → "Count", added "Value"). For **% discounts:** Count = # orders, Value = total $ discounted (summed from the negative `type:'discount'` line in `line_items`), Last Use = most-recent order date. For **fixed coupons:** Count = # claims, Value = total $ credited (Σ `discount_value_snapshot`), date = last claim — unchanged source (claim ledger), since fixed coupons add account credit and don't set `orders.discount_id`. Verified live numbers: NON PROFIT 81 orders/$494.00, LOVELAUNDRY 34/$492.56, SENIORS 106/$386.65, EDUCATORS 8/$53.25, VETERANS 2/$5.90. JS syntax-checked, 0 errors. **No migration, no backfill, no schema change** — usage is now derived (can't drift). Note: `discount_redemptions.order_id` column exists but is unused/always NULL; the claim ledger and order-usage are intentionally separate concepts.*

*Previously: June 15, 2026 — Session 177 (cont.) — **ALL card charging was down ~15 hours (session 176's `charge_in_progress_at` column + `charge-order` v48 shipped in the same push; data-API schema cache never picked up the column) → restored via project restart + `charge-order` v49, then recovered the backlog.** **Morning audit (June 15):** system broadly healthy — 0 wrong-date stops, 0 ghost/desync issues except 1 fixed below, 0 orphan auth, cron clean, credit-overpay clean (session 176 fix holding); flagged 4 small zone-overlaps (largest Hayward/Concord 0.87 sq km), SF route soft-over capacity (informational), and the SMS-consent baseline count (1,116 = migration artifact, NOT opt-outs — did NOT touch). **Fixed Lilly Krenn #6765** (delivered order whose delivery stop was stuck `en_route` → set `complete`; one-off heal, root cause of the missed auto-sync not chased). **THE CHARGE OUTAGE (John reported "Could not acquire charge lock: column orders.charge_in_progress_at does not exist"):** session 176 added `orders.charge_in_progress_at` (migration `session_176_charge_in_progress_lock`, ~02:14 UTC) AND deployed `charge-order` v48 referencing it via `db.from('orders').update({charge_in_progress_at})` — but PostgREST/data-API served a pre-column schema snapshot, so EVERY charge 500'd (raw Postgres 42703 in postgres logs, even though `information_schema` showed the column and a direct `UPDATE` worked). Because v48's failure path (`stampChargeFailed`) ALSO wrote that column, the bug couldn't even stamp `billing_status='failed'` — so the resulting "failed" orders were genuine pre-bug declines, and the bug's victims were just left silently unbilled. **What did NOT fix it:** `NOTIFY pgrst, 'reload schema'` (×2), a `COMMENT ON COLUMN` DDL nudge to fire `pgrst_ddl_watch`, and `pg_terminate_backend` on all `authenticator` connections — fresh post-termination connections STILL 42703'd. **What DID fix it:** (1) David restarted the project from the Supabase dashboard (rebuilt the schema cache — the only thing that cleared it; confirmed fresh `authenticator` conns post-restart at 18:21 UTC); (2) in parallel, deployed **`charge-order` v49** which DROPS all `charge_in_progress_at` reads/writes (immune to the stuck cache) and preserves double-charge protection via a deterministic Stripe idempotency key `co_<orderId>_<paymentMethod>`. ⚠️ **The session-176 DB-level anti-double-charge claim is currently REMOVED (v49); the idempotency key covers rapid double-submits. Re-introducing the DB claim is an optional follow-up now that the cache is healthy — if you do, ADD THE COLUMN AND PROVE THE API SEES IT BEFORE deploying code that uses it (see below).** **Backlog recovery (preflight-gated, David approved "16 w/ cards, mute decline msgs"):** of 20 failed delivery orders ($2,126.55), 16 had a card on file; temporarily disabled the `payment_failed` template (sms+email), fired `charge-order` for the 16 via `net.http_post`, **14 succeeded = ~$1,306.85 recovered**, 2 genuine re-declines (Andrea Diaz #4659 $74.95, Ruby Anderson #7210 $139.95 — no message sent, template was muted), then **restored `payment_failed` to sms+email ON**. Remaining on unpaid widget: those 2 + 4 card-less orders (need a card). **Skill updated:** `washroute-migration-review.skill` — corrected the session-177 note (connection-bounce does NOT reliably fix a wedged data-API cache; the real fix is a project restart, and the PREVENTION is ordering: add column → prove the API sees it → THEN deploy dependent code; never both in one push). **Root-cause prevention now lives in the migration-review skill.** Commit for v49 is edge-function-only (deployed via MCP, repo should be synced next time someone touches charge-order — repo is behind production v49).*

*Previously: June 14, 2026 — Session 176 — **Receipt double-counted account credit (Todd Bower #7240) → fixed across all 3 receipt paths.** Todd emailed "your receipt differs from my account": his emailed receipt for order #7240 showed the "Account credit applied −$34.10" line TWICE and a "Paid by Card" of $20.75, but his card was actually charged $54.85 (one $34.10 credit applied; confirmed by the charge txn + the "$54.85 received" SMS). **Root cause:** every receipt renderer showed the account credit from BOTH the `type:'credit'` line item AND the `customer_transactions` credit_use sum, and computed the card total as `(total_amount + tip) − credit` — but `orders.total_amount` is ALREADY net of the credit, so the credit was subtracted a second time. **Fixed in all three paths:** `send-receipt` (edge fn **v41**, deployed), admin `printInvoice`, and POS thermal + HTML receipt builders. Account credit now renders exactly once (from the transactions sum, with a line-item fallback for legacy orders that predate the credit ledger); the gross total is built UP from gross services so the card amount is no longer double-reduced; service discounts (SENIORS etc.) still render as their own lines. **Verified end-to-end** against Todd's real order data: exactly one credit line, "Paid by Card $54.85", the bogus $20.75 gone. David is issuing Todd a goodwill credit separately. (The two-day mishap Todd referenced — he texted "Cancel please", admin skipped it, but a driver was still dispatched — was the session 175 pt 5 stop-revival bug, already fixed; he also got a misleading "on the way" SMS that day.) send-receipt deployed via MCP; admin + POS changes pushed → Vercel auto-deploy.*

*Previously: June 13, 2026 — Session 175 pt 6 — **Driver app didn't auto-show orders booked mid-route (Angel, Jun 12) → fixed the realtime gate + added a mobile safety refresh.** Realtime infra is healthy (route_stops/orders/routes all in `supabase_realtime`; SELECT RLS for authenticated = `true`, so events ARE delivered). Two real gaps: **(1)** the driver-app `route_stops` INSERT handler gated on `isMyRoute && isMyDriver` — but a customer booking mid-route can make `auto_route_order` spin up a BRAND-NEW route run the app hasn't loaded, so `isMyRoute` was false and the new stop was silently dropped until a manual reload. Fixed to mirror the UPDATE handler: reload if the stop is `explicitlyMine` (driver_id = me) even on an unknown route, or inherited (`driver_id IS NULL`) on a known route. **(2)** No fallback when realtime misses an event — the websocket dies when the phone sleeps / signal drops (Angel was on 2 bars), and events arriving during a reload are skipped by the `_dataLoading` guard, with recovery only on a clean re-SUBSCRIBE. Added `startStopRefreshLoop()`: refresh on foreground (`visibilitychange`→visible) + every 60s while visible. `refreshStopsQuietly()` self-guards (`appReady`/`currentDriver`/`_dataLoading`/visibility), piggybacks any in-flight `loadDriverData()`, and re-renders only the route/home lists — never the open stop detail — so a driver mid-entry isn't disrupted. Idempotent (`_refreshLoopWired`). Client-only change (no DB/SMS); QA clean. Last night's mid-route add on Angel's active Berkeley PM run (order `7844df99`, 6:46 PM) is now `complete`; the no-show was the dropped realtime event with no fallback. driver-app/index.html only — pushed → Vercel auto-deploy.*

*Previously: June 12, 2026 — Session 175 — **Receipt-vs-Transactions investigation (Valerie Yasukochi #7081) → found + closed a rack-time $0 billing leak; 4 orders written off; subscription report add-on column fixed.** Valerie's receipt said "$5.70 paid with credits" but her Transactions tab showed nothing — because NOTHING was ever collected. **Chain of events:** intake computed $5.70 (Oxi ×2 − 5% senior) → moved back to Intake and re-saved 17s later at **$0** (the intake panel loads customer prefs ASYNC after opening; with weight prefilled the Save button was live immediately, so the fast save dropped the Oxi chip) → racked: `rack_order` saw total $0 → `fully_credited_no_tip` branch marked it **paid / method 'credit' / "Fully covered by credits"** with zero credits involved → moved back AGAIN, re-intake restored $5.70, but move-back never reset billing → re-rack saw 'already paid' → never charged. **Bigger finding — same hole, nastier entry:** subscription orders are usually $0 at rack time (plan covers base) and the lb-overage trigger (`apply_subscription_usage_fn`) only adds the overage DURING the status UPDATE — but old `rack_order` decided billing on the PRE-update total, so subscription overages were systematically marked paid-without-charging: **Lachar Burns #6896 ($74.25) + #6977 ($118.25)**. Honest labels: $0-due-no-credits orders get notes 'No balance due' + NULL method (receipts stop claiming credits). (2) migration `session_175_reintake_resets_false_credit_paid` — `refund_order_credits` (step 1 of every `record_order_intake` + moveOrderBack) now ALSO resets billing fields when `stripe_payment_intent_id='credit_applied'` (synthetic marker only — real `pi_...` payments never touched), so re-rack re-bills on the new total. *Previously: June 10, 2026 — Session 173 — **Fixed: customer booking could default delivery on/before pickup → hard DB failure (Sina S).** Sina reported her order failed because the default delivery was before the pickup date. Investigated the customer-app booking flow — it's a CLASS of bug, not one line: (1) when the preferred delivery slot is full, the "nearest available" alternative finder calls `get_nearest_available_slots` which searches SYMMETRICALLY around the preferred date and can surface a delivery slot on/before the pickup day (`_selectAltSlot` then sets it); (2) stale `isSameDay`/`deliveryDate=pickupDate` state if the pickup window changes to one where same-day isn't available; (3) `selectSchedWindow('delivery', idx)` reads `draft.availableWindows[idx]` (pickup-day windows) instead of `_deliveryWindows` — the `fullIdx` is an availableWindows index so cross-date delivery selection no-ops/mismatches. The existing soft guards only dead-ended with a toast. **Fix shipped:** a final correctness guard in `placeOrder` (customer-app) — if the computed delivery start isn't strictly after the pickup start, NON-same-day orders auto-correct to the next valid service day via `getNextDeliveryDay` instead of failing; same-day keeps its explicit block. This makes the booking robust against ALL the above paths. Commit `75d6019`, build 20260610214830. **✅ ROOT FIXES SHIPPED (commit `56da044`, build 20260610220100):** (1) delivery alt-slots now hard-filter `run_date > draft.pickupDate` (the `_deliveryOptions` restriction was skipped when that list was empty, letting the symmetric `get_nearest_available_slots` search leak on/before-pickup dates) — so no before-pickup delivery alternative is ever shown; (2) `selectSchedWindow(which, slotStart)` now resolves the tapped window by its `slotStart` against the correct source (`_deliveryWindows` for delivery vs `availableWindows` for pickup) instead of an availableWindows index — fixes cross-date delivery-window taps silently no-op'ing. The submit-time guard (75d6019) stays as defense-in-depth. JS syntax-checked, deployed live.*

Computed in the front-end (`loadDeliveryKpis`): `delivery pounds (RPC current/previous.pounds) ÷ Σ(laborData.laundry_hours)` from `fetchSquareLaborHours()` for the current + prior-month windows (the SQL RPC can't call the Square edge function). Verified live: shows 26.5 lbs/launderer-hour ▲2.8% vs last month (realistic; and free of the route-hours data artifact since Square hours are well-tracked). Null-safe: shows — when launderer hours are 0/unavailable. Commit `f3d937b`, build 20260610191803.

**⚠️ ROUTE-HOURS DATA RELIABILITY — investigation findings (David asked to investigate + report; NO behavior changed):** The per-hour KPIs' denominator = Σ(`routes.completed_at` − `routes.started_at`) for routes with valid times. That data is unreliable historically and has a structural going-forward gap:
- **Coverage by month** (routes with valid start+finish): Mar 3%, Apr 29%, May 73%, Jun 34% (June low partly because many routes are recent/in-progress). For *past* routes in the last 21 days coverage is actually **82%** (170/207).
- **Stop-span fallback is also bad pre-May:** Mar/Apr route_stops.completed_at span ~80h/route (migration backfill / completions over long spans) → only realistic May onward.
- **How times are stamped:** `complete_route_stop` RPC (driver tap-to-complete) sets `routes.started_at = COALESCE(started_at, now)` on first stop completion and `routes.completed_at = now` ONLY when the last stop makes `v_pending=0` (all stops terminal). So drive-time-to-first-stop isn't counted (minor under-count), and **single-stop routes get started_at==completed_at → 0 duration** (the ~14% non-positive, now excluded).
- **ROOT CAUSE of the 32/37 recent missing-`completed_at` routes:** they have ALL stops terminal but no `completed_at` (0 were due to lingering open stops). Cause: route-completion stamping lives ONLY in `complete_route_stop`. When a route's final stop reaches terminal status via a DIFFERENT path — skip/fail (`cantCompleteStop`), or admin-side completion via `advance_order_status` — the route's `completed_at`/`completed_stops` are never recomputed.
- **PROPOSED FIX (needs David sign-off, future session — cleanest path):** move route counter/`completed_at` recomputation OUT of `complete_route_stop` into an `AFTER UPDATE OF status ON route_stops` trigger (or a shared helper every stop-mutation path calls), so EVERY terminal transition (complete/skip/fail/admin) keeps the route timestamps correct. Optionally a nightly backstop that stamps `completed_at = max(stop.completed_at)` on past routes whose stops are all terminal. This makes per-hour metrics trustworthy going forward (pre-May stays unreliable; comparisons get solid once 2+ clean months exist).

*Previously: June 9, 2026 — Session 171 — **Receipts printed stale pricing from cache (post price-increase) — fixed at the reprint paths.** David printed two receipts for #6269 (Melissa Boldridge, recurring delivery order created June 2, pre-increase): the 10:48 AM slip showed the OLD estimate (2 bags × $59, no overage, blank weight) while the 1:44 PM slip showed the correct repriced total (2 bags × $65 + 5 lb overage = $154.95). **Root cause:** the customer was charged correctly ($154.95 at 11:46); only the earlier reprint lied. Intake recalculated to $65+overage at 8:22 AM, but the 10:48 reprint rendered a STALE in-memory cache copy of the order (booking estimate, weight_lbs=0). `printBagTag` (admin reprint) builds the receipt from `o.line_items`/`o.total_amount`/`o.weight_lbs` off whichever cache it finds the order in (`allOrders`/`allCleanOrders`/`allFoldOrders`/`allProcOrders`) with NO fresh DB read — so a screen open since before intake, or a different device, prints the old price. Same class as session 154 pt 5 (print path leans on caches). **Fix:** `printBagTag` now re-fetches `line_items, total_amount, weight_lbs, total_bags, tip_amount, tip_type, pickup/delivery_window_start, delivery/pickup_address_id, order_number` fresh from the DB and overlays them onto the cached order right before building the receipt (keeps the cached `o.customers`+addresses embed the address resolver needs under non-admin RLS). The await is placed AFTER the sync `window.open` so the popup isn't blocked. **`_autoPrintIntake` left as-is — verified fresh by construction** (prints the just-saved `lineItems` + `bd.preTipTotal`, never the cache). **Blast radius:** POS `reprintQueueOrder` (pos/index.html) had the identical pattern — reprints from the `queueOrders` cache via `queuePosPrintJob`→`buildPosReceiptMarkup`. Low-risk for walk-in retail (charged immediately, not repriced) but the POS now also shows delivery orders (session 149 "🚚 For delivery") that CAN be repriced at intake, so same fix applied: fresh re-fetch of `line_items, total_amount, tax_amount, weight_lbs, total_bags, tip_amount, tip_type` overlaid before printing. **Lesson banked: any receipt/bag-tag print MUST re-fetch pricing+weight from the DB at print time — never trust the on-screen order cache.** Both files saved on disk; build-version bumped. **Needs David's `git push` + `cowork-manual-deploy` to ship (admin + POS).***

Because the **admin order detail + customer app order detail render all line items from the order**, they now show the included line via DATA ALONE — no front-end deploy needed for the live views. **Renderers that gated on amount>0 or hardcoded the label were patched:** `send-receipt` v40 (email — now shows the $0 included line) and the 3 admin print/thermal/HTML invoice builders (use the stored label for the $0 delivery row instead of hardcoded "Delivery Fee"). Verified live: all 14 orders carry one "Delivery — included" $0 line; non-subscription delivery ($9.95) unaffected; admin redeployed (build 20260608235534, relabel present); send-receipt v40 deployed. **⚠️ Lesson: `service_fees.show_in_app` is display-only (banked last entry); and receipts that filter `amount>0` will hide legitimate $0 "included" lines — gate on the label/type, not amount.** Commit on origin via David (`132535a`).*

*Previously: June 8, 2026 — Session 170 continued — **Driver app: phantom stale stop after reschedule fixed.** Driver John Taladiar reported Suzanne Stroebe's order #6396 "still showing on the AM Berkeley route" after he rescheduled its delivery to the evening. Investigation: the DATA was already correct (delivery repointed from Berkeley AM → Berkeley PM via `reschedule_order_leg` at 8:57am; pickup stop complete on AM Sat, delivery stop pending on PM tonight — clean, no orphan). The bug was in the **driver-app realtime handler**: the `route_stops` UPDATE handler removes a stop when it's reassigned to another driver or a route that isn't the driver's, but when a stop is rescheduled to ANOTHER OF THE SAME DRIVER'S routes (AM→PM), it fell through to a partial `Object.assign(allStops[idx], updated)`. The realtime payload carries only raw `route_stops` columns — not the joined route name or the order's window — so the stop stayed grouped under the OLD (AM) route showing the OLD time. The driver kept seeing a phantom AM stop, which even led to a spurious `delivery_failed` (Amalia, 10:01am) that auto-corrected on the next reschedule (10:03am). **Fix:** when a stop's `route_id` changes but it's still the driver's, trigger a full `loadDriverData()` reload so route sections, grouping, and joined window data all refresh (driver-app/index.html, route_stops UPDATE handler). Syntax-checked. Commit `a5f836c`, pushed. **⚠️ Needs `cowork-manual-deploy` to ship** (driver app). **Process note banked: ALWAYS `git push` after committing — session-170 front-end work didn't deploy the first time because commits were local-only; the Vercel hook builds from GitHub origin/main.** build-version bumped.*

*Previously: June 7, 2026 — Session 170 continued — **Fees tidied by price list + $0 Subscription delivery fee hidden from app (display/pricing decoupled).** David noticed a second "Delivery Fee" at $0 appearing in Services → Fees AND App Display, and asked what it was + to hide it. It's the **subscriber free-delivery fee** (`service_fees` id `0b21ae51`, pricelist='Subscription', created session 168) — load-bearing: it's how a subscriber's delivery resolves to $0. It was leaking into every price list's App Display because the admin fee views didn't filter by `pricelist`. **Fixes:** (1) admin **App Display** now filters fees by the selected price list (`feeInList`: scoped fee shows only under its own list, global/null fees everywhere). (2) admin **Fees tab** badges price-list-scoped fees (+ "hidden from app") so the two "Delivery Fee" rows are distinguishable. (3) **⚠️ Decoupled display from pricing** — the customer-app loaded `allServiceFees` with `.eq('show_in_app', true)`, and `getCustomerFees()` (which computes the delivery fee CHARGED + displayed) reads that array. So hiding the $0 row via show_in_app would have made subscribers fall back to the $9.95 row at booking. Changed the customer-app fee load (index.html ~line 3047) to `.eq('is_active', true)` instead, so pricing always resolves every active fee while `show_in_app` governs only the customer-facing pricing MENU. (4) **Data:** the $0 Subscription Delivery Fee is kept `show_in_app=true` (visible) UNTIL the customer-app code above is deployed — because the LIVE (un-deployed) app still loads pricing by `show_in_app`, so hiding it pre-deploy made subscriber Andrew Foster fall back to $9.95 in the booking summary (caught + reverted same minute). **POST-DEPLOY STEP:** once `cowork-manual-deploy` ships the customer-app change, set `show_in_app=false` on `service_fees` id `0b21ae51` to hide it from the customer pricing menu — safe then because pricing loads by `is_active`. Billing was always protected by the session-169 `link_subscription_on_order_fn` trigger (forces subscription orders' delivery to $0 at write); only the displayed amount was affected. Verified via SQL sim: with the new load, subscriber delivery resolves to $0, Delivery customers $9.95. **⚠️ Lesson banked: `service_fees.show_in_app` is DISPLAY-only — never gate pricing/charging queries on it; load pricing fees by `is_active`.** Commit `7a605ac`. **Front-end → must trigger `cowork-manual-deploy` Vercel hook to go live; until then the booking summary may show $9.95 delivery to a subscriber (billing still $0 via trigger), so deploy promptly.** build-version bumped.*

*Previously: June 7, 2026 — Session 170 — **SMS reorder booked a closed Sunday — day-of-week convention bug fixed + deployed (twilio-webhook v42).** David spotted order #6632 (Parker Thomas) with a Sunday pickup window that hit a routing error ("No matching pickup route"). Investigation: Parker texted "Pickup" on Saturday Jun 6; the SMS reorder handler auto-created the order and replied "booked for Sunday, Jun 7." **Root cause:** `getNextPickupDayPT` in `twilio-webhook` used JS `Date.getDay()` (0=Sun…6=Sat) to compare against `route_templates.schedule_days`, which uses WashRoute's **0=Mon…6=Sun** convention. Sunday (getDay()=0) falsely matched schedule_days value 0 (= Monday), so the SMS path booked closed Sundays — and the same off-by-one made Saturday pickups un-bookable via SMS (Saturday→read as Sunday→rejected). Bug only bites at the Sat/Sun boundary, which is why it surfaced rarely. **Fix:** convert via `(getDay()+6)%7` (new `wrDow` helper, mirroring customer-app + admin). Also hardened the DELIVERY side — it previously set delivery = pickup + turnaround_days blindly, so a Saturday pickup +1 would land a closed Sunday delivery; new `getNextDeliveryDayPT` skips forward to the next running day. **Data:** #6632 was already hand-corrected by David earlier today (pickup→Mon Jun 8, delivery→Tue Jun 9); audit found no other affected orders (0 Sunday-window orders system-wide). **Verified** via standalone logic test: Sat Jun 6 text → pickup Mon Jun 8, delivery Tue Jun 9 (exactly David's manual fix); Sat pickup + turnaround now skips Sunday delivery to Monday. Deno typecheck clean. Deployed twilio-webhook v42 (verify_jwt:false preserved). Preflight confirmed deploy fans out 0 messages (function is inbound-webhook-only, no cron/trigger caller). Commit `7a21ac2`.*

*Session 169 continued — **Subscriber mischarge bug (Jenn Holloway #6567) — 2 root causes fixed + DB backstop.** David spotted that subscriber Jenn Holloway's order today was charged $9.95 (card declined) when it should've been $0, AND her subscription usage didn't update. Root-caused to TWO independent bugs in the admin intake path: **(Bug A — wrong delivery fee)** `getDeliveryFees()` read the customer's pricelist from the stale `allCustomers` cache (`allCustomers.find(...)?.pricelist`), while the base service used the FRESH-fetched pricelist. Jenn subscribed the evening before, so the cache still had her as 'Delivery' → base resolved $0 (Subscription) but delivery fee resolved $9.95 (Delivery). The $0 Subscription Delivery Fee row exists + is active; the bug was purely the stale source. Fixed: added module var `procPricelist` set from the fresh fetch in `openIntakePanel`; `getDeliveryFees` now uses it so base + delivery fee share one source. **(Bug B — not linked → no usage, treated as billable)** `record_order_intake` never sets `subscription_id`/`is_subscription_order`, and the usage trigger `apply_subscription_usage_fn` gates on `subscription_id IS NULL` → never fired; the unlinked order also looked billable so charge-order tried $9.95. Fixed at the DB level (cleanest path, David's call): new **`trg_aa_link_subscription_on_order`** BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger (`link_subscription_on_order_fn`, SECURITY DEFINER, migration `session_169_auto_link_subscriber_orders`) auto-resolves `subscription_id` from the customer's active sub whenever the customer is on Subscription pricelist + order is unlinked, and enforces invariant `is_subscription_order = (subscription_id IS NOT NULL)`. Named `trg_aa_` so it fires before `trg_apply_subscription_usage`. Covers EVERY order-creation/intake path, not just admin intake. Tested both paths (flag-sync + auto-link via rolled-back txn). **Data fix:** #6567 repriced to $0 (delivery→$0), linked to her sub, 17 lbs accrued (sub now 17/100, 1 pickup), failed charge cleared → billing_status='paid'/'credit'. Also healed #6611 (Sukhesh — was linked but is_subscription_order=false). Blast radius was tiny (subs just launched). Note: Jenn's old #5908 is a pre-subscription pickup_failed order ($80.95, unlinked) — inactive, left as-is. Files: admin-dashboard/index.html (4 edits), build-version.txt bumped, migration applied. Edge function charge-order already handles $0 orders correctly (creditsOnly path → paid, no Stripe call) so no change needed there. **Fast-follow worth considering: `is_subscription_order` is fully derivable from `subscription_id IS NOT NULL` — could become a generated column later to eliminate the flag entirely.***

Headline catch: the **Stripe webhook signing secret in Supabase did not match Stripe** — every real Stripe event was being rejected with HTTP 400 (this is why David's June 2 $275 invoice had to be backfilled with a NULL payment_intent). David updated `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` in Supabase; verified fixed by resending an event (Test 1.1) and then by a live new-card signup (Test 1.2) that produced a real `subscription_invoice` row with a real PI + LTV bump. Then a deep iteration on the subscription pickup picker surfaced and fixed a string of issues (zone filtering, 2-hour windows, address-in-flow, delivery date display, a $59-instead-of-$0 pricing bug, today-eligibility, same-day delivery, standing paid extras synced to Preferences). Two DB trigger migrations + edge-function + customer-app changes. **Four real bugs found + fixed (all existed in deployed code): webhook secret mismatch, $59-instead-of-$0 recurring pricing, overage DOUBLE-CHARGE, admin order address showing "No address on file."** Tests 1.1–1.4 PASS; 1.5 (subscription-invoice refund), 1.6 (cancel/reactivate), Block 2 (go/no-go), Block 3 (soft-launch) still pending — session paused here. **Strong recommendation: keep the soft-launch group very small and watch the first real subscriber orders closely, since several of these bugs were in production code.***

*(a) **Test 1.1 — Stripe webhook secret mismatch FIXED (critical).** `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` in Supabase ≠ Stripe's signing secret → `constructEventAsync` threw → 400 on every event. David corrected it in the Supabase dashboard. Resending the June 2 `customer.subscription.created` landed (200, idempotent, no dup, LTV unchanged). NOTE: resending a `subscription.created` event re-writes the sub row with the event's CREATION-time snapshot (status went to `incomplete`); corrected David's row back to `active` to match Stripe. Lesson: to re-verify the webhook, resend an idempotent `customer.subscription.updated` or rely on a live signup, not `subscription.created`.*

*(c) **Server-side subscription allowlist (create-subscription v3 + create-checkout v33).** Per the session-157 lesson that a UI flag is NOT a billing boundary: with the $275 plan now `is_active=true` for soft-launch, both subscription-creating edge functions gained a hardcoded `SUBSCRIPTION_ALLOWLIST` (currently `dmacquart@gmail.com`, `dmacquart+wrsignup1@gmail.com`, `dmacquart+sub4@gmail.com`). While non-empty, only those emails can create a subscription server-side even though the plan is active. create-checkout's subscription branch also gained the gate (it had `is_active` but NO ownership check — a back door that only returns a Checkout URL, but closed anyway). **MONDAY LAUNCH: set both arrays to `[]` and redeploy to open to everyone.** Also confirmed charge-order v45 is subscription-agnostic — it bills `total_amount + tip` regardless of subscription, so add-ons / same-day / overage all charge correctly as long as they're line items in total_amount.*

Now renders only active `subscription_plans` rows with a clean empty state. The `PLANS` const was deleted.*

*(f) **Subscription pickup picker — full overhaul (`showSubscriptionPickupPicker` + `saveSubscriptionPickupPreference`).** Issues found + fixed in sequence: (1) **Zone filtering** — picker showed every zone's windows (incl. the Kidango COMMERCIAL route) because a no-address subscriber had no zone; now requires an address (new `showSubscriptionAddressPrompt` reuses the address modal via a `window._afOnSaved` callback, then reopens the picker zone-scoped); if address maps to no serviced zone → "book manually" message. Order #6530 verified: $0 base + $0 delivery + Vinegar $3 = $3, today pickup, correct windows, subscription linked.*

Without this, recurring subscription cycles delivered same-day / applied extras but never billed them (the same-day delivery window itself was already preserved via the pickup→delivery turnaround interval). Existing NON-subscription recurring same-day customers were never affected (their branch copies line_items wholesale) — 25 active recurring same-day orders verified correct. Subscriptions aren't live so zero customer impact; fixed pre-launch.*

*(k) **OVERAGE DOUBLE-CHARGE — most important catch (fixed).** A subscriber over-cap order was charged the lb-overage TWICE: the processing intake (`_buildIntakeLineItems` + `saveIntake`) persisted a `type:'overage'` line + counted it in `total_amount`, AND the `apply_subscription_usage_fn` trigger ALSO wrote a `lb_overage` line + bumped total at ready_for_delivery. On David's 120-lb test (20 over): $55 + $55 = order charged **$116 instead of $61**. Fix: for subscription orders the trigger is the SOLE authority — intake no longer persists the overage line and subtracts it from the persisted subtotal (caps intake credit to the smaller total); the breakdown still PREVIEWS it so the launderer sees the full figure. The intake overage line was also mislabeled "$3.00/lb" (service rate) though the amount was the correct $2.75 subscription rate. Non-subscription overage unchanged. Blast-radius checked: the admin order-panel Details edit (`opSaveDetails`) is SAFE because the Subscription W&F service has `has_weight_overage=false` (so it never writes an overage line; base resolves to $0). Commit `7da6fa1`. **This bug was in deployed code — any subscriber over-cap order processed before today would have been double-charged.***
