*Previously: June 4, 2026 — Session 167 continued — **Phase 7 + 8 shipped: in-app subscribe modal (saved-card path) + embedded Stripe Payment Element (new-card path) + cancel/reactivate UX fixes.** Customers now subscribe entirely in-app with zero Stripe-hosted handoff. Both paths share a bottom-sheet modal with spinner during the 3-4s Stripe round-trip. Cancel/Reactivate both render correctly now — earlier the UI re-rendered identically because (a) `cancel_at_period_end` was never read by the renderers and (b) the post-action refresh called `renderAccount()` which doesn't exist (the real function is `loadAccount`). New edge function `create-subscription` v1 (saved-card path, idempotency-keyed, server-side plan is_active gate, error_if_incomplete payment behavior). Extended `resume-subscription` v9 to clear `cancel_at_period_end` (used by the Reactivate button). New customer-app modal `showSubscribeNewCardModal` lazy-loads Stripe.js + mounts Payment Element + confirms SetupIntent + saves card + creates subscription, all without leaving the app. `_startSubscriptionViaCheckout` (the old hosted Checkout fallback) removed from dispatch. Launch runbook rewritten for v2 architecture. Ready for Monday June 8 — two switches: (1) flip `SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED()` body to `return true` in customer-app, (2) check Active on the plan in admin. See LAUNCH-RUNBOOK-275-SUBSCRIPTION.md for the full sequence.*

*(u) **Phase 7A — create-subscription edge function v1.** New function (~150 LOC) that creates a Stripe subscription using the customer's `stripe_default_payment_method_id` (no Stripe-hosted handoff). Auth: require ownership (caller's profile_id must match customer.profile_id, anon-key rejected). Validates: plan exists + is_active (Session 157 server-side gate); customer has stripe_customer_id + saved card; no duplicate active sub. Auto-creates Stripe Product + Price on first use (mirrors create-checkout pattern). Uses `payment_behavior: 'error_if_incomplete'` so first-invoice card-decline surfaces immediately (returns HTTP 402 with code='payment_failed') instead of leaving the sub in 'incomplete' status. Idempotency key keyed on `(customer.id, plan.id)` so double-tap returns the same Stripe sub. Webhook (v45) handles all subsequent DB sync: pricelist auto-switch to Subscription, subscriptions row upsert, customer_transactions insert for the first $275 invoice, lifetime_value bump.*

*(v) **Phase 7B — customer-app in-app subscribe modal (saved-card path).** `startSubscription(planId)` now dispatches: customer has `stripe_default_payment_method_id + card_last4` → `showSubscribeConfirm` (saved-card bottom-sheet modal); no card → `showSubscribeNewCardModal` (Payment Element). Modal shows plan summary + "Billed to Amex •••• 3004" + Subscribe button. On confirm: spinner + "Processing…" → calls create-subscription → on success closes modal, refreshes user data, opens existing pickup-day picker. On error: shows red error box, restores button, suggests "update your card" if code matches payment_failed/declined.*

*(w) **Phase 7C — payment-failure banner + Reactivate flow.** Dunning banner (`#home-sub-dunning`) was already there from session 165 but pointed to the wrong panel — fixed to deep-link directly to `sub-payment` (one tap closer to the card management UI). Added a Reactivate button to the Subscription panel when `cancel_at_period_end=true`, with a yellow "Cancellation scheduled · Your subscription will end on [date]" notice. `resume-subscription` v9 extended to also clear `cancel_at_period_end` (Stripe accepts both `pause_collection` and `cancel_at_period_end` in a single update call). All three subscription actions (Subscribe / Cancel / Reactivate) gained inline `.sub-spinner` + "Processing…" / "Cancelling…" / "Reactivating…" button text during the 3-4s Stripe round-trip.*

*(x) **Phase 7 follow-up fixes:** (i) Cancel button looked like it did nothing because `renderHomeSubscriptionCard` + the Subscription panel never read `cancel_at_period_end`. Fixed: home card shows "Cancelling" badge + "Cancels on Jul 2" label when scheduled; panel swaps Cancel for Reactivate button + cancellation notice. (ii) Initial cancel/reactivate fix referenced a nonexistent `renderAccount()` function so the panel never re-rendered post-action. The real function is `loadAccount()` — switched to that. The DB state was always correct; the only issue was that the customer-app's post-action refresh was a no-op.*

*(y) **Phase 8 — embedded Stripe Payment Element for new-customer subscribe.** Replaces the previous fallback (hosted Stripe Checkout redirect) so customers WITHOUT a saved card can subscribe end-to-end without leaving the app. New `showSubscribeNewCardModal(planId)`: lazy-loads Stripe.js (~50KB, only fetched on first need; cached) → calls `create-setup-intent` → mounts Stripe Payment Element with `layout: 'tabs'` → enables Subscribe button on Element 'ready' event. On submit: `stripe.confirmSetup` with `redirect: 'if_required'` (US-only, no SCA expected) → gets payment_method_id → calls `save-payment-method` (attaches to customer + sets as default if first card) → calls existing `create-subscription` (now sees the just-saved card) → success path identical to the saved-card modal. Both paths share the same bottom-sheet styling, spinner, success toast, and pickup-day picker. Old `_startSubscriptionViaCheckout` function removed from the dispatch — Stripe-hosted Checkout is no longer a customer-app code path (the `create-checkout` edge function still exists for backwards compatibility but isn't called anymore). `STRIPE_PK` constant added to customer-app (publishable key, safe to embed client-side, same value as admin-dashboard).*

*(z) **Launch runbook rewritten** (`LAUNCH-RUNBOOK-275-SUBSCRIPTION.md`). Old runbook described the v1 bill-split architecture which was discarded yesterday. New runbook: (a) architecture summary; (b) pre-flight system-health SQL checks (duplicate-services audit, plan inactive, no surprise live subs); (c) launch sequence — two switches only: flip `SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED()` body in customer-app to `return true`, then check Active on the plan in admin; (d) end-to-end smoke test steps; (e) soft + hard rollback procedures; (f) inventory of what's wired (edge functions, triggers, UI surfaces); (g) known limitations + post-launch monitoring checklist.*

*(aa) **Cancel/Reactivate UX polish (Phase 7 afterburner).** Cancel button looked like it did nothing because `renderHomeSubscriptionCard` + the Subscription panel never read `cancel_at_period_end` (which is what Stripe + our DB set on a graceful cancel). Fixed: home card shows "Cancelling" badge + "Cancels on Jul 2" label when scheduled; panel swaps Cancel button for a navy Reactivate button + yellow "Cancellation scheduled" notice. The first attempt at the fix called a nonexistent `renderAccount()` function so the panel never re-rendered post-action — switched to the real function `loadAccount()`. Also added inline `.sub-spinner` + "Cancelling…" / "Reactivating…" / "Processing…" button text on Subscribe / Cancel / Reactivate so the 3-4s Stripe round-trip has visible feedback. `resume-subscription` v9 extended to also clear `cancel_at_period_end` (Stripe accepts both `pause_collection` and `cancel_at_period_end` in a single update call). Lesson banked: **renderer must read every field that an action can mutate** — `cancel_at_period_end` was set server-side correctly the whole time; only the UI was stuck because the renderers didn't consume it.*

*(bb) **Phase 8 PM — production monitoring suite (3 builds + ops index).** Catching the "7 hours of zero customer orders before anyone noticed" class of failure with three layers of automated checks. **(1) `health-monitor` edge fn + pg_cron job 17** — every 15 minutes counts customer-app orders in last 60min vs same 60min 24h prior; SMS-alerts David's phone (Twilio direct, not via send-sms so the monitor still works if send-sms is what broke) when business-hours order rate drops to 0 (baseline ≥3) or >80% (baseline ≥5); 60min dedup; 6-hourly heartbeats; would have caught yesterday's HOTFIX in 30-60min instead of 7h. **(2) `audit_duplicate_services()` SQL function + `database/audits/daily_audit.sql` bundle of 8 daily checks** — orphan route_stops, unpaid delivered orders, recent health alerts, subscription usage sanity, credit-ledger imbalance, RPC warnings spike, dormant Stripe customers, AND the new "duplicate `(name, sort_order)` services" check that catches the HOTFIX class structurally. **(3) `nightly-smoke-test` edge fn + pg_cron job 18** — 3am PT runs an end-to-end happy-path: service resolution sanity, dup-services audit, Delivery Fee lookup, schema-level INSERT with safety-belt `WR-SMOKE-TEST` marker, validate total, cleanup delete; ~800ms; failure → critical SMS. **`_health_alerts` table** is the audit trail for all of the above. **Verified live:** all 3 monitors fired correctly during build; the smoke test even CAUGHT the dup-services collision (Subscription W&F + Delivery W&F both at sort_order=1) and 3 real SMS alerts hit David's phone before I bumped Subscription W&F to sort_order=100 to resolve. **MONITORING.md** at repo root is the single-page running index: every monitor + audit + alert routing + how to disable/pause + emergency stop + change log + ideas for future builds. Add a row every time we ship a new check.*

*(cc) **Defense-in-depth fix — Subscription Wash & Fold sort_order bumped from 1 → 100.** The HOTFIX patched the customer-app's `defaultService = allServices.find(...)` to filter by pricelist, so the dup rows are no longer dangerous at the client level. But at the DB level the two W&F rows both being sort_order=1 was still ambiguous — and Postgres's implicit ordering is implementation-defined. The bump removes the ambiguity entirely: now any future `.find()` (even one that forgets the pricelist filter) deterministically picks Delivery first. Per session 167 lesson (A) — structural prevention is cheaper than relying on every developer remembering the filter.*

*(dd) **Klaviyo welcome v3 fix — silent 400 rejection across all 11 signups since June 3.** Investigation triggered by David asking "the welcome message is still not being sent to anyone." Of 11 customer-app signups in the last 33 hours, 5 had `email_marketing_consent_at` stamped (consent was true → should have fired Klaviyo) but the Email List `VDLwxj` had only 3 profiles total. Edge function logs showed ZERO klaviyo-subscribe invocations until I triggered one manually — and that one came back from Klaviyo with `400 'last_name' is not a valid field for the resource 'profile'`. Same class as the v1→v2 fix (June 1, phone_number rejection): Klaviyo's `profile-subscription-bulk-create-jobs` endpoint accepts ONLY `{email, subscriptions}` (plus phone_number when SMS consent is included) — anything else fails the whole batch with the function still returning 200 to the customer-app (non-fatal pattern hides it). v3 strips first_name + last_name; names still reach Klaviyo via the nightly `sync-klaviyo` profile upsert. Backfilled all 8 customers with email_marketing_consent_at stamped since June 1 via a one-shot DO block calling klaviyo-subscribe in a loop — Email List jumped from 3 → 11 profiles, all enrolled in the Welcome Series within minutes. **Lesson banked: this endpoint has a TINY allowed field set. Don't add ANY profile attribute without checking the Klaviyo API spec first. The 200-OK-on-failure pattern hides bugs for days/weeks if no one's checking the Klaviyo list count regularly.** Worth adding to MONITORING.md as a future check: weekly query of recent (consent stamped) customers vs Klaviyo Email List membership; alert on drift.*

*Previously: June 3, 2026 — Session 167 — **Subscription rebuild day: discarded the session-165 v1 bill-split overlay and rebuilt subscriptions as a separate pricelist (Subscription Wash & Fold $0/bag + $0 delivery fee + lb_overage line item written by DB trigger).** Eight commits + 7 migrations + multiple edge function redeploys. After David tested v1 the previous evening, he asked the architecture question that triggered the whole day: "Are we building this the right way, or will subscriptions add more spaghetti code? Would it be simpler to treat Subscriptions as a separate price list?" The answer was yes, and we spent the day executing the rebuild. Then a production outage at 16:00 UTC (7 hours of zero customer-app orders, surfaced via Barbara F's SMS "your app charged me zero and refuses to continue") forced a HOTFIX at 23:50 UTC — `defaultService = allServices.find(s => !s.is_addon)` was picking the new Subscription Wash & Fold ($0) row instead of the Delivery one ($59) because the two had identical name + sort_order, so EVERY non-subscriber customer saw $0 estimates and the RPC rejected their orders with "Base line item amount must be > 0". Fixed by tightening the filter to `(!s.pricelist || s.pricelist === 'Delivery')`. The rebuild itself is correct, end-to-end tested on David's account, and the framework is launch-ready for Monday June 8. Phase 7 (in-app saved-card subscribe modal) ships Thursday; Phase 8 (embedded Payment Element for new customers) ships Friday.*

*(a) **Phase 1 — Revert v1 bill-split overlay code (commits `a25593c`, `e78df87`, `4eb50c5`, `fa20c29`).** The session-165 v1 architecture computed subscription totals via display-layer derivations across 8+ surfaces. After dogfooding David's verdict: "There's simply too many display issues (admin still showing per pound). It's a mess." Reverted all customer-app + admin + edge function display work; charge-order reverted to pre-v35 state then re-patched to drop the subscription_id guard.*

*(b) **Phase 2 — Schema + Subscription pricelist data (migration `session_166_subscription_pricelist_v2`).** Added `service_fees.pricelist TEXT NULL` (NULL = global). Added `subscriptions.previous_pricelist TEXT NULL` so cancellation can restore the original pricelist. Created Subscription Wash & Fold service ($0/bag, lbs_per_bag=25, has_weight_overage=false, is_active=true, show_in_app=true — that show_in_app=true is what later caused the HOTFIX) and Subscription Delivery Fee ($0).*

*(c) **Phase 3 — stripe-webhook v44: auto-switch customer.pricelist on subscribe/cancel.** On `customer.subscription.created`: snapshot current pricelist → `subscriptions.previous_pricelist`, then set `customers.pricelist='Subscription'`. On `customer.subscription.deleted`: restore from previous_pricelist (fallback 'Delivery'). After deploy: every booking + intake + admin lookup naturally resolves to Subscription-priced services for active subscribers, no display-layer code anywhere.*

*(d) **Phase 4 — lb_overage line item logic in trigger (migration `session_166_lb_overage_line_item`).** Replaced `apply_subscription_usage_fn`. On transition to `ready_for_delivery` for a subscriber order: compute weight_over = max(0, weight - (plan.weight_limit - subscription.usage_pre_order)), overage_dollars = weight_over × plan.overage_price_per_lb. Strip any existing lb_overage from line_items (idempotent on re-fire), append the new one, adjust total_amount. Update subscription usage + pickups + overage_amount_due. Expanded the subscription_usage_log CHECK constraint to include 'order_ready', 'order_delivered', 'overage_invoiced', 'final_overage_invoiced'.*

*(e) **Phase 5.1 — Relax `create_order_for_customer` RPC guard.** Migration `session_167_relax_base_amount_guard_for_subscription`: split the `base_amount > 0` check into three conditions — NULL still errors, < 0 still errors, but `= 0` only errors when `customer.pricelist <> 'Subscription'`. Subscribers can legitimately have $0 base orders. Non-subscribers still get the safety guard. This single guard is what later caught the HOTFIX bug at the DB boundary — every Delivery customer's app sent base_amount=0 and the RPC rejected them with "Base line item amount must be > 0 for non-subscriber orders" (clearly logged in postgres logs, 15 distinct errors over 7 hours).*

*(f) **Phase 5.2 — Recurring trigger Subscription branch (migration `session_167_recurring_trigger_subscription_branch`).** `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` now has an `ELSIF v_pricelist = 'Subscription'` branch alongside the existing Commercial branch. Re-resolves service_id to Subscription pricelist, rebuilds line_items as [base $0 "Wash & Fold · N bags × $0.00", delivery_fee $0]. If parent had subscription_id=NULL (customer just subscribed mid-chain), looks up customer's active subscription. Commercial/HCEB/Delivery branches untouched.*

*(g) **Phase 5.3 — Customer-app `getCustomerFees()` helper.** Deduplicates service_fees by name, preferring the pricelist-specific row over the global (NULL pricelist) row for the current customer. Replaces `allServiceFees.filter(...)` at 4 call sites + Pricing panel query. Without this, a Subscription customer would see BOTH the global $9.95 Delivery Fee AND their $0 Subscription-specific row when summing route fees, double-charging them.*

*(h) **Phase 5.4 — Admin `getFeesForPricelist(pricelist)` helper.** Mirror of the customer-app helper. Replaces `allFees.find('Delivery Fee')` at 4 admin sites: opRecalcEstimate, opSaveDetails, saveOrder, getDeliveryFees. Added `pricelist` to `_orderSelectCols` customer join. Same-day surcharge lookups remain global per David's clarification.*

*(i) **Phase 5.5 — stripe-webhook v45: subscription invoice → customer_transactions + LTV.** On `invoice.payment_succeeded` with subscription_id: parse invoice.amount_paid, idempotency-check on stripe_payment_intent_id, INSERT `customer_transactions` row with `type='subscription_invoice'`, description like 'Subscription · Jun 2026', payment_method='credit_card', card_brand+card_last4 from default card. Then bump `customers.lifetime_value`. This makes LTV accurate for subscribers — without it the monthly $275 would be invisible to all revenue reports.*

*(j) **Phase 5.6 — Backfill David's $275 initial invoice.** One-off DO block (idempotency-checked) inserted the missing subscription_invoice row for his existing test sub. Updated lifetime_value from $0 to $275. The row has stripe_payment_intent_id=NULL because the original payment_intent wasn't captured at signup time — Phase 6 fix #6's refund resolver handles this by looking it up from Stripe.*

*(k) **Phase 6 fix #1 — Processing intake subscription-aware overage.** David tested at 1 bag / 150 lbs: intake showed "125.0 lbs over limit · +$375.00" (computed from service defaults: 25 lbs/bag × $3/lb). Correct: 50 lbs over · +$137.50 (vs plan 100 lbs cap × $2.75/lb). Fix: `openIntakePanel` now fetches active subscription + plan into `procSubscription` global for Subscription-pricelist customers. `calcProcTotal` branches to plan-based math when procSubscription exists. Subtitle, alert, and breakdown rows all switch to plan-based labels. Mirrors the DB trigger so launderer's preview matches what gets billed.*

*(l) **Phase 6 fix #2 — send-receipt nets credit_use minus credit_refund (send-receipt v38).** Re-saved intakes can fire multiple credit_use/credit_refund pairs but send-receipt only summed credit_use. David's $0 subscriber order receipt showed "Account credit applied −$7.80 · Total $0 (paid with credits)" (fictitious). Fix: query both types, net them per order_id. Zero-net produces a clean receipt. Side note: this same deploy accidentally enabled verify_jwt:true on send-receipt (the deploy tool requires explicit verify_jwt per call), causing every admin "Send Receipt" call to silently 401 for ~2 hours — fixed in Phase 6 fix #6's redeploy.*

*(m) **Phase 6 fix #3 — Admin Set-Status modal stays silent on customer SMS.** I initially added send-order-notification calls to setSingleOrderStatus + batchSetStatus to match the kanban Advance behavior. David pushed back: "admin should not fire off a text anyway." Driver-app remains the canonical trigger for picked_up / OTD / delivered SMS; all admin-initiated status changes are silent.*

*(n) **Phase 6 fix #4 — lb_overage trigger idempotency (migration `session_167_lb_overage_trigger_idempotent`).** Bug: after RFD → OFD → RFD → OFD → RFD bouncing, David's subscription showed "150/100 lbs · 3 pickups · $137.50 overage" for one 50-lb order. The trigger's original guard caught no-op same-state UPDATEs but not legitimate status bounces — each RFD transition re-added 50 lbs. Fix: new column `orders.subscription_usage_lbs_applied NUMERIC NULL`. Trigger applies DELTA (`weight - prior_applied`) instead of absolute weight; pickup count only increments on FIRST apply; pre-order usage basis subtracts this order's prior contribution so lb_overage recomputes correctly. Handles no-change re-fire (delta=0), weight-change re-fire (delta applied), status-bounce (no double-count). Known limitation: usage doesn't auto-reverse on order cancellation (admin Adjust Usage button covers).*

*(o) **Phase 6 fix #5 — Customer-app order details polish.** Three issues: (1) Total showed "Confirmed at pickup" instead of "$0.00" because truthy check fails on 0. Fix: explicit isSub check via subscription_id || is_subscription_order. (2) Weight row missing — added with guard `parseFloat(o.weight_lbs || 0) > 0` (avoids misleading "0.0 lbs"). (3) No receipt email arrived after RFD — updateOrderStatus (Advance arrow) charged but never called send-receipt; only the rack flow did. Added send-receipt fire on every ready_for_delivery transition to mirror the rack flow.*

*(p) **Phase 6 fix #6 — Admin refund for subscription_invoice transactions (refund-charge v23 + admin button + send-receipt v38 redeploy).** Two-part fix: (1) Admin rendering — refund button now appears for `type='subscription_invoice'` rows; relaxed stripe_payment_intent_id precondition for that type. (2) refund-charge — allowedTypes includes 'subscription_invoice'; new `resolvePaymentIntentForSubscription` helper looks up customer's recent paid Stripe invoices, matches by amount, skips already-recorded payment_intent_ids, backfills resolved ID onto the row. Subscription refunds correctly DON'T touch order state (no `billing_status='refunded'`). Customer gets tailored SMS. Same deploy redeployed send-receipt v38 with verify_jwt:false restored.*

*(q) **HOTFIX (commit `568fad1`) — `defaultService` picked wrong row, 7 hours of zero customer orders.** Production outage 16:00-23:00 UTC. Postgres logs: ~15 attempted orders, all rejected with "Base line item amount must be > 0 for non-subscriber orders". Barbara F SMS'd "your app charged me zero and refuses to continue." Loren Lovgren + at least one other had the same complaint. **Root cause:** Phase 2's new Subscription Wash & Fold ($0/bag) was created `show_in_app=true, sort_order=1` — identical to Delivery Wash & Fold ($59/bag, sort_order=1). loadServices ordered by sort_order with no secondary sort; Postgres returned the rows in arbitrary order. `defaultService = allServices.find(s => !s.is_addon)` picked WHICHEVER came first. In sessions where Subscription came first, every Delivery customer got basePrice=$0 → baseTotal=0 → RPC rejection. **Fix:** `defaultService = allServices.find(s => !s.is_addon && (!s.pricelist || s.pricelist === 'Delivery'))`. Subscribers still get their service via `_pricelistServices[pl]` (unchanged). Blast-radius QA pass searched all four apps: every other `.find()` on allServices either looks up by explicit ID OR filters by category='Delivery' OR cascades pricelist → Delivery → any. Only the customer-app's defaultService line was vulnerable. **Damage:** ~15 rejected real customer orders. David needs to follow up with Barbara, Loren, and any others.*

*(r) **Lessons banked from session 167:** (A) **Duplicate row hazards in `.find()`** — adding a new row to a shared table that other code uses `.find()` on requires auditing every consumer's filter. Structural prevention options: Postgres unique partial index, daily-audit check, washroute-audit skill rule, or a documented pattern in PROJECT-NOTES. (B) **Reverts + new schema in the same session need an end-to-end check before declaring done.** Phase 1 revert + Phase 2 row addition each looked safe alone but interacted catastrophically. (C) **DB error logs are the canary** — Postgres logged 15 explicit rejection errors over 7 hours; if we'd checked `_rpc_warnings` or postgres ERROR logs at the end of each migration, we'd have caught the regression within minutes. New pre-launch checklist item: spot-check rejection logs. (D) **verify_jwt is sticky on edge function redeploy** — the deploy tool's `verify_jwt` parameter is set per-call, not inherited. Always preserve via `get_edge_function` before redeploying OR explicitly pass the existing setting. (E) **`type IN (...)` extensions are the right pattern for ledger growth** — refund-charge was easy to extend because the original code used a type allowlist. Future ledger types fit the same pattern. (F) **Subscription-as-pricelist > subscription-as-overlay.** The v1 architecture (display-layer derivations across N surfaces) was correct but unscalable. The v2 architecture (subscription IS a pricelist; the order naturally has $0 base + $0 delivery; lb_overage written by a trigger) means existing code keeps working unchanged. Every total-display surface, every report, every receipt — they all just render what's in the order, and the order is already correct. (G) **Stripe invoice ordering matters for refund resolution** — the Phase 6.6 resolver could match the wrong invoice if multiple identical-priced invoices exist and the right one isn't recorded. Post-launch: also store `stripe_invoice_id` on customer_transactions for exact lookup. (H) **Idempotency via state column > guard predicates** — the lb_overage trigger's `OLD.status = 'ready_for_delivery'` guard caught no-op UPDATEs but not status bounces. Tracking `subscription_usage_lbs_applied` on the row gives the trigger the data to compute deltas instead of absolutes. Pattern: when a trigger has accumulative side effects, track the accumulated state on the row that triggered it.*

*(s) **Outstanding tech debt from session 167:** (i) Add a unique partial index OR daily-audit check OR washroute-preflight rule that catches "duplicate (name, sort_order) pairs both show_in_app=true and is_addon=false within the same name" — structural prevention for the HOTFIX bug class. (ii) `resolvePaymentIntentForSubscription` in refund-charge could match the wrong invoice if multiple identical-priced invoices exist for one customer. Post-launch: also store `stripe_invoice_id`. (iii) lb_overage trigger doesn't reverse usage when a subscriber order is cancelled after intake — admin's Adjust Usage button covers but the automatic path would be cleaner. (iv) Recurring trigger doesn't rebuild line_items for the just-canceled-subscriber case (would copy parent's stale $0 line_items). Document as Phase 7 cleanup OR admin manual reprice button. (v) Phase 7 (in-app saved-card subscribe modal) + Phase 8 (embedded Payment Element for new customers) ship Thursday + Friday for Monday June 8 launch.*

*(t) **Deployed in this session.** Edge functions: `charge-order v44` (subscription guard removed, $0 subscriber orders allowed), `stripe-webhook v45` (pricelist auto-switch + subscription_invoice → LTV recording), `send-receipt v38` (credit netting + verify_jwt:false restored + lb_overage in DISPLAY_TYPES), `refund-charge v23` (subscription_invoice support + Stripe invoice resolver). Migrations: 7 total covering pricelist schema, lb_overage trigger v1 + v2, recurring trigger Subscription branch, RPC guard, event_type CHECK expansion, label polish, idempotency column. Commits: 8 in admin/customer-app for Phases 5-6 + HOTFIX. PWA build-version bumped multiple times.*

Launch-prep work shipped the bill-split logic + admin Subscriptions tab + customer-app polish. Evening test pass dogfooded the entire flow on David's own account behind an email allowlist, found + fixed an RLS gap on `subscription_plans`, removed Pause entirely (cleaner than the half-baked Stripe pause_collection semantics), rebuilt the Reports → Subscriptions dashboard to David's preferred layout, and most importantly: **propagated the subscription-aware bill-split to EVERY surface that displays an order total** — home cards, order detail pages, admin order popups + edit modal, intake panel breakdown, charge button labels + confirm dialogs, email + SMS receipts, and the Klaviyo OrderValue event. **NOTE (session 167):** This v1 architecture was DISCARDED on June 3 and replaced with subscription-as-pricelist. The display-layer code described in (n) and (o) below was REVERTED in session 167 phase 1. See session 167 entry above for the rebuild.* Current gate state at end-of-session: **plan `is_active=true`** (left on for the test pass — David should flip back to false until launch day to close the server-side gate), **1 live subscription** (David's own `dmacquart@gmail.com` test sub), `SUBSCRIPTIONS_ENABLED` gated by an email allowlist (only David's two emails see UI). On June 8: confirm plan still active, flip allowlist body to `return true` in customer-app, push.*

*(a) **Klaviyo welcome OTP fix (commit `ad05a83`, customer-app only).** Root cause confirmed against production data — last 30 days by provider: `phone` = 220 signups, 0 with `email_consent` in `auth.users.raw_user_meta_data`; `email` = 9 signups, 1 with `email_consent='true'` (David's `dmacquart+wrsignup1` test). Klaviyo's flow report for `XdXF4f` ("Email Welcome Series", trigger = Added to List → `VDLwxj` "Email List") confirmed: 2 recipients in 30 days, both David. The `klaviyo-subscribe` edge function (v2 from session 164) was already correct shape — it just was never being called from the OTP path. Three customer-app sites created customer rows without firing the helper: `handleNameSubmit` (OTP main path), `loadUserData` post-confirm fresh-insert branch, and `renderConfirmAuth` order-flow fresh-insert branch. Only `handleSignup` + `loadUserData` were wired in session 164. **Fix:** Auth panel 3 (name-collection) gained a new `name-email-consent` checkbox (default-checked, mirrors `s-email-consent` on the email-signup form); `handleNameSubmit` now reads it, writes `email_marketing_consent_at` on insert, on `claim_existing_customer` UPDATE only writes when the row hasn't already opted in/out (preserves session-160's permanent-opt-out invariant), and fires `subscribeKlaviyoWelcome` fire-and-forget when consent + email both present. `renderConfirmAuth`'s fresh-insert branch now also fires the helper when `m.email_consent` is true. Result: every customer-app customer-creation path now wires Klaviyo with the same consent semantics. **Decision: no backfill of the ~50–80 OTP signups from the last 30 days** — many already placed orders so a welcome email today would feel out-of-context (they stay on Klaviyo profiles via the nightly sync; they just don't get the Welcome Series specifically). Klaviyo's Added-to-List trigger only fires on FIRST add, so any double-fire across the four code sites is a guaranteed no-op.*

*(b) **Subscription launch prep — usage display polish (commit `aecf738`, customer-app + admin).** Three customer-app surfaces + two admin surfaces gained the polish needed for the $275/100lb launch. Customer-app home subscription card + My Plan detail panel: added explicit "X lbs left" line alongside "47.5 / 100 lbs used" so customers don't do the math; unlimited-pickup handling so `plan.pickup_limit=NULL` renders "3 pickups this cycle" instead of "3 / null"; renewal label changed from "Renews —" to "Renews on Jul 8" per David's wording. Admin Reports → Subscriptions table: new "Lbs Left" column (10 cols total now); same unlimited-pickup handling on the Pickups cell. Admin customer profile → Billing → Subscription block: same wording + unlimited handling; **new "Past cycles" table** below the active-sub display, sourced from `subscription_usage_log` rows with `event_type='period_reset'` (up to 12 most recent — laundry subs roll monthly so that's a year before pagination is needed). **Bug fix in `_callSubscriptionFn`**: was sending `SUPA_ANON_KEY` as the bearer token to pause/resume/cancel-subscription, which would have failed `assertOwnership()` with 401 on every admin click. Now passes the admin's `session.access_token`. Hadn't surfaced because no production subscriptions existed. Closes a launch-day blocker.*

*(c) **Subscription launch prep — Services & Pricing → Subscriptions tab (commit `9561b4d`, admin only).** New admin tab for defining + activating subscription plans, replacing direct-SQL plan management. Tab shows a plan table (name + description, price/mo, included lbs, overage rate, pickup limit or "unlimited", live subscriber count, ACTIVE/INACTIVE badge, Edit button). "+ New Plan" opens a modal with: name, description, price/month, included lbs/month, overage \$/lb, pickup limit (blank = unlimited), read-only Stripe price ID (auto-created by create-checkout on first activation), Active checkbox. Flipping Active ON from previously off surfaces a yellow warning banner inside the modal AND fires a `confirm()` before save with human-readable summary — two-step activation prevents accidental flips. Editing an already-active plan or saving without flipping suppresses the warning. Modal uses the standard `.modal-overlay.open` pattern for free click-outside + Escape handling. This is the admin home for plan management; Reports → Subscriptions tab stays for the operational view (who's subscribed, usage, dunning).*

*(d) **Subscription launch prep — bill-split logic, the core correctness work (commit `11560e4`).** Migration `session_165_subscription_bill_split` applied + charge-order v43 deployed. **The architecture problem we solved:** pre-launch, the `apply_subscription_usage_fn` trigger fired on `delivered` and stamped the ENTIRE order as `billing_status='paid' / billing_payment_method='subscription'` — which would have silently zero-billed add-ons + tips + same-day surcharges + lb-overage the moment subscribers existed. The charge-order edge function had a v35 guard that returned early for any order with `subscription_id`, compounding the gap. **New architecture:** (i) Trigger moves to `ready_for_delivery` (same moment charge-order computes the residual), no longer stamps billing_status — charge-order becomes the sole billing arbiter for ALL orders. (ii) New SECURITY DEFINER RPC `compute_subscription_residual(p_order_id uuid) → jsonb` reads `order.line_items`, categorizes line items by type (`base`/`overage`/`delivery_fee` → absorbed by sub; `pref_service`/`same_day_surcharge` → card; anything else → card to be safe), computes lb-overage against the plan's monthly cap × `overage_price_per_lb`, returns full jsonb breakdown with `card_charge_total`. `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on subscriptions row so concurrent ready_for_delivery transitions can't race. Grants per session-148 lesson-F: `REVOKE PUBLIC` + `REVOKE anon` + `GRANT authenticated, service_role`. (iii) charge-order v43 removes the v35 guard, calls the RPC, uses `card_charge_total` as the basis for the Stripe charge; credits still apply to non-tip portion only (tips never reduced by credits). New `subOnly` path: when residual = 0 (subscription fully absorbed), skip Stripe entirely + mark `billing_status='paid'` / `billing_payment_method='subscription'`. `billing_notes` records the split ("subscription absorbed $X; $Y lb overage; $Z credit applied"). Stripe metadata carries `subscription_id`, `subscription_absorbed`, `lb_overage` for downstream attribution. **Verified against 4 scenarios in rolled-back transactions:** (A) 25 lbs under cap with add-on + same-day + tip → sub absorbed \$86.95, card \$20.00 ✓; (B) 80 lbs at 50 over cap → sub absorbed \$86.95, card \$157.50 incl. \$137.50 lb-overage ✓; (C) paused subscription → full retail \$106.95 to card ✓; (D) base + delivery only, 20 lbs in plan → card \$0 (sub-only path) ✓. **Reversibility:** trivial — rollback in `migrations-draft/session_165_subscription_bill_split.sql` documents the prior trigger body; new RPC can be dropped.*

The old \$260 plan (id `fd2b5a64`, stripe price `price_1TMrk…`) stays in DB as INACTIVE for history. Customer-app loads ONLY `is_active=true` plans → 0 currently → no customer visibility.*

Average Revenue formula: `(prorated recurring rev + sum of customer_transactions type='charge' for subscriber customer_ids in period) / # active subscribers`. Both Recurring Revenue (pro-rated by periodDays/30) and Overage Revenue (uses `subscriptions.overage_amount_due` running total as a proxy) are approximations until we (a) record Stripe subscription invoice payments to `customer_transactions` and (b) add `orders.lb_overage_dollars` per-order column. Both refinements punted to post-launch.*

*(n) **Subscriber-aware estimates on the booking flow.** Two sites in customer-app: (i) Bag Count step's "estimated total" card — for subscribers shows only add-ons + same-day surcharge ($0 if neither), with note "Wash & Fold and delivery covered by your subscription"; (ii) Confirm step's Pricing card — base wash & fold and delivery render as italic gray "Included"; add-ons + same-day + tip show as line items; bottom-line label changes to "Charged today" reflecting only the card-side amount; footnote about the 100 lbs/month cap and $2.75/lb overage. Sticky CTA bar total mirrors the same card-side amount. Non-subscribers see the existing PAYG breakdown unchanged. Driven off `_activeSub.status === 'active' || 'past_due'`.*

*(o) **Subscription-aware order totals across EVERY surface** — the big phase 2 of the bill-split. Reworked the shared `orderChargedTotal(o)` helper in both customer-app + admin-dashboard + send-order-notification (as `orderCardCharge`) to return the CARD-CHARGED amount (subscription residual + tip) instead of the full PAYG total. New companion helper `orderSubscriptionAbsorbed(o)` parses `order.line_items` the same way as the server-side `compute_subscription_residual` RPC: `base + bag-overage + delivery_fee → absorbed`; everything else → card-paid. Requires `subscription_id, line_items` in every order SELECT (audited + added where missing — intake fetch, send-receipt, send-order-notification). Auto-fixed (no targeted edit) surfaces that already used `orderChargedTotal`: customer-app Home active orders card, customer-app Orders list, admin Orders table Total column, admin Unpaid Orders list + summary, admin A/R list + summary, admin customer-panel balance, admin Revenue stat. Targeted edits made: customer-app order detail Pricing block (new "Subscription · Covers $X (Wash & Fold + delivery)" line + tip + "Charged to card" total), admin order popup Details cost breakdown (absorbed items render italic gray "Included"; bottom-line label switches to "Charged to card"), admin Order Edit cost breakdown (same pattern), Processing Queue intake panel `renderBreakdownRows` (same pattern; Total label becomes "Charged to card"), admin Charge button label + confirm dialog + logged charge event (uses `orderChargedTotal`; confirm dialog includes a subscription-coverage note), email receipt via `send-receipt v35` (absorbed line items render italic "Included"; subtotal + total subtract absorbed; "Total Paid" matches the customer's bank-statement charge; new footnote about subscription coverage), SMS receipt `{{amount}}` template variable via `send-order-notification v38` (uses `orderCardCharge`), Klaviyo "Placed Order" `OrderValue` event (uses `orderCardCharge` so analytics + LTV reports match Stripe). Non-subscriber orders unchanged in every case (subscriptionAbsorbed returns 0). **Charge-order is unchanged** — its v43 already does the same math server-side via `compute_subscription_residual`; the work in (o) is purely about DISPLAYING the same answer everywhere a customer or admin reads a total.*

*(p) **Tested end-to-end on David's own subscription.** David subscribed his `dmacquart@gmail.com` account via Stripe Checkout, placed a test order (2 bags, $5 tip), walked it through the customer-app + admin views, confirmed: home subscription card shows "0 / 100 lbs · 100 lbs left · Renews on Jul 2"; My Plan detail panel shows same + the cancel button works without text vanishing; admin Reports → Subscriptions shows the row with all 6 KPIs populated; admin order popup shows base + delivery as "Included" with "Charged to card: $5.00" total; the Processing Queue intake panel does the same. Test verified the bill-split logic produces the right card-side numbers for the most common case (under-cap subscriber order with tip + no add-ons). Add-ons + same-day + overage paths verified in the SQL scenario tests earlier in the session, not exercised against live Stripe. **Remaining test gap: David hasn't yet walked an order through ready_for_delivery to verify the actual charge-order call fires correctly and the post-charge `billing_status='paid' / billing_payment_method='subscription'` stamping works on a real subscriber order.** Recommended as the next test step before launch.*

*(q) **Deployed in this session.** Edge functions: `charge-order v43` (subscription-aware bill-split via the new RPC), `send-receipt v35` (subscription-aware email receipt), `send-order-notification v38` (subscription-aware SMS receipt + Klaviyo OrderValue). Migrations: `session_165_subscription_bill_split` (new `compute_subscription_residual` RPC + updated `apply_subscription_usage_fn` trigger), `session_165_subscription_plans_admin_write_rls` (admin write policies). Commits: ~20 commits spanning helper updates, UI polish, RLS fix, plan cleanup, edge function deploys. PWA build-version bumped multiple times so customer-app reloads pick up the new JS automatically.*

*Previously: June 1, 2026 — Session 164 — **Klaviyo Phase 1 (conversion / laggard flow) shipped end-to-end in one session.** Three new mechanisms (signup → list-add, real-time order events, follow-up email templates) plus signup UX cleanup. Live, deployed, verified against production traffic.*

*(a) **Signup → Klaviyo Email List wiring.** New edge function `klaviyo-subscribe` (verify_jwt:false, calls Klaviyo's `profile-subscription-bulk-create-jobs/` endpoint with the `KLAVIYO_API_KEY` server-side secret). Hardcoded list id `VDLwxj` ("Email List" — trigger for "Email Welcome Series" flow `XdXF4f`). Non-fatal at every layer: every error path returns 200 with `ok:false` so a Klaviyo outage cannot delay or break signup. Customer-app `handleSignup` + `loadUserData` (post-email-confirm fresh-customer INSERT path) both fire a `subscribeKlaviyoWelcome({email, first_name, last_name})` fire-and-forget helper — gated on the email-consent checkbox. Both paths fire belt-and-suspenders style; Klaviyo's "Added to List" trigger only fires on FIRST list add so the double-fire is a guaranteed no-op for the welcome flow. Scope deliberately limited to customer-app self-service signups — admin-created customers and POS-attached customers do NOT auto-subscribe (they haven't consented through a checkbox). **v1 → v2 bug found mid-test:** v1 sent `phone_number` in the bulk-subscribe profile attributes alongside email-only consent; Klaviyo rejects that combination silently, so every real signup was failing while the function still returned 200 (non-fatal pattern hid it). v2 omits phone entirely from this call — phone still reaches Klaviyo via the nightly `sync-klaviyo` profile upsert, this call exists only to fire the Welcome Series. Also added full Klaviyo error body to console.warn so the next bug like this is one log read away. Commits `b5856ea` (v1) + `16c244d` (v2).*

*(b) **Signup form UX overhaul.** David pushed back hard on the required SMS-opt-in checkbox ("the customer needs to check the box in order to receive information about their order? That seems over the top"). Restructured to the Uber/DoorDash pattern: dropped the SMS consent checkbox entirely, added a fine-print disclosure paragraph under the Create Account button — "By creating an account you agree to receive operational SMS from Family Laundry (order updates, driver ETAs). Msg & data rates may apply. Reply STOP to opt out." TCPA-compliant for transactional SMS because the click on Create Account is the affirmative action and the disclosure names sender / message type / rates / opt-out. JS now sets `smsConsent = true` implicitly (`sms_consent_at` still timestamps in customers + auth metadata). Also softened the email-opt-in checkbox copy from "Email me deals, updates & reminders from Family Laundry. Unsubscribe anytime." → David's pass: "Subscribe to Family Laundry's newsletter for occasional deals and updates." Default-checked. Marketing SMS opt-in NOT bundled in — bundling under one "and/or" checkbox is risky for the SMS half per TCPA + A2P 10DLC channel-specific consent rules. SMS marketing deferred until there's an actual campaign worth shipping. Commits `abacf5c` (softer email copy) → `3514d05` (SMS checkbox removal) → `99ee17e` ("newsletter" tweak).*

*(c) **Real-time Klaviyo `Placed Order` event from `send-order-notification`.** v37 of the edge function now POSTs a Klaviyo standard "Placed Order" event whenever it receives `event=confirmed` (customer-app order placement), attributed by customer's `email_cache`, with `OrderId / OrderValue / BagCount / PickupDate / DeliveryDate / $value / value_currency=USD / unique_id=order.id` (dedups against double-firing). This unlocks the laggard conversion flow's "has placed an order yet?" conditional split — which would otherwise lag by 24h waiting for the nightly profile sync. **Architecturally restructured** so the Klaviyo block runs BEFORE the template / phone / SMS path: toggling off the order_confirmed SMS template in admin must NOT silently break the Klaviyo flow trigger. Two paths are now fully independent. Klaviyo's "Placed Order" is also the standard event that auto-feeds Klaviyo's CLV / RFM reports — no extra work needed for those. **Verified on production traffic** at 18:33 UTC: David's test order #6137 ($71.95) AND a real customer order #6136 from `iyngkarrankumar@gmail.com` ($78.95) both fired cleanly within 11 seconds of each other, all properties present. Commit `32cb69b`. Function version v37.*

*(d) **Klaviyo Phase 1 flow assets (laggard / conversion).** WELCOME15 discount code created in admin Discounts page (type=`fixed`, value=$15, active=true, no expiration, no max uses). Two new Klaviyo email templates designed and created via the MCP: **"WashRoute — Laggard Day 3 ($15 off)"** (template id `WPatki`) and **"WashRoute — Laggard Day 17 (last call $15)"** (template id `W7wanh`). Clean conversational copy matching the welcome email's tone, single navy CTA → `https://app.familylaundry.com/`, prominent WELCOME15 code box, Klaviyo `{{ first_name|default:"there" }}` merge tag, `{% unsubscribe %}` template tag. David assembled the Day 3 step of the flow in Klaviyo UI (the flow-builder side is UI-only, no API). Flow structure: existing welcome → 3-day delay → conditional split "Placed Order at least once since starting this flow" (NO → Day 3 email → 14-day delay → same split → NO → Day 17 email). David assembled the Day 3 leg in Klaviyo this session, will watch it for ~1-2 weeks before iterating on subject lines / timing / copy.*

*(e) **Phase 2 (loyalty milestone) — design only, not built.** David confirmed the architecture: WashRoute owns the dollars + ledger; Klaviyo owns the words. On the customer's 10th / 20th / 50th / 100th DELIVERED order, an atomic Postgres transaction will (1) credit `customers.credits += $10`, (2) insert a `customer_transactions` row with type `loyalty_reward` (matches session 128 invariant), (3) insert a `loyalty_rewards (customer_id, milestone_number, awarded_at)` row for idempotency. Then send-order-notification POSTs a `Loyalty Milestone` event to Klaviyo with `milestone_number` + `credit_applied` + `first_name` merge tags. Klaviyo flow listens for the event, sends thank-you email. **Auto-apply confirmed** as David's preference: charge-order already auto-applies any available `customers.credits` at billing time so no new redemption logic needed — Sarah's next charge automatically deducts the $10. **MANDATORY pre-launch step (DO NOT forget):** snapshot every existing customer's current delivered-order count and backfill `loyalty_rewards` rows marking every previously-passed milestone as "already awarded — no credit, no email". Otherwise the very first post-launch delivery to a 47-order customer would trigger 4 milestones simultaneously and we'd send loyalty rewards to dozens of customers at once. The backfill flow: SELECT customer_id, COUNT(delivered orders) → FOR EACH milestone in [10,20,50,100] WHERE count >= milestone → INSERT loyalty_rewards (customer_id, milestone_number, awarded_at=now(), backfilled=true). Build whenever ready; no urgency since it depends on customer tenure not signups.*

*(f) **Bugs found + fixed mid-session:** (i) klaviyo-subscribe v1 phone-rejection bug (above). (ii) Two architectural improvements to send-order-notification: order + customer lookups moved above template check (so Klaviyo path doesn't break when SMS templates are toggled off); explicit `BEFORE the phone check` guard so customers with email-but-no-phone still register events. (iii) Recovered from the recurring stale `.git/HEAD.lock` + `.git/index.lock` cluster mid-rebase (session 142/162 quirk) — used the cowork file-delete grant to clear both locks, then `git rebase origin/main` followed by `git checkout HEAD -- .` to drop spurious reverse-deltas from the stash pop. Push clean.*

*(g) **Existing nightly `sync-klaviyo` cron still runs** — pushes `wr_lifetime_value`, `wr_total_orders`, `wr_last_order_at`, `wr_customer_since`, `wr_billing_type`, `wr_customer_type`, `wr_credits`, `wr_marketing_status` as Klaviyo profile properties at 3am PDT daily. Continues to be useful for cohort segments. Real-time events fill the gap for behavioral flows.*

*(h) **Outstanding tech debt for the Klaviyo path:** (i) Welcome arrives BEFORE email confirmation — if a customer typos their address, the welcome goes to a stranger. Conservative alternative would be to gate `handleSignup`'s call on `data.session` so only auto-confirmed/instant-login signups get the immediate welcome and `loadUserData` becomes the sole post-confirm path; left as-is for now since it matches industry norm. (ii) SMS marketing list (`SLz4un`, double-opt-in) NOT wired — needs its own consent checkbox separate from the existing operational Twilio SMS consent. (iii) Backfill of ~5,206 existing consented customers onto the Email List would mass-send welcomes; skipped pending David's go-ahead. (iv) Reverse opt-out sync (Klaviyo unsubscribes → WashRoute `email_marketing_opt_out_at`) per session-160 deferral, so opt-outs are honored everywhere. (v) Phase 2 backfill, see (e) above.*

*Previously: May 28, 2026 — Session 163 — **Angel route-disappears, recurrence #3 — root-caused to a merge regression, not a code regression.** Angel reported he couldn't see his routes again this morning. Investigation found the May 27 fix (commits `ebeb31a` route-cushion + `43666cd` SECURITY DEFINER RPC root-cause) was silently reverted by merge commit `255e79e` later the same day — when conflicts in `driver-app/index.html` were resolved in favor of the WIP side from David's laptop, both fixes vanished from the deployed file. The two RPCs (`get_driver_route_stops`, `get_driver_override_stops`) stayed in the database (verified end-to-end under Angel's JWT — returns all 25 stops today with non-null orders/customers/services for every row), but the client stopped calling them, so the original RLS-embed race reproduced. **Restoration (commit `7d54a38`):** `git checkout 43666cd -- driver-app/index.html` restored the exact post-fix state (4 RPC call sites: main fetch, carry-forward, override, upcoming); `build-version.txt` bumped so PWAs auto-reload. Pushed to main; Vercel auto-deploys. **Guardrail (audit skill update — Check 24, P0):** new file-system check at session start greps `driver-app/index.html` for the three SECURITY DEFINER RPCs (`get_driver_route_stops` ≥3, `get_driver_override_stops` ≥1, `get_driver_stop_addresses` ≥1). Any zero count = P0 fix-regression; the check tells the next session how to find the last good commit via `git log -S` and what to do. Skill rebuilt + presented for re-install. **Lesson banked (sessions 134/143/148/162 architecture-principle line): a SECURITY DEFINER RPC migration is only half the deliverable — the CLIENT CALL SITE is the other half, and a botched merge silently undid only that half.** Database migrations are durable; client edits are diff-fragile. Any future fix that's split across a DB migration + a client patch needs a canary on the client side because the migration's existence falsely signals "fix is live." Future-proofing: if we add more such RPCs (planned: complete_route_stop on driver, record_order_intake on admin, etc.), extend Check 24 with each new RPC name. **No code-side fix needed** — the May 27 design is correct and is now back in production. The merge-regression class of bug is closed by the audit canary.*

EN/ES translations added for every new string. **Verification:** TZ helper node-unit-tested (4 cases incl. both DST flips); an exact SQL replica of the function's query confirmed 2026-05-27 = 4 sales / \$77.15 (matches the register screenshot: #5705/#5700/#5687/#5684) and 2026-05-19 = 21 sales incl. 8 fully-refunded now surfaced (old code returned only 13); site scope isolates Foothill from 23rd Ave; `node --check` clean; QA blast-radius = `list_recent_pos_sales` has no other caller, edge fn uses the service-role key so no RLS gap. *🔜 **QUEUED FOR NEXT SESSION (May 27 morning) — POS → separate Stripe account cutover.** David created a SECOND Stripe account ("FAMILY LAUNDRY POS", statement descriptor `FAMILY LAUNDRY POS`) so retail (POS) vs delivery payouts can be distinguished in the books. **Decision rationale (settled with David):** bank account is SHARED, so the goal is per-channel attribution for **Xero accounting** — two Stripe accounts produce two separately-labeled payout streams into the one bank, which reconcile cleanly to different Xero accounts/tracking categories instead of having to decompose one blended payout. Dovetails with pending Xero sync (backlog #12). **The split is automatic by design:** POS in-person card payments run through the `stripe-terminal` edge function; delivery is charged later via `charge-order` (card on file). Delivery is NEVER paid in person at the reader (confirmed by David), so re-keying ONLY `stripe-terminal` to the new account cleanly routes retail→new, delivery→existing, with zero branching logic. **Already done:** new account created; statement descriptor set; `STRIPE_POS_SECRET_KEY` (new account secret) added to Supabase secrets. **David's remaining steps (tomorrow AM, Stripe-side, physical):** (1) old account → Terminal → Readers → remove the S700; (2) on the reader → Settings → factory reset → note the registration code (needs the admin passcode — the common snag); (3) new account → create Foothill Location → register reader with the code; (4) grab the new `tmr_…` reader ID and paste it to Claude. **Claude's steps (coordinated ~5-min cutover, ATOMIC — do back-to-back with the reader move at a low-traffic moment):** (a) re-key `stripe-terminal` to read `STRIPE_POS_SECRET_KEY` instead of `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` (this re-routes ALL its actions incl. refunds to the new account); (b) update `pos_devices.stripe_reader_id` for Foothill Register to the new `tmr_…`; (c) teach `get-stripe-fees` to pick the account key by order (`source='walk_in'`→POS key, else existing); (d) **pre-cutover refund fallback** — refund_pos_payment/delete_pos_order should try the new account then fall back to the old key, because retail sales made BEFORE cutover live in the OLD account and would otherwise fail to refund for ~2 weeks; (e) live test: one small charge + refund on the reader, confirm it lands in FAMILY LAUNDRY POS. **⚠️ Rollback is NOT just reverting code** — once the reader is physically registered to the new account, the old account no longer has it, so going back means re-registering the reader to the old account. Hence the coordinated one-shot cutover + immediate test. Everything delivery (`charge-order`, `create-checkout`, subs, `refund-charge`, `stripe-webhook`) stays on `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` — unchanged. Historical POS charges stay in the old account (Stripe can't migrate charges); clean separation is forward-looking from cutover.*

He'd been using the Phone Code tab, which logged him into a SEPARATE older phone-only account (`9c41e69f`, created May 15 — before his real account existed on May 23) for which the driver app had auto-created an empty `role='customer'` driver record (`dc9be21e`); he kept landing in an empty app with no routes. **Fix:** reset his real account password to a temporary `abcd1234` (via `auth.users.encrypted_password = crypt(...)`, verified it validates) so he uses the **Password** tab. **Orphan deleted:** confirmed `9c41e69f`/`dc9be21e` had zero references (route_stops, routes, driver_locations, driver_messages, orders, sms_messages, addresses, customer_transactions all 0), then deleted driver row + profile + auth user (identities cascaded). **Root-cause code fix (commit `7dcdfe9`):** the driver app's first-login fallback (`_loadDriverDataInner`) auto-created a brand-new active driver record for ANY authenticated user with no driver row — including phone/customer logins that default to `role='customer'`. *Previously: May 23, 2026 — Session 158 — Admin "Recall to Route" fix. David reported the Recall to Route action on delivered orders had "stopped working last night" — clicking **Recall to Route** in the modal showed the spurious error "No order is open. Close this modal, reopen the order, and try again." even though the order was clearly open (modal subtitle correctly read "Order #5164 — Yvonne Mckimens"). **Root cause: a z-index layering inversion.** The recall modal (`#recall-order-modal`) was at `z-index:1002`, but the order detail panel sits at `z-index:1101` and its click-to-close backdrop (`#order-panel-backdrop`, whose only handler is `onclick="closeOrderPanel()"`) is at `z-index:1099`. So when recall was launched from inside the open order panel, the panel's backdrop covered the modal; a stray click in the modal area landed on the backdrop instead, fired `closeOrderPanel()`, and nulled the `opCurrentOrderId` global. `confirmRecallOrder()` then read that now-null global → the "No order is open" guard tripped. Recall was uniquely affected because it's the only order-panel-launched overlay that BOTH layered over the open panel AND read the live global at confirm time — the sibling status picker (`openSingleStatusPicker`) already captures its id into `_singleStatusOrderId`, and the reschedule pickers are inline panel sections (panel stays on top, global stays valid). **Two-layer fix (commit `3b20218`):** (1) raised `#recall-order-modal` to `z-index:1102` so modal clicks no longer fall through to the panel backdrop; (2) `opRecallOrder()` now captures the order id into a modal-scoped `_recallOrderId` and `confirmRecallOrder()` uses that instead of the live `opCurrentOrderId`, matching the established `_singleStatusOrderId` pattern — durable even if the panel ever closes mid-flow. No DB change: the `recall_delivered_order` RPC was verified intact with its exact 4-arg signature. Lesson: any modal launched from inside the order panel (z-index 1101) must sit above it AND capture its target id rather than reading the live `opCurrentOrderId` global.*

*— **Session 158 (also): On Account "Record Payment" — B2B payment ledger.** David receives a single consolidated check from B2B accounts (e.g. Kidango Group — $6,682.30 across 16 subaccounts / 78 April orders) and needed a way to post that payment against all of an account's subaccounts from the Reports → On Account view. Before this, the only On-Account actions were Preview PDF + Send Invoice; "paid" was a binary flag (`orders.billing_status='paid'`) with no record of the actual check. **New: a real payments ledger.** Migration `session_157_payments_ledger` (NOTE: applied during session 158 — name is one behind, cosmetic only) adds: (1) `public.payments` table (id, billing_group_id, customer_id, amount, applied_amount, method check/ach/wire/other, reference, received_date, notes, actor_name, voided_at, voided_by) — RLS enabled, `is_admin()`-only (stricter than billing_groups since these are financial records); (2) nullable `orders.payment_id` FK → payments (ON DELETE SET NULL); (3) extended `mark_orders_paid` with an optional trailing `p_payment_id` param (all 3 existing named-arg callers unaffected — verified no positional callers, no other pg_proc references before DROP+recreate) that links each settled order to its payment row; (4) `record_account_payment(p_order_ids[], p_amount, p_method, p_reference, p_received_date, p_notes, p_actor_name, p_billing_group_id, p_customer_id)` — inserts the payment, snapshot-then-applies (settles ONLY orders still unpaid right now), calls mark_orders_paid to flip them + log attributed `billing` events, stores `applied_amount` = sum of settled charged-totals; (5) `void_account_payment(p_payment_id, p_actor_name)` — reverses a payment, restoring its orders to `billing_status=NULL` (the confirmed outstanding state) ONLY where still-paid + still-attached, logs void events, stamps `voided_at`. Both RPCs SECURITY DEFINER with an `auth.uid() IS NOT NULL AND NOT is_admin()` guard (blocks authenticated non-admins; trusted backend / service_role passes; anon REVOKEd) + REVOKE PUBLIC/anon + GRANT authenticated/service_role per the lesson-F checklist. **UI (admin commit `5a05d1e`):** a Record Payment button on the On-Account account action bar opens a modal — editable date range (prefilled from the report pickers), a checklist of unpaid orders in range with a live "Applying" total + select-all, amount/method/reference/date-received fields, an over/under mismatch warning vs the ticked total, and a recorded-payments history list with one-click Void. SMS-safe: verified no order trigger fires on `billing_status` change and no edge-function/SMS call is in the path (marking paid was already the production behavior of mark_orders_paid). Verified end-to-end via a transactional round-trip on 2 real Kidango orders (record → settled + linked → void → restored to NULL) that rolled back, leaving production untouched. **David is testing the live posting of the real Kidango check himself** — not posted by Claude.*
