## Session Log

### Aug 7, 2026 (session 207) — Payroll: employee tax withholding was being double-counted (found by auditing the previous day's own work)

**Trigger:** David looked at the "Post Catch-up to Xero" modal for a $16,293.07 employee-federal-tax catch-up I'd built hours earlier, saw it blocked with a $12,723.95 shortfall, and said: *"There shouldn't be a gap. This tells me we're not allocating things correctly at the source."* He was right, and my earlier explanation of the leftover Direct Wages balance was wrong.

**The bug (mine, shipped the same day).** Employee tax withholding is by definition already inside GROSS pay, and gross pay is exactly what `payroll-xero-post` debits to the department **wage** accounts. Verified to the penny across all 5 July periods from the raw Square CSVs:

```
gross − EE federal − EE CA − EE health − EE 401k + insurance reimbursement = net pay
```

So `payroll-xero-post` **v14** (added employee CA tax as an extra department **tax** debit) and **v15** (same for employee federal tax) were expensing the same dollars twice.

- **v14 reached the ledger**: $4,465.21 of duplicate department payroll-tax expense across the 5 posted July periods — $803.49 inside the 6/22–6/28 period journal plus $3,661.72 in the `[171-CATCHUP-JUL2026]` journal. Both credited 171 for the *correct* amount; the error was purely that the offsetting **debit** went to the department tax accounts instead of simply reducing the credit taken from 170.
- **v15 never posted** — the real-cash safety check blocked it. **The block was the symptom, not the cause, and I initially misread it as a bank-feed timing lag.** That misreading is the actual lesson of this session.

**How the money really moves per pay period** (established from the CSVs and confirmed against every Xero bank transaction):

| Draw | Lands in |
|---|---|
| Square draws NET PAY | 170 |
| IRS draws EE federal tax + ER federal tax | 170 |
| EDD draws ER California tax | 170 |
| EDD draws EE California tax | **171** |

So `170 cash = net pay + EE federal + ALL employer taxes`, `171 cash = EE California`, and the correct expense is simply **gross wages + employer taxes**.

**Full July reconciliation (every dollar, no residue):** actual cash into 170 was $129,219.75 (incl. the $81,295.86 668-misroute add-back) vs. $126,224.75 the five periods should draw. The $2,995.00 difference was two off-cycle "payroll" bank draws with no Square CSV behind them ($2,975.00 + $20.00). The remaining $145.12 was the net distortion of the wrong formula. $2,995.00 + $145.12 = **$3,140.12**, the exact "orphaned Direct Wages" figure David spotted on the P&L.

**`payroll-xero-post` v16 — the corrected journal:**
```
DEBIT  department wage account  = wage (gross ex-tips)
DEBIT  department tax account   = employer tax ONLY
CREDIT 171 Direct Payroll Taxes = employee CA tax   (the real EDD cash there)
CREDIT 170 Direct Wages         = (wage + employer tax) − employee CA tax
```
Balances by construction, clears the real EDD cash out of 171, and leaves in 170 **only** items that genuinely aren't department wage/tax costs.

**Remediation shipped:**
- `payroll-fix-federal-catchup` → **v2, permanently-disabled stub** returning `already_posted:true`, so a stale browser tab can never fire it. Do not revive.
- `payroll-fix-ca-doublecount` → new one-time reversal (credits each department tax account its EE-CA share, debits $4,465.21 back to 170; 171 deliberately untouched since it cleared correctly). No balance check needed — it only reduces expense and returns money.
- Dashboard (`926a4db`): federal catch-up card/modal/JS removed entirely; reversal card added in its place.
- Superseded commit `dd57a96` (the v15 UI) — kept in history, but its feature is dead.

**Verified end state.** David posted the reversal and separately reclassified the $2,975 off-cycle draw to Repairs & Maintenance. He then also reclassified the $20.00 sibling draw. Live Xero final: **170 = $5,039.33 YTD**, all five department tax accounts down by exactly their EE-CA share, 171/358/675 unchanged. July's 170 component is **$4,610.33** and reconciles **exactly** — with no unexplained bank activity remaining, only structural items:

```
+ 7,038.00  paycheck tips paid out (customer money, not a wage expense)
+   600.00  insurance reimbursement stipend (2 periods × $300)
−   491.32  employee health withheld   (belongs against 675)
− 2,536.35  employee 401k withheld     (belongs against 358)
= 4,610.33
```

**Lesson banked — a safety net firing is a signal to re-derive, not a number to explain away.** The real-cash check did its job and stopped $16,293.07 of bad data, but I treated the block as a cash-timing inconvenience and told David to "check back in a few days." Had he accepted that, the error would have posted the moment enough cash landed. **When a guard blocks something you believe should pass, the first move is to re-derive the quantity from source data — not to rationalise the gap.** Corollary, now permanent: **never add a payroll component to an allocation without first checking whether it's already inside another component you're posting.** Gross pay contains all employee withholding; net pay + withholding is not additional cost.

**Part 2 (same session) — two follow-on bugs found while wiring up the flags.**

**A. Stale "Needs Attention" flags.** Three posted July periods kept showing "waiting on cash" hours after they had posted. `payroll-check-attention` selects only `status in ('parsed','reviewed')`, so once a period posts it is never revisited and the flag written while it was still `reviewed` is orphaned permanently. Flags were written 22:49; the periods posted 23:28–23:29.

Fixing only the edge function would leave the same trap for every other write path, so the invariant went into the database: trigger `trg_clear_payroll_attention` (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE on `payroll_imports`) nulls `attention_*` whenever `status` leaves the actionable set. The function additionally sweeps orphans as defense in depth. Same principle as sessions 134/143/148: when a fix has a code half and a data half, push the guarantee down a layer.

Also corrected that function's shortfall math, which still used the v14-era `wage + employer tax` estimate and therefore reported figures that did not match what posting would really do. It now mirrors `payroll-xero-post` v17 (170 needs net pay + EE federal + employer tax; 171 needs EE CA tax). **These two must be kept in sync — if one changes, change the other.**

**B. The "Refresh Now" button had never worked.** It failed with a bare `Failed to fetch`. `payroll-check-attention` v1/v2 shipped with **no CORS block and no OPTIONS handler**, but the dashboard's `_payrollFn()` calls edge functions with a raw cross-origin `fetch()` carrying `apikey` + `Authorization` + `Content-Type`, which always triggers a preflight. The preflight returned 200 with no `Access-Control-*` headers, so the browser blocked the real request.

It went unnoticed for a day because **the pg_cron job invokes this server-side, where CORS does not apply** — so the panel's data always looked fresh even though the button was dead. Every other payroll and loan function already had the wrapper; this one was the outlier. Verified after the fix that the preflight returns `access-control-allow-origin` and swept all 10 browser-callable payroll/loan functions — the rest were already fine.

**Lesson banked — a background job can mask a dead UI control.** If a feature has both a scheduled path and a manual path, the scheduled one succeeding proves nothing about the manual one. Any new browser-callable edge function needs the `cors` block AND the `OPTIONS` short-circuit, and should be exercised from the browser (or with an `Origin:` header) at least once before being called done.

**C. `payroll_notices` (new table).** Standing payroll problems that are not tied to one pay period had nowhere to live on `payroll_imports`. First entry is the legacy 171 balance below. Computed live by `payroll-check-attention` so it clears itself when resolved, rather than a hardcoded banner someone must remember to remove. RLS: read for admin/manager/cpa; writes are service_role only (no INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy granted to authenticated users on purpose). Employee CA tax belonging to not-yet-posted periods is excluded from the figure so a normal mid-week EDD payment never false-positives.

**Part 3 (same session) — Stripe payout sync: a double count, a false alarm, and a day of revenue that silently vanished.**

**A. August payouts were being recorded twice ($22,840.60).** David spotted it on a 403 Delivery - Wash & Fold report: each August payout had two rows. Every payout had TWO bank transactions in Wells Fargo with identical totals — one contact "Stripe", 8–10 lines, properly split (405/403/404/401 revenue, less 605 merchant fees, 828 Stripe fees, 345 credits, 346 coupons, 304 Capital repayment), unreconciled; and one contact "Family Laundry", a single line dumping the whole deposit into 403, reconciled. Both are bank transactions, so both added cash *and* revenue. Aug 3 $5,071.31 + Aug 4 $5,006.64 + Aug 5 $12,762.65.

**Root cause: a Xero BANK RULE, not the sync.** `xero-payout-sync` is a Stripe `payout.paid` webhook that pre-creates the split bank transaction so the incoming statement line can be **matched** to it — that is the correct Xero pattern and the code is right. A bank rule was intercepting the statement line and creating its own "Family Laundry" transaction instead of matching. July never showed this because the webhook wasn't live yet: back then the rule made the single 403 entry and a manual journal reallocated it afterwards, which nets to the same place. The two mechanisms started stacking on Aug 3.

**Resolved by David in the Xero UI** (Remove & Redo on the three reconciled "Family Laundry" entries, delete, then Find & Match each statement line to the Stripe transaction). Verified afterwards: all three duplicates `DELETED`, all three Stripe transactions flipped to reconciled, zero remaining duplicate pairs. **Xero exposes no bank-rule API**, so whether the rule itself is gone cannot be confirmed from here — the definitive test is the next payout.

**B. The $125,000 "duplicate" was a false alarm — my error.** I reported that a duplicate $125,000 Stripe Capital advance was sitting in 471 Other Income overstating July revenue. It was already `DELETED` in Xero and 471 actually holds −$30.66. My audit pulled `BankTransactions` **without filtering on Status**, so a deleted record came back looking live. The guarded delete function I built refused to run — its `already deleted` check caught it — which is the only reason this didn't compound. **Lesson banked: always filter/report `Status` when reading Xero transactions; deleted and voided records come back from the API looking exactly like live ones.** This is the same class of mistake as the payroll double-count earlier the same day: reporting a number without re-deriving what it actually represents.

**C. Aug 6 revenue ($5,753.10) silently never reached Xero.** David asked "what happened to August 6th?" — the 403 report jumped from Aug 5 to Aug 7. Xero had **zero** transactions dated Aug 6, in any status. The `xero_payout_syncs` row for `po_1U1FUnGACgbvEugHa2Ax5hIa` was created at 02:02:19 on Aug 6 with status `pending` and stayed there: no `error_message`, no `xero_bank_transaction_id`, nothing anywhere to notice.

**Root cause — a silent-failure hole that cannot be closed from inside the function.** `xero-payout-sync` claims the sync row as `pending` BEFORE the expensive work (100+ Stripe API calls classifying every balance transaction), then posts and flips to `posted`/`failed`. That work runs in a background task whose only error handling is `console.error`. If it throws, the trace ages out of log retention. If the isolate is **terminated** mid-flight — very plausible at that call volume — even the `.catch()` never runs. Compounding it: the webhook returns `200 {received:true}` immediately, so **Stripe records a clean success and never retries**. Confirmed from Stripe's own event log: event `evt_1U1GdFGACgbvEugH6bVzlpG9`, delivered 200 OK at 02:02:06, thirteen seconds before our row was created.

**Recovered** by resending the `payout.paid` event from the Stripe dashboard. Verified afterwards: posted with the full ten-account split, sync row `posted`, all five August payouts now present with no gap and no duplicate. 403 for August = $31,565.07.

**`xero-payout-watchdog` (new) + `wr-xero-payout-watchdog` cron (every 30 min).** Because a killed isolate can't run its own cleanup, detection has to come from outside. The watchdog finds sync rows still `pending` past 30 minutes, flips them to `failed` with an explicit recovery instruction, and reports every payout not yet in Xero with a total. It deliberately does **not** auto-retry — a payout that failed for a real reason would just fail again, and auto-retrying financial posts without a human looking is exactly how the payroll incident earlier that day happened.

**Lesson banked — an immediate webhook ACK disables your provider's retry safety net.** Returning 200 before the real work means Stripe (or any provider) can never help you recover; the failure is invisible to both sides. Any handler that acks-then-works needs its own external watchdog, and the state it writes must distinguish "in progress" from "abandoned" — `pending` forever looks identical to `pending` right now.

**Open items at end of session:**
- **The Xero bank rule** coding Wells Fargo deposits to "Family Laundry" → 403. David believes he deleted it; unverifiable via API. Watch the next payout: a **Match** offer means it's gone, a self-created "Family Laundry" entry means it isn't.
- **Aug 6 and Aug 7 payouts** ($5,753.10 + $5,490.40) are correctly in Xero but unreconciled — the bank feed is only through Aug 5. Match them via Find & Match when the lines arrive; do NOT let anything create new ones.
- **A stray $1.00 unreconciled Stripe fee** dated Jun 5 (SPEND to 828, ref INV-0009).
- **~$100 discrepancy** between the three unreconciled transactions I can see via the API ($11,242.50 net) and Xero's reported balance difference ($11,342.50). Xero's transaction endpoint doesn't expose unimported statement lines; likely visible only in Xero's own reconciliation report. Flagged rather than hand-waved.
- **Watchdog duplicate detection is unbuilt** — intended to scan for two live transactions sharing a date and amount and flag them, which would remove the need to manually watch for the bank rule recurring. A deploy attempt hit a JSON formatting error and was abandoned rather than leave half-built code in a financial path.
- **Temp diagnostic edge functions still need retiring** (now also `temp-403-aug-audit`, `temp-403-aug-audit2`, `temp-aug6-check`, `temp-unreconciled-wf`, `temp-acct-names`, `temp-audit-dept-tax`, `temp-cpa-journal-peek`, `temp-170-*`, `temp-delete-dup-125k`). Long-standing housekeeping debt.

**Open items for David / the CPA:**
- Both off-cycle draws ($2,975.00 and $20.00) turned out to be miscodes and were reclassified by David — **not** payroll. 170 now contains only structural items.
- Tips ($7,038.00) still sit in 170 with no tips-clearing account; the 401k/health pieces belong against 358/675, which is where the CPA has always put them. Neither is wired up — these are the obvious next automation, and unlike the tax components they are *not* already inside gross wages, so they can be added safely.
- **Account 171 carries $5,389.23 from May that has never been reallocated by anyone.** If gross wages already contain that money, it may be double-counted in the CPA's own books too. Now surfaced in-app as a `payroll_notices` entry with an explicit "don't clear it here without their answer" — needs a real conversation with the CPA.

## Pending / Next Up
- **⚠️ DEPLOY: commit `d8a73d0` (session 208, recurring retail-cash-reclass UI) needs `git push` from David's Mac.** The `retail-cash-reclass-monthly` edge function is already live in Supabase; the admin dashboard change pointing the Bookkeeping → Overview card at it is committed locally but not yet pushed/deployed to `admin.familylaundry.com`. After push, verify live: card should be hidden (July + current partial month already covered or not yet due), and should correctly appear with the right month name once a new completed month has cash-paid retail revenue.
- **🟢 Follow-up from session 189 (SMS centralization): centralize the `confirmed` (order-placed) SMS too.** Currently still fires from two independent client-side call sites — admin's `saveOrder()` and customer-app's order-placement flow — each right after their own order-INSERT succeeds. Same shape of fragility as the bugs just fixed (session 188/189), just lower risk today since there's only one caller per app. Natural fix, if/when there's a shared order-creation RPC to hang it off of; otherwise low priority.
- **🟢 Skill refresh needed: `washroute` skill's "What's Pending" list is stale.** Item 3 (`complete_route_stop`) has been shipped and wired since session 136 — the skill wasn't updated when it landed, and following that stale pointer sent session 189 investigating a dead lead before the real scope (SMS centralization) was found via fresh code reading. Needs a pass to bring the skill's completed/pending sections in line with this file.
- **🔵 PLANNED for Sunday, Aug 2 (2026-07-27 planning session): standardize AM/PM pickup windows to 7-10, drop the split-hour sub-windows.** David is announcing this via newsletter for the Aug 3 rollout, alongside an ETA-text feature that's been explicitly deferred (see below). **Window change:** Alameda AM, Berkeley AM, Concord AM, Oakland AM move from 7-11am (split 7-9/9-11 sub-windows) to a single 7-10am/3hr window (`window_end` 11:00→10:00, `arrival_window_hours` 2→3). Alameda PM, Berkeley PM, Oakland PM move from 6-10pm (split 6-8/8-10) to a single 7-10pm/3hr window (`window_start` 18:00→19:00, `arrival_window_hours` 2→3). Hayward AM, Hayward PM, and San Francisco are UNCHANGED — they already run 7-10/3hr, which is why this configuration isn't experimental; it's already proven in production. Kidango and COMMERCIAL are explicitly excluded (B2B/commercial, not part of the residential rollout). **Already-booked orders:** as of July 27, 26 orders (3 Alameda, 5 Berkeley, 18 Oakland, 0 Concord) already exist with pickup_window_start ≥ Aug 3 carrying the OLD window — these get re-snapped via `snap_window_to_template()` (the same function `trg_create_recurring_order_fn` already trusts for this) immediately AFTER the template update, not before/separately — doing it first would silently no-op since the function reads live template values. **This count will have grown by Aug 2** as this week's recurring orders complete and chain into next week's occurrences via the trigger — re-query live at execution time, don't reuse the July 27 snapshot. No individual customer notification for these 26ish orders — David's newsletter announcement covers it. **Reminder change:** disable the 7am day-of reminder (`message_templates.sms_enabled = false` where `trigger_key = 'pickup_day_reminder'`) — a pure settings toggle, zero code. The night-before reminder (`pickup_reminder_recurring`) is untouched. **ETA-text feature: explicitly deferred to a later phase, NOT part of this rollout.** David's reasoning: routes get adjusted throughout the day (delays, driver reassignment), so a "locked-in" 30-minute ETA sent early could go stale by actual arrival and generate more complaints than it prevents — better to ship it once the 7-10 windows have settled. **Timing:** both the template update and the order-fix happen together, sometime Sunday Aug 2 — confirmed via order history that zero orders have run on any Sunday in the surrounding weeks, so it's a genuinely dead operational day with no precision-timing pressure (unlike a midnight-cutover approach). A `send_later` reminder is scheduled for 8am PT that day to come back and execute. **Architecture notes worth remembering:** `routes`/`route_stops` carry no denormalized window copies (display always derives live from `orders.pickup_window_start/end`, so no separate admin/driver-app fix needed); `subscriptions.preferred_pickup_window` is a display-cached text label that COULD go stale on a window change but is currently NULL on every active subscription, so nothing to clean up this time — worth rechecking if that field ever gets populated before a future window change.
- **🔵 Objective (added July 27, 2026, target ~1 week out): fine-grained GitHub token scoped to a feature branch, for Cowork/Claude cloud-session access.** Decision from session 186 pt 2: instead of giving the cloud sandbox broad push/delete access, set up a GitHub fine-grained personal access token (or GitHub App) scoped to ONLY the `washroute` repo, with contents read/write and no admin/other-repo access. Pair it with a **branch protection rule on `main`/the default branch blocking direct pushes**, so the cloud session always works on its own feature branch and opens a PR — David reviews/merges. This removes the current manual "commit locally, David runs `git push`" step while keeping main protected, and sidesteps the `device_bash` sandbox's no-network / can't-delete constraints since the cloud session (not the device bridge) would hold the token. Not yet built — no token created, no branch-protection rule added, no repo cloned into the cloud session.
- **⚠️ DEPLOY: commit `5e824df` (session 186, per-day driver override) needs `git push` from David's Mac.** DB migration is already live; the admin UI change is committed locally but not yet pushed/deployed.
- **🟡 Tech debt (session 186): `route_driver_overrides.driver_id` is `NOT NULL`**, so the "Just this day" driver popover scope can't record "explicitly no driver for just this day" on a date >~14 days out where no route has materialized yet and a weekly default (`route_driver_schedule`) exists — clicking "Unassigned" in that narrow case just deletes any existing one-day override and falls through to the weekly default once the route is created. Works correctly for every near-term date (a route already exists, so "Unassigned" updates it directly). Fix would be widening the column to nullable + a sentinel, or a boolean `is_explicit_unassign` column — low priority until it actually bites.
- **🟢 Thin-slice: staging environment + config decoupling (scoped July 16, 2026 — see `WashRoute-Staging-Config-Scope.md`).** The internal-only slice of "productization" worth doing even if we never sell WashRoute as SaaS. Prize = a **safe staging environment** (test migrations / SMS-template changes / bulk ops against real-shaped data with **zero risk of texting or charging real customers**), which retires the accidental-mass-SMS incident class the preflight skill exists for. Audit finding: the app is already mostly decoupled (pricing/tax/sites in DB tables, Twilio/Stripe creds in Supabase secrets, brand in `/assets` images, and `SUPA_URL`/`SUPA_ANON_KEY` are a single const pair per app), so the work is small: **Track A** (~2–3 days) = env switch on the SUPA consts (hostname → prod/staging) + stand up a staging Supabase (dedicated project, or a branch for one-off migration checks) + an outbound **kill-switch** in the SMS/charge edge functions that hard-refuses real sends in staging + anonymized seed data + staging front-end + repoint the `washroute-test` skill at staging; **Track B** (~1 day) = fold stray constants into a small `WR_CONFIG` block. **Do A1 (env switch) + A3 (kill-switch) first — nothing else matters if staging can reach real customers.** Total ~3–5 days. Bonus: it's the same first step the multi-tenant SaaS path needs, so it's not wasted if we later decide to sell. Broader strategy context in `WashRoute-Productization-Estimate.md`.
- **🟡 DB performance — index + RLS optimization on hot queries (logged May 29, 2026 from scheduled health check).** Same shape as the pre-May-26 IO-exhaustion trend; DB is responsive today but throwing ~35+ `statement timeout` errors/hour, and the top 5 pg_stat_statements queries are all averaging >500ms. Specifically: (1) `route_stops` SELECT with orders+customers+services join — **5,775 ms** mean × 279 calls; (2) `sms_messages` SELECT filtered by `created_at` — **4,378 ms** × 473 calls; (3) `route_stops` SELECT (no joins) — **1,509 ms** × **3,193 calls** (biggest cumulative burn — 80+ min of DB time on this one query alone); (4) `orders` UPDATE (billing_status / charge_failed_at) — **1,273 ms** × 7; (5) `orders` UPDATE (archived_at) — **1,119 ms** × 1. Also: `realtime.list_changes` repeatedly taking 10–12 sec. Fix plan: add indexes on `route_stops` JOIN columns + the filter used by the 3,193-call query; add index on `sms_messages.created_at`; review RLS policies on `route_stops` / `sms_messages` / `orders` for repeated subquery patterns that can be cached or rewritten. No dashboard intervention needed today, but schedule within the next few days — this is the same trajectory that led to the May 26 lockout.
- **Migration 2: drop `customer_type` column + sync trigger** (session 97p3 → next session): After confirming nothing still reads the legacy column for ~24h, drop `trg_sync_customer_type_pricelist` first, then `DROP COLUMN customers.customer_type`. Pre-check: `grep -rn "customer_type"` across all app files and `pg_proc` should return zero hits. Only proceed if clean.

## Test Order SQL Template

When inserting test orders directly via SQL, use this template so that DB triggers fire correctly and the order behaves like a real customer-placed one. **Replace the placeholder values** with real IDs from the DB.

```sql
-- ╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗
-- ║  TEST ORDER INSERT — copy, fill in placeholders, run once   ║
-- ╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
--
-- REQUIRED: fill in customer_id, address_id, zone_id, and dates.
-- The auto-route trigger handles everything else (route, stops, driver).
--
-- Zone IDs (for reference):
--   Oakland:       fbc4627b-7026-44e2-9b00-2c3c867f4460
--   Berkeley:      2dfc9835-3528-4143-bfa1-c6a9961bb3c2
--   Alameda:       9d624c91-2991-45d0-bcdd-db0906ad88c2
--   San Francisco: 670d7bb1-68f0-4696-9b8c-a726d8c85da4
--   Hayward:       39d9a0c0-c906-45f6-a8ab-6fe4a992e026

INSERT INTO orders (
  customer_id,
  status,
  total_bags,
  total_amount,
  pickup_window_start,        -- UTC timestamp (Pacific + 7 in winter, + 8 in summer)
  pickup_window_end,
  delivery_window_start,
  delivery_window_end,
  zone_id,
  pickup_address_id,
  delivery_address_id,
  source,
  line_items,
  recurring_interval          -- NULL, 'weekly', 'biweekly', or 'monthly'
) VALUES (
  '________-____-____-____-____________',   -- customer_id (required)
  'scheduled',                               -- must be 'scheduled' for trigger to fire
  2,                                         -- total_bags
  0,                                         -- total_amount (set when charged)
  '2026-03-__T__:00:00Z',                   -- pickup start (UTC)
  '2026-03-__T__:00:00Z',                   -- pickup end (UTC)
  '2026-03-__T__:00:00Z',                   -- delivery start (UTC, typically +1 day)
  '2026-03-__T__:00:00Z',                   -- delivery end (UTC, typically +1 day)
  '________-____-____-____-____________',   -- zone_id (required — see list above)
  '________-____-____-____-____________',   -- pickup_address_id
  '________-____-____-____-____________',   -- delivery_address_id (same as pickup if identical)
  'customer_app',                            -- source: customer_app, scheduled, walk_in, recurring
  '[]',                                      -- line_items (JSONB array)
  NULL                                       -- recurring_interval (NULL for one-time)
);

-- ⚠️ DO NOT set pickup_run_id or delivery_run_id — leave them NULL
--    so the auto_route_on_insert trigger fires and assigns routes.
--
-- ⚠️ Pickup time must fall within a route template's window for the
--    order's zone + day-of-week, or no route will be assigned.
--    Current windows: AM = 07:00-11:00, PM = 18:00-22:00 Pacific
--
-- To verify it worked:
-- SELECT id, pickup_run_id, delivery_run_id FROM orders WHERE id = '<new-id>';
```
